The research record

Every experiment this crate has run, in one place, each with the command that reproduces it, the criterion it was judged by, what it refuted, and what it does not settle. Generated from docs/lights/RESEARCH.json; the readable form is docs/lights/RESEARCH.md.

41 experiments

39 with a copy-pasteable command · 31 cross-experiment findings

The record

A dimmed leaf is an experiment whose command cannot be pasted into a shell as recorded — the record explains what is known about how it was run, and that gap is what this document exists to close. The tree groups experiments by what they were asking about; the cross-experiment node holds what the records say about each other — contradictions, tables that are not in one currency, and confounds shared across runs — and the arm audit gives every arm one verdict, grouped by it, with the target its evidence was taken against as the leaf label. Drag the divider to resize; it and your last selection are remembered in this browser. Without JavaScript every record is shown in the right pane in the same order.

What lights set out to prove

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

lights began as a question about crisp UI content: can anisotropic atoms, placed by a fitter, describe what a user interface draws, and at what cost in atoms, bytes and time against an analytic renderer drawing the same shapes from the same definition. P2 asked it first, and aspects 1 to 3 asked it along three axes: the atom's shape, signed atoms and wavelets against positive ones, and whether one description holds across scale.

cites p2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atoms · a1-atom-curve · p4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets · p5-aspect3-resolution-independence

The PM then ruled that lights is also a simple 3D game engine, and every experiment in the record until then had answered only the UI question. The 3D questions that followed are the frame rate at 1080p, a normal to shade with, surfels, compositing against a floor, deformation, and what precision costs.

cites l123-frame-rate-1080p · l141-gradient-normal-shading · l122-surfel-stored-normal · l153-interpenetration-composite · l165-affine-closure · l163-bandwidth-crossover

Since 2026-09-29 lights is an experimental proving ground for the fulgor/nexus paradigm: interchangeable strategies on three axes, kernel, method and precision, measured against each other, with an agreement contract between independent implementations, so that a champion can be measured and the losers pruned. That is the claim this report judges the record against, and the arm table gives the verdict per arm.

cites arm:method/splat-raster · arm:method/ray-cast · arm:probe/audit

What it measured

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

On crisp UI content atoms lost to analytic geometry. The analytic arm met every registered criterion at 10.2-38.9 us from an 83-274 byte description, while the splat arm matched it on flat fill alone and never met the 12 px glyph at any budget. The atom's shape moves the result without moving the verdict: a super-gaussian at beta=4 closes the right-angle-corner gap 11.5x, and no kernel meets the 12 px glyph at any budget.

cites p2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atoms · a1-atom-curve

Signed atoms earn nothing: the optimiser drives the Gabor carrier to zero wherever it helps, and sign placement is ruled colour. Resolution independence is real for atoms on smooth and near-smooth content and fails on text: frozen atoms on the 12 px glyph decay 0.22339 to 0.45811 rel L2 against an analytic bar at 0.01352.

cites p4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets · p5-aspect3-resolution-independence

Every atom count above rests on one greedy fitter, and the record names it as the confound the experiments share. Read as prefixes, its published ladders are worse than the fit at that budget in 26 of 30 cells, mean +23.6%. An Adam optimiser loses to it on all six classes, and still loses with an analytic gradient that agrees with the central difference to 9.1e-10 of |g|. Against image-gs it is about 8 dB behind, 7.96 and 8.51 dB, and only as a cross-dataset figure.

cites l142-prefix-shortfall · l132-adam-against-the-ladder · l147-analytic-gradient · l43-greedy-at-imagegs-bitrates

The fit arm's instruments were measured before they were trusted. The finite-difference step was measured at eps = 1e-6 rather than assumed, and the first choice was refused by its own sweep. A per-atom beta beats the tuned gaussian on 42 of 43 rungs and matches an oracle over the five fixed families on only 23 of 43. Inside a candidate evaluation only the fused residual mattered, about 1.9 us of roughly 18.

cites l131-finite-difference-gradient · l135-per-atom-beta · l97-candidate-evaluation-cost

For the 3D engine the frame rate is measured. At 1920x1080 on 18 cores splat holds a 60 fps frame to somewhere between 10k and 100k atoms, 4.858 ms at 10k and 23.923 ms at 100k, while the ray caster is 15.590 ms at 10k and 183.551 ms at 100k, and at 100k atoms 76% of splat's frame is bind. Doubling every atom's bytes never moves the frame out of the 1.3x noise floor, splat's f64/f32 ratio being 0.985-1.019 from 100k to 10M atoms, so compressing atoms is a capacity question and not a speed question. Compressing the atom would shrink an array the render loops never read: a block of 17.3-21.3 B would cut the per-atom footprint by 10-16% and move no frame.

cites l123-frame-rate-1080p · l163-bandwidth-crossover · l164-dgf-atom-mapping

Shading exists two ways. The ray caster's density gradient gives a silhouette normal at no stored cost: a side-lit sphere's lit half is 3.5426x its dark half, against the 3.5465 an image-plane radial normal predicts. A surfel stores its normal, and a 900-surfel sphere matches the ideal Lambert sphere with mean absolute error 0.0208 under a side light and 0.0074 under a headlight.

cites l141-gradient-normal-shading · l122-surfel-stored-normal

Compositing two passes does not serve an interpenetrating scene: both orders disagree with the interleaved truth on 15.6% and 15.7% of the frame, against a bar of zero pixels. Deformation through a cage is exact to rounding, 1.98e-15 relative in f64 over 9,519 random maps, and costs 7.5-9.3 us per update against 0.107-0.283 s for one re-fit. A moment-matched hierarchy is exact in its own terms and wrong for this renderer: the first merge in every scene already costs 48.6-223 code values of max error.

cites l153-interpenetration-composite · l165-affine-closure · l169-cage-deform-vs-refit · l166-moment-matched-early-stop

The host exists and is measured. AppKit delivered every event the contract names in 5 of 5 runs, where winit in pump mode dropped every window event raised outside its own callback. The fused BGRA8 resolve takes 2.574-2.649 ms at 1920x1080 in release against 6.843-7.092 ms for the three passes it replaced, byte-identical. In the gallery splat draws the 41-atom demo object in 4.910-5.063 ms of CPU per warm frame and the ray caster in 21.609-22.290 ms.

cites l186-event-path-arms · l190-fused-bgra8-handoff · l197-gallery-frame-times

Text was measured as geometry. Covariance inflation holds against a box reference until the thinnest feature falls below about 0.7 px; on nine real glyphs that rule holds as a bound once the paper between strokes counts, and read on ink alone it fails once, at 0.798 px. A real font converts through ttf-parser to the crate's line and arc edges with exact coverage within 0.0075 of a scanline integral at 32 px/em, the converter's tolerance holds two-sided at 0.9999x, and the platforms' own rasterisers depart from the geometry further than inflation does, 31.4-201.8 steps for the macOS default.

cites l159-glyph-inflation-over-scale · l168-real-glyph-inflation-over-scale · l171-real-font-round-trip · l161-quad-to-arc-bound · l173-system-rasteriser-oracle

Light transport between cards was gated rather than built. Rung 1 passes on a light canvas only, a coloured contact bleed up to 17 CIEDE2000 that neither screen-space pass reproduces, and rung 2 kills the patch line because the patches that would resolve the bleed are the ones the ladder names as unaffordable.

cites l107-transport-gate

Parallelism was measured as a strategy like any other. Mapping whole fits across the cores buys 5.30x-6.29x on lights-p2 and 5.60x-5.82x on lights-p4 with byte-identical outputs, while tile parallelism inside one fit is 1.7x-4.3x slower at the viewports the fitter works at.

cites l96-sweep-parallelism

What the measurements say about the fulgor/nexus paradigm

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

The paradigm's strongest evidence is agreement, not speed. Two independently written renderers are certified against each other pixel by pixel, and the ray caster's production binning must reproduce its brute-force oracle bit for bit, which is how its tile pairs fell from 109,188 to 46,717 without moving an image. The same shape recurs wherever the crate holds two implementations of one thing: the analytic gradient's forward image equals the tile renderer's bit for bit, and the fused BGRA8 fill is byte-identical to the three passes it replaced.

cites arm:raycast/brute-force · arm:probe/audit · l147-analytic-gradient · l190-fused-bgra8-handoff

Interchangeable strategies worked as a method of measurement. Every arm has one verdict, with the target its deciding evidence was taken against and whether that evidence carries to the surviving mission: 16 KEEP, 6 PRUNE and 9 RE-JUDGE, and no arm is pruned on evidence that does not transfer.

cites arm:kernel/gabor-density · arm:3d/moment-hierarchy · arm:fit/arm · arm:kernel/gaussian

Interchangeability has a measured price, and the speed it was argued for did not arrive. Static dispatch over precision, kernel and probe is 96 render-loop bodies across the two built methods; the pixel-parallel vector width that static dispatch exists to preserve is 0.1%-0.4% of the loops' work; and inverting the loop does not fill it, at 1.12x on min and 1.23x on median, both inside noise.

cites l67-monomorphisation-budget · l68-render-loop-vector-width · l149-atom-outer-loop-inversion

Measuring the strategies overturned the design's own premises. The splat frame is bind, 76-91% of it, and bind's dominant work is a single-threaded sort whose width does not depend on precision, so the bandwidth premise was wrong in its mechanism; f64 is free per frame, so both precisions stay; and the CPU pack that looked like a floor on the handoff was fused into the resolve.

cites l163-bandwidth-crossover · l190-fused-bgra8-handoff

The paradigm cannot rescue a strategy whose cost is physical. The DrawList's UI frame is 97.8-99.2% pixel traffic at 4.999-6.374 ns per visited pixel, and no choice of kernel, method or precision touches that term. What the paradigm did do is make the loss cheap to find, because the incumbent was measured first: egui's whole CPU frame is 0.1654-0.9011 ms at 1x, so the bar a port had to clear was about one millisecond, not 16.67.

cites l175-drawlist-ui-frame · l174-optical-egui-baseline

The comparison, stated plainly

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

Against a mature CPU rasteriser lights loses on 2D chrome, and not narrowly. On the identical op sets captured from eleven real optical tabs, tiny-skia draws every frame 2.22-10.16x faster than lights' DrawList, from a 4 B/px buffer against lights' 24 B/px, six times the memory, and with no pack at all.

cites l176-renderer-arms

The reason is not the algorithm and not the paradigm: 97.8-99.2% of lights' frame is pixel traffic, and the ops themselves cost under 0.02 ms of it. Serially the DrawList frame is 11.0252-28.4196 ms at 1x; over bands on 18 threads it is 1.3679-2.9135 ms, which is still 2.4-8.3x egui's whole CPU frame while it occupies every core.

cites l175-drawlist-ui-frame · l174-optical-egui-baseline

What lights keeps on that comparison is narrow and real. It passes the registered quality gate at 1x where tiny-skia fails the glyph-like target, drawing a 1 px stroke as a hairline at peak 0.6157 against the 0.90 bar where lights reads 0.9369, and its bands are bit-exact where tiny-skia's depart by up to 191 levels. It was not enough to keep the target: with the two measurements above, this is why the optical half of L-120 was withdrawn.

cites l176-renderer-arms

What stands

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

The 3D atom renderer stands, and it is the one thing here that no other crate offers: splat as the frame-rate arm, the ray caster as the reference and as the home of the gradient normal and the depth-limited interleave, surfels with stored normals, deformation by cage, the fused fill into the host's buffer, and an AppKit host a hand can drive.

cites arm:method/splat-raster · arm:method/ray-cast · arm:3d/surfel · arm:3d/cage-deform · arm:3d/depth-limited-composite · arm:host/fused-bgra8-fill · l123-frame-rate-1080p · l197-gallery-frame-times · l141-gradient-normal-shading · l153-interpenetration-composite · l122-surfel-stored-normal · l169-cage-deform-vs-refit · l186-event-path-arms

The strategy axes stand, pruned to what the evidence supports: the gaussian kernel, both methods and both precisions are KEEP, f64 because it costs nothing per frame, and both probes are KEEP because the audit probe is the certificate itself.

cites arm:kernel/gaussian · arm:precision/f32 · arm:precision/f64 · arm:probe/counter · arm:probe/audit · l163-bandwidth-crossover

The agreement contract stands, and the audit forbids cutting below it: no method row is PRUNE and both are KEEP, because two independently written renderers that converge on one image is the paradigm case in miniature, and the brute-force oracle the fast binning is checked against is the same case at its smallest.

cites arm:method/splat-raster · arm:method/ray-cast · arm:raycast/brute-force

What goes, what waits on a ruling, and what cannot be reproduced

final report2026-09-29tree daedf929

Six arms are PRUNE on evidence that holds in a 3D scene: the two density-signed kernels, the Haar and CDF 9/7 transforms, the DrawList, and the moment hierarchy. L-200 deletes them only after this report, and the records whose commands run them were re-run on this tree for that reason.

cites arm:kernel/gabor-density · arm:kernel/gauss-hermite-density · arm:fit/transform-haar · arm:fit/transform-cdf97 · arm:ui/draw-list · arm:3d/moment-hierarchy · p4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets · p5-aspect3-resolution-independence · l96-sweep-parallelism · l175-drawlist-ui-frame · l176-renderer-arms · l166-moment-matched-early-stop

Nine arms are RE-JUDGE, each with the experiment that would decide it stated and not run. Five of them share one experiment, a 3D fit the crate cannot run yet, so whether a 3D fit is coming is the PM's question, and without one they fall to PRUNE and take the fit feature with them. Whether the engine draws its own text decides the Tier 0 glyph path.

cites arm:fit/arm · arm:fit/adam · arm:kernel/super-gaussian · arm:kernel/gabor-colour · arm:kernel/gauss-hermite-colour · arm:text/tier0-glyph-path

Part of the record cannot be reproduced, and it says so. The first parallelism experiment was lost with a RAM disk and stands only as history beside the rebuild that replaced it; aspect 2's rel-L2 and byte figures came from harnesses nobody committed, and only its reach and byte-axis tables re-run; and the 7.444 ms CPU pack reads 4.643-4.658 ms under its own unchanged command. The transport study and the corpus triage are readings with no command at all, and this report quotes neither as evidence.

cites l96-sweep-parallelism · p4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets · l150-cpu-pack-term · l190-fused-bgra8-handoff

P2 — what crisp UI content costs in gaussian atoms, measured against an analytic renderer drawing the same shapes from the same definition

representation2026-09-08copy-pasteable command8 refuted
the answer

Analytic SDF rendering beats gaussian splatting on all six crisp UI content classes — meeting every registered criterion at 10.2–38.9 µs from an 83–274 byte description while the splat arm matches it on flat fill alone and never meets the 12 px glyph at any budget — but the experiment's own atom column is an upper bound on greedy matching pursuit rather than a cost of gaussians, and its tile-binning result inverts the atom-count win.

the question it was built to answer

For six classes of crisp authored UI content (flat fill, 30° straight edge, right-angle corner, 3 px curve, 1 px stroke, 12 px glyph), how many anisotropic gaussian atoms — and how many bytes and microseconds — does it cost to meet a registered sub-pixel quality criterion, and how does that compare against an analytic renderer drawing the same shapes from the same primitive description?

criterion, and its convention

Criterion::registered(), src/fit/edge.rs:30-39, re-measured from a fresh render of the emitted atoms (never read from the fitter's bookkeeping): 10–90 transition width max_transition ≤ 1.0 PIXEL; contrast ≥ 0.90 as a FRACTION OF THE TARGET's own contrast (min_contrast_frac); peak ≥ 0.90 FRACTION (min_peak_frac); FWHM 1.0 px ± 0.5 px (target_fwhm / max_fwhm_error); interior ripple ≤ 1/255 = 0.00392156862745098 in RENDERED LEVEL, not in 8-bit codes. Which sub-criteria apply is set per target by its probes (src/fit/target.rs:380-532, ProbeKind Edge/Stroke/Interior/Apex), so absent probes report nan and are not judged — T1 is judged on ripple alone, T2/T3/T4 on width+contrast, T5/T6 on peak+FWHM. A separate overfit column re-renders each cell through a camera shifted half a pixel and flags gaps above OVERFIT_FLAG_PX = 0.2 px (src/fit/report.rs:161). The whole judgement is void unless the analytic-exact control arm meets the same criterion first (control_arm_valid, exit 3).

reproduce it
cargo run -p lights --all-features --bin lights-p2 -- --out-dir docs/lights/p2/run-48 --max-atoms 48
cargo run -p lights --all-features --bin lights-p2 -- --out-dir docs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled --max-atoms 8 --tiled-field

BOTH RUN AND COMMITTED 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-48). These are not reconstructions any more: the two lines above were executed from the repository root, they wrote docs/lights/p2/run-48/ and docs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled/, and each table's own first line is the `#invocation` stamp src/bin/support/invocation.rs wrote for it. Both exit 1 — the documented "a cell never met its criterion inside --max-atoms" RESULT, not a failure. crates/lights/tests/p2_artefacts.rs reads the committed tables and pins the figures below.

TWO invocations are needed because no single run produced all the published figures. The first is the headline and the atom counts; the second is every tile figure, and its 8 atoms x 40 copies is where the scene population of 320 comes from. docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md recorded the run only as `lights-p2 --max-atoms 48`, which CANNOT run as written — no --out-dir, and the binary exits 2 without one (lights_p2.rs:174-178, returned by main() at :258) — and that line is corrected in the same commit.

THE TABLES ARE DEBUG AND THE 2026-09-08 RUN WAS RELEASE. That the two agree on every deterministic column is measured, not assumed: box_over_ellipse_area 123.406101, the four glyph atom counts, and — with the 2026-09-08 binner restored — mean_tile_list 232.023438 / max_tile_list 315 / tile_atom_pairs 237,592 all reproduce BIT-EXACTLY against the release figures published in 2026-09-08's artefacts. Wall-clock columns do not carry over and the tables say so in their own `#build` header.

The binary is feature-gated (`required-features = ["fit"]`, crates/lights/Cargo.toml:25-28). Exit-code ladder (lights_p2.rs:29-32, main() at :253-276): 0 clean; 1 a target never met its criterion inside --max-atoms (a RESULT, flagged in the table, and the budget was NOT extended until something passed); 2 no --out-dir; 3 the analytic control arm failed its own criterion, so the criterion is invalid and NO ATOM COUNT IS PRINTED; 4 an error, including LightsError::BudgetExhausted.

Other flags (lights_p2.rs:13-27): --targets 1..6|all, --blends over|normalised|all (the BLEND PAIR only — the kernel families belong to lights-p3), --profiles prefiltered|contract|all, --tile (16), --repeats (5, after one discarded warm-up), --depth (10), --max-width (1.0), --min-contrast (0.9), --no-sensitivity.

RE-RUN 2026-09-29 (L-199), both commands into scratch: every PNG byte-identical and every deterministic table identical to the committed runs, with two differences that are the tree's and not the figures'. sweep.tsv has gained a twelfth column, prefix. And report.tsv's candidates_tested reads 0 in the committed tables and 8192 today (24 rows at --max-atoms 48, 48 in the tiled run): the line that fills it was last changed in eb232f57 (L-96, 2026-09-28), after these tables were written, so the committed 0 is an unfilled column and not a count of zero candidates.

tile saturation on a 512×512 field of 40 copiesWITHDRAWN as a statement about today's crate. Published: the average tile's candidate list holds 232.023438 of the 320 atoms (72.5%), worst tile 315. Measured 2026-09-15 on the same cell: 25.747070 of 320, worst tile 37 — a 9.01x prunedocs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled/report.tsv, t2-straight-edge - gaussian - prefiltered - tiled: mean_tile_list 25.747070, max_tile_list 37, tile_atom_pairs 26,365, and the whole 24-cell tiled sweep peaks at 30.583008. The published 232.023438 / 315 / 237,592 reproduces BIT-EXACTLY, but only with the per-tile rejection in src/method/splat/bin.rs removed — which is how the prune was measured rather than argued. That rejection landed in commit 6b1b9ab8 on 2026-09-14, after P2 ran: span_of_box still supplies the candidate rectangle, and each tile in it is then dropped unless the atom's own whitened quadratic form reaches it. So the sentence 'an 8-atom edge is 8 atoms tested against essentially every pixel' describes a binner this crate no longer has. On T1 tiled, mean_tile_list is still 78.235352 of 80, because a flat fill legitimately covers the field.
why a 64×64 viewport cannot measure tile costT4 reports exactly 32 tile-atom pairs under BOTH profiles at natural size; the same fits tiled 40× report 1,280 and 27,472harness_rs_comment/what_is_crossed_with_what.md, "Why the tiled field exists"; the tiled figures reproduce in p2_run_a/report.tsv (t4-curve gaussian prefiltered 1,280; gaussian contract 27,472). At 32×32 with a 16 px tile there are four tiles, so an 8-atom fit saturates at 32 regardless of atom shape — the equality across profiles is the proof, not an anecdote.
the one class splats win: flat-fill interior rippleNormalised exactly 0.000000; Over 0.000198 and 0.000201p2_run_48/report.tsv t1-flat-fill interior_ripple. A partition of unity reaching a rendered pixel, which alpha compositing provably cannot do. Both still MEET the 1/255 = 0.00392 criterion; the win is exactness, not passing. It is also a filled rectangle, which the analytic arm renders exactly for free.

The detector is nan wherever the target carries no edge probe — half the matrix is unpoliced.

sensitivity of the answer to its own constants, measured on T2alpha_cutoff over three decades moves the width by under 0.3%; screen_dilation from 1/12 to 0.25 moves it by ~46%harness_rs_comment/what_is_crossed_with_what.md, "Sensitivity holds the representation fixed". The plan's own mitigation threshold (withhold the headline if the width moves >10%) is NOT triggered at edge scale. The 10.16% spread an earlier stage measured was at d = 6σ.
fitter calibration that licenses reading atom counts as a cost at alla target that IS one atom is recovered by one atom to under 1 part in 255 at the worst pixel, at six refinement passes; at three passes the same case stalls at ten parts in 255greedy_rs_comment/objective_is_not_the_criterion.md; DEFAULT_REFINE_ITERATIONS = 6 for that reason. The ladder is a deterministic 9 knobs × 8 levels × 2 signs = 144 renders per refinement pass.
the flat-fill win's cross-method second opinion, which the certified path refuses to giveworst channel splat against ray cast: 0.1411988743075459 under Normalised, 0.12389689254766625 under Over — a ratio of 1.1396MEASURED 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-52) by tests/agreement.rs::the_normalised_blend_is_refused_by_the_certified_path_and_compared_without_it, on spheres-anisotropic-no-ground at 320x180 f64, both methods at contract settings. run_pair REFUSES GaussianNormalised — it gates on blend_is_over() first, which the coverage mix fails, and on curve_is_gaussian() second, which it passes — because the bound in src/bound.rs is derived for transmittance-weighted compositing and Normalised has no transmittance. So the figures above are UNCERTIFIED: they are what the two methods did on one scene, not a bound on what they may do. The Over figure is the one the certified path passes at, which is why the RATIO is the quantity recorded and asserted rather than the absolute gap.
what it settled
  • Analytic rendering beats gaussian splatting on every crisp UI content class and it is not close: one clamp(0.5 − sdf) per pixel meets every registered criterion on all six targets at 10.2–38.9 µs per frame from an 83–274 byte description, with no fitter and no failure mode (p2_run_48/report.tsv analytic-sdf rows).
  • On the 12 px glyph — the class the whole question was about — the splat arm produced no passing result in any of four configurations at any budget, while analytic-sdf meets it at 6 primitives / 274 B / 38.9 µs; the failing fits cost 1,344–2,464 B (f64) or 336–616 B (f16) and 54.9–120.7 µs.
  • Splats win exactly one class and it is the one nobody needs them for: flat fill under normalised blending gives interior ripple of exactly 0.000000 — a partition of unity reaching a rendered pixel, which alpha compositing provably cannot do (Over leaves 0.000198) — and a flat fill is a filled rectangle, which analytic renders exactly for free.
  • Cost scales with corners and curvature, not area: 2 atoms for a fill, 8–10 for a straight edge, 14 for a 3 px curve, 2–12 for a 1 px stroke, 17–25 for a corner, and never for a glyph — a ~10× spread that a single "atoms per glyph" figure would have hidden entirely.
  • The binning result inverted the atom-count win AGAINST THE BINNER OF 2026-09-08, and half of it has since been withdrawn. What stands: the axis-aligned footprint box reaches 123.4x the true ellipse AREA (123.406101, reproduced bit-exactly 2026-09-15 from docs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled/report.tsv). What is WITHDRAWN 2026-09-15: "on a 512x512 field of 40 copies the average tile's candidate list holds 232 of the 320 atoms in the scene, so an 8-atom edge is 8 atoms tested against essentially every pixel". Commit 6b1b9ab8 (2026-09-14) gave the splat binner a per-tile rejection against the atom's own whitened quadratic form, and the same cell now measures 25.747070 of 320 — a 9.01x prune. The old figure returns bit-exactly (232.023438 / 315 / 237,592) with that one rejection removed, which is how the prune was measured. The lasting finding is the DISTINCTION: box_over_ellipse_area measures the atoms and mean_tile_list measures the binner, and quoting the first as evidence about tile cost is the error.
  • A 64×64 viewport structurally cannot measure tile cost — T4 reports exactly 32 tile-atom pairs under both profiles at natural size because 8 atoms × 4 tiles is the ceiling — and the equality across profiles is the proof, not an anecdote; the tiled row carries quality_measured = false and prints n/a rather than no/never so a cost row is never read as a quality failure.
  • The two control arms are deliberately both shipped so the comparison cannot be rigged: analytic-exact is the quality ceiling (reusing the ground-truth coverage routine, 23.1 ms on the glyph, three orders slower) and analytic-sdf is the cost contestant, with a test asserting the cheap one measurably differs from the ceiling so it is not a free straw man.
  • The harness itself is sound in the one way that matters: every judged column is re-measured from a fresh render of the emitted atoms and never read from the fitter's own bookkeeping, so a broken fitter cannot lie to it — deliberate breaks produced 3.6 px, contrast 0.24 and nan, not plausible numbers.
  • In 22 of 24 cells (the run's own report.tsv; the write-up says 20) the FITTER stopped rather than the budget, so every "never meets" is a statement about greedy matching pursuit with coordinate descent and every atom count is an upper bound on the true cost of the representation.
  • The fitter descends residual_energy while the report judges edge geometry, and those are different functions — a four-atom construction passes the glyph criterion at residual energy 68.19 against an empty scene's 50.74, i.e. it passes while being worse than painting nothing, and the 15-atom fit that actually reproduces the glyph fails.
  • Closing the fitter confound needs joint gradient descent, which the crate does not have; it is now a backlog item in docs/plan/TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md §8 together with the calibration measurement that would turn the confound into a number.
  • The experiment refuted a standing project premise: TWO_HORIZON_PLAN §1 had said since 2026-07-05 that "the data model survives, the painter is swapped" and that lights is the engine optical eventually runs on; commit 75701a56 amends that in place rather than deleting it, so what was believed and what changed it both stay visible.
what it refuted
  • THE PROJECT'S OWN STANDING PREMISE. TWO_HORIZON_PLAN §1's "the data model survives, the painter is swapped" (held since 2026-07-05) does not hold for crisp chrome. Amended in place, with date and evidence, by commit 75701a56 "docs: the painter is not swapped — record the measurement that contradicts the premise". What survives is narrower: analytic draws everything already held as geometry (fills, borders, rules, wires, text); lights draws what has no analytic description to hold.
  • THE IMAGE-GS PREMISE DID NOT REPRODUCE. "A straight edge is two atoms under normalisation" does not hold here, for a structural reason and not a tuning failure: this crate's Normalised is a coverage MIX, background·(1−c) + (weighted/weight)·c with c = min(weight,1), which reduces to Image-GS Eq. 5's pure top-K ratio only where total weight reaches one. An edge sharp enough to meet a sub-pixel criterion has σ²/d < 0.23, so midpoint weight is far below a half, c collapses, and the mix fades toward the background exactly at the edge. Raising opacity cannot rescue it — covering a 32 px deep half-plane with an across-edge σ under half a pixel would need opacity of order e^2500. Normalised lost to Over on all five non-fill targets.
  • THE RUN'S OWN ATOM COLUMN, refuted by its own authors in both directions. Downward: one hand-placed needle meets the 1 px stroke criterion where the fitter needed twelve, collapsing all four T5 cells from never/12/never/never to 2/1/2/1, because the fitter minimises L2 energy and the L2-optimal stroke does NOT meet the criterion while a deliberately-too-thin needle does. Upward: a four-atom construction passes the glyph criterion at residual energy 68.19 against an empty scene's 50.74.
  • THE PREDICTED SIZE OF THE BINNING WASTE. Correction 5 predicted ~6× for the axis-aligned footprint box against the true ellipse; measured 123.4×, i.e. "confirmed harder than predicted", and it inverts the atom-count win rather than merely qualifying it.
  • P2'S OWN READING OF THE BINNING RATIO AS A KERNEL PROPERTY, refuted later by aspect 1: the ratio is 4r²√(sxx·syy)/(πr²√det), r² cancels, so it measures fitted-covariance ANISOTROPY and not the kernel. P2 closed "It is the span_of_box AABB, and an OBB or ellipse test is the fix." THAT FIX SHIPPED on 2026-09-14 in commit 6b1b9ab8 and the record did not notice until 2026-09-15: bin_and_sort still takes span_of_box as its CANDIDATE RECTANGLE, then drops each tile in it that the atom's own whitened quadratic form does not reach. Measured consequence, a 9.01x prune on P2's own most-cited tile cell. What remains open is narrower than P2's sentence: the SPAN is still axis-aligned, so tiles the ellipse cannot reach are still enumerated and pay a rejection test, and nobody has measured whether an oriented span is worth anything on top of a 9x prune.
  • PRIOR-ART CLAIMS THE READING PASS KILLED, recorded in docs/lights/CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:100-111 so they are not repeated: "gaussians terrible at hard edges" — SPLIT, true only of Image-GS as configured (σ∈[5,10], fp16 means); "a step costs unbounded atoms" — REFUTED; "30 dB/160 KB is on photographs, the favourable case" — REFUTED, it is a stylised illustration and photographs are the paper's UNFAVOURABLE case; "a UI is ~90% crisp chrome" — no source, and by area a node graph is mostly flat fill, the cheapest content there is; "hybrid split at text, borders, 1 px strokes" — right conclusion, WRONG SPLIT LINE, borders are cheap and text is the expensive one. Only "authored rects don't need a splat renderer" survived untouched.
  • THE STRUCTURE TENSOR'S CREDIT FOR THE ANSWER. It is load-bearing for the SEED and not for the result: at one refinement pass it is nearly three times better than isotropic (7.55 vs 20.81 on the thin stroke), but at six passes the two are within a few percent (12.55 vs 12.39). A report crediting the tensor for a final atom count is crediting the wrong part of the fitter.
  • "MORE REFINEMENT IS BETTER" AS A MONOTONE PROPERTY. Refining the current atom harder is NOT monotone in the final result — a better fit to today's residual can leave a worse configuration for the atoms that follow. Greedy myopia is visible in one column: T5 under Over+contract meets at 2 atoms and does NOT meet at 3 or 4.
what it does NOT settle
  • THE CASE FOR SPLATS WAS NEVER TESTED. Every target tested was a shape an SDF renders exactly, from a description you already hold. The content splats exist for — photographs, soft shadows, band-limited noise, fitted textures, continuous fields — has NO ANALYTIC ARM AT ALL and is entirely unmeasured. This work bounds where splats lose. It says nothing about where they win.
  • THE ONE CLASS SPLATS WON HAS NO CERTIFIED CROSS-METHOD CHECK — narrowed 2026-09-15, not removed. P2's flat-fill win runs on GaussianNormalised and the agreement instrument refuses that kernel outright, so the result the whole splat case rests on is the one result with no second opinion from the other renderer. BURNDOWN L-52 narrowed the consequence: what is missing is the BOUND, not the comparison. Both methods render the coverage mix, and on spheres-anisotropic-no-ground at 320x180 f64 they differ by 0.1412 worst channel against 0.1239 under Over — the same order, and Over is the blend the certified path passes at. Deriving a Normalised divergence bound in src/bound.rs remains undone and is not claimed. The ruling is in src/agreement_rs_comment/the_one_class_with_no_certified_bound.md and the falsifier is executable: the test asserts the RATIO stays under 1.5, because the absolute gap moves with the scene and the ratio does not.
  • RESOLUTION INDEPENDENCE IS UNMEASURED. Everything here is at one scale, and one fitted description surviving a zoom range is the property a per-resolution rasteriser lacks — the only structural advantage splats have. (Later addressed as aspect 3 in docs/lights/P5_RESULTS.md, and P2_RESULTS.md:9-15 warns the four tables on the published page are NOT in one currency — P2 ran --max-atoms 48 against a pass criterion, aspect 3 ran 8 against probe widths — and must not be cross-quoted.)
  • EVERY ATOM COUNT IS AN UPPER BOUND and a statement about greedy matching pursuit rather than about gaussians. Closing that needs joint gradient descent, which the crate does not have.
  • TWO BLIND SPOTS MAKE THE FITTER CONFOUND INVISIBLE FROM INSIDE THE REPORT (commit 6ac92f33): the 10–90 width is TRANSLATION-INVARIANT, so a ground truth shifted a whole pixel still reports "meets"; and the overfit detector is nan wherever there is no edge probe, which is 12 of the 24 cells.
  • THE ATOM COUNTS ARE A FUNCTION OF THE --max-atoms FLAG, not just of the content. "The sweep is the first n atoms of the FINAL fit, not the fit truncated at n" — refine_tail re-refines earlier atoms after later ones are placed, so running with --max-atoms 2 does NOT reproduce the n = 2 row of a --max-atoms 8 run (harness_rs_comment/what_is_crossed_with_what.md). Observed: the corner reads 8/8/8/8 at --max-atoms 8, 23/17/24/24 at 24, and 25/17/48/48 at 48.
  • THE REPORT HEADER DOES NOT RECORD THE FLAG THAT SETS THE NUMBERS. header_lines (src/fit/report.rs:393-421) emits hardware, build, precision, method, sort, tile, threads, PIXEL_BOX_VARIANCE, alpha_cutoff, Z0, DZ, quadrature_depth, undecided_area, field, the six criterion values, control_arm_valid and the viewports — but NOT --max-atoms and NOT whether --tiled-field ran. The comment folder is literally named a_table_without_its_header_is_unreproducible.md; the header is still missing the two flags that most change the answer.
  • TWO CAVEATS THAT BOTH FAVOUR SPLATS, stated on the page so the comparison is not read as harsher than it is: Atom<f64> is a 3D atom carrying a quaternion and a z coordinate these 2D targets never use, and a 2D-native atom would be about 8 scalars; and a fair like-for-like read is the f16 row (28 B/atom), not f64. Even at f16 the smallest fit that FAILS costs more than the analytic description that MEETS.
  • RENDER TIMES ARE RELATIVE, NOT A FRAME BUDGET: single-threaded f64 on a 64×64 field (32×32 for T4 and T6), five repeats after one discarded warm-up. analytic-exact's wall_nanos is explicitly "the weakest number in the struct" (analytic_rs_comment/two_arms.md).
  • THE OPACITY CAP HANDICAPS THE NORMALISED ARM. Opacity is clamped to [2·alpha_cutoff, 1] on BOTH arms; under Over that costs nothing (alpha is capped at 0.99 anyway) but under Normalised opacity scales the weight and weight is what buys coverage. Kept because the two arms must search the same box or the comparison measures the box — but the coverage deficit it creates is a real property of the coverage mix.
  • THE FITTER'S OPENING SEED IS DEGENERATE ON UNIFORM RESIDUALS. The argmax scan is row-major first-wins, so on a flat fill or the deep interior of T2's ink half-plane every candidate ties, the seed lands at pixel (0,0) with a structure tensor of exactly zero and coherence zero, and the first atom is seeded isotropic. Left as-is because it is deterministic and stated.
  • P2 NOW HAS COMMITTED ARTEFACTS, and auditing them immediately moved three published figures — so the entry below is superseded and the lesson is kept. BURNDOWN L-48, closed 2026-09-15: docs/lights/p2/run-48/ and docs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled/ hold both invocations' tables, each stamped with its own command, read by crates/lights/tests/p2_artefacts.rs. Of four figures checked, 123.406101 and the four glyph atom counts reproduced bit-exactly, "232 of 320" named a binner that no longer exists, and "20 of 24" was 22. L-48's OWN next step was also wrong and that is worth keeping: it said to ADD an --out-dir TSV writer to lights-p2 and lights-p4, and both binaries already had one on the day the entry was written. Nothing needed building; the runs needed making. The p4 half is L-134.
  • NEITHER HAND-BUILT COUNTEREXAMPLE IS BUILT ANYWHERE IN THE TREE. BURNDOWN.toml L-44 (raised 2026-09-09, still open): grep for 68.19/50.74/meets_criterion over tests/fit_greedy.rs and tests/fit_edge.rs returns nothing, and no greedy test ever runs the metrology. Only ONE direction is tested — the closed-form pair beside the fitter's two atoms on T2. The two counterexamples the P2 report actually rests on remain prose only.
  • THREE PUBLISHED FIGURES I COULD NOT RECONCILE WITH ANY SURVIVING ARTEFACT. (a) "in 20 of 24 cells the fitter stopped" — the run's own report.tsv gives 22 of 24 (improvement_exhausted=yes), the two exceptions being t3 under gaussian-normalised. (b) "17–27 for a corner" — no run in the scratchpad contains a 27-atom corner; the maximum anywhere is 25 (p2_run_48, Over·prefiltered), and no cell in any p2/p3 report.tsv has atoms=27. (c) T5's "never/12/never/never" — p2_run_48 reads 21-never / 2-meets / 12-never / 11-never, so the Over·contract cell already met at 2 before any hand-building. Would be settled by re-running command (1) and diffing against docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md.
  • THE 123x BINNING RESULT IS NO LONGER AN OPEN DEFECT IN CODE — corrected 2026-09-15. The sentence this replaces ("span_of_box is still a pure axis-aligned box→tile-span and still the only binner ... the fix P2 named has not been started") was already false when L-48 was read: the per-tile Mahalanobis rejection is at src/method/splat/bin.rs, added in 6b1b9ab8 on 2026-09-14, and the ray caster got the exact silhouette conic in L-20 (src/extent.rs). span_of_box does survive — as the CANDIDATE RECTANGLE that the rejection then filters — which is why grepping for the fix's name found nothing and reading the loop body would have found it. What is genuinely still open: the span itself is axis-aligned, so unreachable tiles are enumerated and rejected rather than never enumerated, and that residue has never been measured.
  • THE SIBLING BINARY'S INSTRUMENT WAS LATER FOUND WRONG in a way worth knowing when reading any of these tables. In lights-p5, nan &lt; criterion.max_transition is false in Rust, so every glyph row in the TSV read "no" where the honest answer was "this target carries no transition probe", and the FWHM verdict was never written to the file at all — the printed table was right the whole time and the machine-readable artefact a report generator consumes was the wrong one, reading as though analytic SDF never meets the glyph and CONTRADICTING P2 ON THE SAME PAGE (docs/lights/P5_RESULTS.md:235-258; fixed, with a regression case and a mutation). P2's own report.tsv does not share this bug — t1 rows carry nan on every width column and still read meets=yes — but the class of defect is the crate's recurring one.

Aspect 1 — is the gaussian the wrong atom curve? A seven-arm sweep of super-gaussian beta plus a compact bump, on six crisp-UI targets

representation2026-09-08copy-pasteable command7 refuted
the answer

The atom curve is not inert — a super-gaussian at beta=4 closes the right-angle-corner gap to exact box coverage 11.5x (0.205 px against the gaussian's 2.365 px) and is the only kernel meeting there — but the ranking reverses at pixel scale (gaussian and compact bump meet the 1 px stroke at 2 atoms while every beta>=2 fails on peak at 0.78-0.80 against a 0.9 floor) and no kernel meets the 12 px glyph at any budget, so the axis narrows from 7 arms to 4 rather than collapsing to a point.

the question it was built to answer

Is the gaussian the right atom curve, or does varying the atom's shape — a super-gaussian exponent beta, or a compactly-supported bump — change what crisp UI content costs in atoms and in binning work?

criterion, and its convention

Per-target pass/fail from Criterion::registered(), printed as the #criterion.* header of every TSV: 10-90 transition width <= 1.0 DEVICE PIXELS with contrast fraction >= 0.9 for the edge/corner/curve classes; peak fraction >= 0.9 with FWHM 1.0 +/- 0.5 device px for the stroke/glyph classes; interior ripple <= 1/255. The headline column is NOT the absolute width but delta_to_control_px — signed distance from the analytic-exact arm, which is offline quadrature of exact box coverage of the same shape and is the ceiling, not a contestant (its own row is 0.000000 by construction). Convention is device pixels at the target's natural viewport (64x64 for t1/t2/t3/t5, 32x32 for t4/t6), single sampling rate, profile contract (screen dilation zero) — chosen because the prefilter dilation is a gaussian identity and switching it on "would apply a gaussian assumption to non-gaussian atoms and confound the axis under test" (lights_p3.rs:27-29). Cost is counted as KERNEL EVALUATIONS, not nanoseconds.

reproduce it
cargo run --release --features fit --bin lights-p3 -- --out-dir <DIR>

VERIFIED 2026-09-14 that this invocation form is accepted by this tree: run from the repo root into a `mktemp -d` with tiny arguments added (`--targets 1 --kernels gaussian --max-atoms 1 --repeats 1 --no-strips`), both exactly as written and with `-p lights` inserted; both built, parsed every flag and exited 1, the documented never-met result. The published default run was NOT re-executed: the FORM is verified, the FIGURES below are not.

Every other flag is left at its default: --targets all, --kernels sweep, --profile contract, --max-atoms 8, --tile 16, --repeats 5, --depth 10, --max-width 1.0, --min-contrast 0.9, --gap 3, --zoom 4, strips on, --sensitivity NOT passed.

NO command line is recorded anywhere in the repo — this one is reconstructed from the binary's own USAGE block (crates/lights/src/bin/lights_p3.rs:17-50) checked against the artefact headers: report.tsv's `#tile 16`, `#quadrature_depth 10`, `#criterion.max_transition_px 1`, `#criterion.min_contrast_frac 0.9`, every row `profile=contract` and `atoms_available<=8`, all seven CURVE_SWEEP kernels and all six targets present, strip-*-4x.png written, and sensitivity.tsv containing header lines only.

Expect EXIT CODE 1, not 0: 27 cells never met the criterion inside --max-atoms 8, and lights_p3.rs:418 returns ExitCode::FAILURE for exactly that (documented at lights_p3.rs:46-50 as "a RESULT, flagged in the table, and expected here"). The `fit` feature is non-default (crates/lights/Cargo.toml:49-50) and lights-p3 declares required-features=["fit"] (Cargo.toml:30-33), so omitting --features fit silently builds no binary.

what it settled
  • The control is real and holds end to end: the super-gaussian at beta=1 is bit-identical to the existing Gaussian in eval_screen, eval_ray, support_screen, support_ray and prefilter at both f64 and f32, and a whole rendered frame is bit-identical through both splat and raycast dispatch (tests/kernel_curve.rs:147, :202, :226); I re-verified it independently from the artefacts by SHA-256 on all six rendered PNGs and they are byte-identical.
  • Exactness is not arranged: there is no beta == 1 branch anywhere — super_gaussian_response is (-(HALF*quadratic).powf(beta)).exp() (src/kernel/supergaussian.rs:24-26) — and the comment folder states plainly that a branch would have made the control test vacuous (supergaussian_rs_comment/shape.md:13-16).
  • Beta is not inert on extended geometry: beta=4 lands 0.205224 px from exact box coverage on the right-angle corner where the gaussian sits 2.365348 px away, an 11.526x reduction in the gap to the ceiling, and it is the only one of the seven arms that meets the corner criterion at all (at 8 atoms).
  • The ranking REVERSES at pixel scale: on the 1 px stroke the gaussian and the compact bump both meet at 2 atoms (peak 0.930980 and 0.909371) while every beta >= 2 fails on peak at 0.780-0.805 against a 0.9 floor, and beta=0.5 fails hardest at 0.697639.
  • No atom curve rescues the 12 px glyph: all seven kernels report meets_criterion=no at 8 atoms while both analytic arms meet, so the P2 conclusion that analytic wins crisp UI content survives the whole curve axis unchanged.
  • beta=8 was eliminated on the instrument rather than on its headline numbers: it is the only kernel above 0.4 px of sample-grid overfit gap ON THE CORNER, at 0.509744 px between the fitted and half-pixel-shifted grids, so its cheapness there is aliasing cancellation at sampled pixel positions rather than representation.
  • The confound cuts in the winner's disfavour, so the shape advantage is a LOWER bound: the greedy search's constants were calibrated against exp(-r^2/2), single-atom recovery is 0.93/255 for the gaussian against 1.38/255 for beta=4, and beta=4 still wins the corner by 2.160124 px.
  • The compact bump's exact finite support does buy real binning work — 1.4049x fewer kernel evaluations over T2-T6, and on the corner 91 tile-atom pairs against 99 with a mean tile candidate list of 5.6875 against 6.1875 — which is exactly and only the part of the compact-support hypothesis that compact_rs_comment/support.md:35-39 predicted would move.
  • The axis narrowed from 7 arms to 4 rather than collapsing: beta=4 as headline, beta=2 as the conservative pick, gaussian as control and thin-feature arm, and the compact bump carried forward to the tiling/LOD aspect where a true support radius matters more than it did here.
  • The blend axis was held on purpose and a test enforces it: tests/kernel_curve.rs:477 asserts every kernel on CURVE_SWEEP blends with Over, with the message that a second axis moving inside the first 'is the exact mistake this run exists to stop repeating'; lights_p3 exists as a separate binary from lights_p2 for the same reason (lights_p3_rs_comment/one_aspect.md:1-9).
  • The cross-method agreement certificate refuses non-gaussian curves rather than silently certifying them: tests/kernel_curve.rs:540 asserts run_pair returns ContractViolation::KernelCurveNotGaussian for compact-bump and for beta=2, and passes with zero failures for beta=1, because the per-atom divergence bound is a derivative bound on exp(-r^2/2) and on nothing else.
  • The compact bump's radius is not a free parameter: R = sqrt(10) is fixed by matching the gaussian's second moment so that 'sigma' means the same thing under both kernels, and tests/kernel_curve.rs:454 integrates the moment numerically over 400,000 steps and fails if the constant is edited.
what it refuted
  • THE COMPACT-SUPPORT HYPOTHESIS, refuted by its own authors: exact finite support was expected to collapse the binning waste that P2 reported at 123x, and it did the opposite — on the corner box_over_ellipse_area ROSE to 48.884148 against the gaussian's 14.378581. The reason is algebraic and closes the question for every future curve: the ratio is 4r^2*sqrt(sxx*syy) / (pi*r^2*sqrt(det)), r^2 cancels, so it measures fitted-covariance anisotropy and no atom curve can move it. The waste is the axis-aligned box against anisotropic atoms, which is an OBB fix, not a kernel question.
  • The premise that the kernel axis could be fixed at the gaussian and dropped — refuted by beta's 11.5x effect on the corner.
  • The prior expectation that a flatter shoulder would help everywhere — refuted at pixel scale, where beta >= 2 fails the 1 px stroke on peak and the gaussian and compact bump meet at 2 atoms.
  • A hope carried in from P2 that a different atom curve might rescue the glyph — refuted: 0 of 7 kernels meet t6 at any budget within 8 atoms.
  • Refuted by this harvest, against the run's OWN headline: 'it is the only kernel tripping the sample-grid overfit flag' (commit e85fdc0a) is false on the run's own instrument. OVERFIT_FLAG_PX is 0.2 (src/fit/report.rs:161) and stdout.txt reports 'SAMPLE-GRID OVERFIT FLAGGED on 3 cell(s)': t3/beta8 0.5097, t4-curve/beta0.5 0.2029, and t4-curve/beta2 0.5749 — which is the LARGEST overfit gap in the entire run, and it belongs to beta=2, the arm the run kept as its conservative pick. The published page's narrower wording ('beta=8 is the only kernel above 0.4 px' in a table column that shows the CORNER only) is true; the commit message's and P2_RESULTS.md:112-115's unqualified version is not.
  • Refuted by this harvest: the cost figure for beta=0.5 does not reproduce. Recomputing kernel_evaluations over T2-T6 by the exact recipe in scripts/report_page.py gives 41,318/23,752 = 1.7396x, i.e. 1.74x more expensive; the commit message and docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:100 both say 1.75x. The other four ratios (1.82, 2.39, 4.00, 1.40) reproduce to two decimals.
  • Narrowed, not refuted: BURNDOWN.toml L-48 says aspect 1 has 'no artefacts on disk'. That is true of the REPOSITORY — find over the repo returns no p3 directory — but the full artefact set survives outside it, 123 files at <scratchpad>/p3_atom_curve/ dated 2026-09-08 14:45, and every table number in this record was re-derived from them today without re-running anything.
what it does NOT settle
  • NO ABSOLUTE ATOM COUNT IS CLAIMED. 27 of 42 cells never met the criterion inside --max-atoms 8 and the fitter, not the budget, stopped them — exactly as P2 found. Only relative ranking is asserted. No PSNR anywhere.
  • The comparison measures the kernel AND the search's affinity for that kernel, together, and they cannot be separated by running the same fitter harder. src/fit/greedy_rs_comment/the_search_is_gaussian_tuned.md:31-40: 'Any claim of the form "curve X needs fewer atoms than curve Y" is therefore a claim about THIS greedy matching pursuit', and the honest bound is that 1.4 to 4.9 parts in 255 of unrecovered signal on a target that is exactly one atom bounds how much of a per-curve difference could be search rather than shape.
  • THE INSTRUMENT LIES, first count: the confound numbers everyone quotes (0.93/255 vs 1.38/255) are PROSE, not assertions. BURNDOWN.toml L-8 records, from a read-only audit, that tests/fit_greedy.rs asserts only the (4 tight, 4 loose) split via recovery_ceiling(kernel) &gt; 1.0/255.0 used as a boolean discriminator, and that SEARCH_LIMITED_RECOVERY = 5.0/255.0 (tests/fit_greedy.rs:617) is never compared against a measured gap — 'if b8's gap degraded from 4.16/255 to 40/255 the test would still pass'.
  • THE INSTRUMENT LIES, second count: lights-p3 is the ONE experiment binary with no test and no mutation row. BURNDOWN.toml L-26 and L-50: p2, p4, p5, lights-agreement and lights-bench each have CARGO_BIN_EXE end-to-end tests; p3 appears nowhere outside Cargo.toml:31-32, its own 444 lines and its comment folder, and scripts/mutations.json has p2:3, p4:3, p5:5, p3:0. 'The real cost of the gap is that a p3 whose output silently changed shape would be caught by nobody.'
  • The kernel-evaluation cost headline is dominated by two targets: t2 and t3 alone are 22,731 of the gaussian's 23,752 evaluations (95.70%). Per-target the ratio for beta=4 swings from 1.136x on the stroke to 6.542x on the curve, so '2.39x cheaper' is essentially the corner-and-edge figure, not a general one.
  • 'Cost vs gaussian' is kernel EVALUATIONS, not time, and it also conflates the per-atom cost of the curve with how many atoms the fitter chose to place (beta=8 met t4-curve at 1 atom, hence 37 evaluations). The measured wall-clock on the corner separates nothing: gaussian 143,108 +/- 41,031 ns against beta=4's 151,717 +/- 44,420 ns, every 95% CI overlapping every other, with beta=4 nominally slower. Nothing in this run shows the evaluation saving reaching a frame.
  • Beta does not renormalise. The second moment of the curve moves with beta, so an atom's covariance stops meaning 'the rendered blob's variance' away from beta=1; the fitter absorbs that in log_scale, which is why the sweep reads as a shape comparison — and why an ABSOLUTE width at one beta must never be compared with an absolute width at another without saying so (supergaussian_rs_comment/shape.md:32-39).
  • The profile is contract (dilation off) on purpose, so the run says NOTHING about how any of these curves behave under the prefilter. The moment dilation is exact only for a gaussian; for beta != 1 and for the compact bump it is a moment-matched approximation, and running the sweep with it on 'would have measured a gaussian identity applied to non-gaussian atoms and called the result a property of the curve'. The SIZE of that confound is explicitly deferred to a later aspect.
  • One sampling rate, one viewport per target. Resolution independence was untested here and is aspect 3's question; aspect 3 later found no atom arm meets the corner at ANY rung, consistent with this run only at its single scale.
  • The published header banner is P2's, not p3's: report.tsv carries #field 512x512 holding 40 copies while every one of the 54 rows has field=natural, and candidates_tested is 0 in all 54 rows — so the tile-saturation counter that BURNDOWN L-48 says 'would make the ratio reproducible on demand' recorded nothing in this run.
  • The 123x binning-waste figure the refutation is argued against is P2's cross-cell maximum, not a corner number; this run's own maximum box_over_ellipse_area is 67.787 (t5/beta0.5) and its corner gaussian value is 14.379. src/kernel/compact_rs_comment/support.md:38 cites the same P2 result as 'up to 89.5x'. Three different numbers name the same P2 quantity across three of this run's own sources.
  • The '7 arms to 4' framing counts beta=1 and gaussian as separate arms although they are bit-identical, so the sweep covers 6 distinct curves, of which 3 (beta=4, beta=2, compact) plus the gaussian survive.
  • The suite figures in the commit — 283 tests with --all-features, 226 with default, 93-mutation manifest re-run with 0 escapes and four gates green — were not re-checked here and cannot be without running cargo test --features fit (or --all-features) and python3 scripts/mutate.py, which this harvest was forbidden to do.
  • The signed-atom arms are absent from the sweep entirely and that is a fitter limit, not a result: the greedy ladder carries no frequency, orientation or phase knob (recorded for aspect 2 at BURNDOWN L-124's sibling and in src/bin/lights_p4.rs:198-205).

Aspect 2 — can a signed atom (Gabor, Gauss-Hermite) or a wavelet basis (Haar, CDF 9/7) beat the plain gaussian atom on eight content classes, and on which axis?

representation2026-09-09copy-pasteable command10 refuted
the answer

The signed atom earns nothing (the optimiser drives the Gabor carrier to zero wherever it helps, and where a real carrier survives the Gabor is WORSE than the gaussian, which a strict superset cannot be — so that cell marks a search failure, not a representational one); sign placement is ruled COLOUR 4-0 on the new classes and 5-3 overall; Haar represents a 30° step exactly at 1024 coefficients while CDF 9/7 overshoots a [0,1] truth to a peak error of 1.5377; and the run's own verify pass then demolished the byte half of the headline, showing it rested on an unpinned 4-vs-8-byte convention, a dyadically aligned noise lattice, and an undocumented MIN_COARSE_SIDE. RE-MEASURED 2026-09-28 (L-108) under ONE f32 currency with atoms at their native nine scalars (36 B an atom): a transform byte-win SURVIVES, but only for CDF 9/7 and only on n1, n3 and n4 - by 1.04x to 3.13x on the transform's pessimistic bracket - while the atoms are cheaper on n2 and on every T-class rung past the first two, and Haar wins exactly one of 160 rungs.

the question it was built to answer

On the four content classes an analytic SDF cannot express plus four carried forward from P2, does a signed oscillatory atom or a wavelet filter bank beat the plain gaussian atom under matched N-term nonlinear approximation — and does the answer survive being measured in terms, in bytes, and in reach?

criterion, and its convention

N-term nonlinear approximation: keep the N largest coefficients, or keep N atoms, and plot error against N. TWO error metrics, always both, never combined (src/fit/nterm_rs_comment/why_not_the_standard_metric.md): relative L2 = sqrt(residual_energy / truth_energy), lower is better, and max-absolute error over the RGB image; the file states they rank the same pair of images in opposite orders (a 0.03 drift across a 16x16 field vs one pixel wrong by 0.30 is ordered 2.77:1 by relative L2 and 10:1 by max-abs). PSNR/SSIM/MSE are banned crate-wide by tests/layering.rs. Bytes are reported as a BRACKET, not a number: for a transform arm bytes_explicit = N*(4 + ceil(log2(3*W*H))/8) (upper bound; 14 index bits at 64x64) and bytes_combinatorial = 4N + log2(C(3*W*H, N))/8 (lower bound); for an atom arm bytes_explicit bills 14 scalars (the 3D convention carried forward) and bytes_combinatorial bills 9 (Atom2d::bits()), BOTH at SCALAR_BYTES = 8.0 while a coefficient value is billed at VALUE_BYTES = 4.0. Neither byte family is quantised, so every byte figure is an upper bound on a real encoding — the module's own prose rules that "Term count against atom count is the only axis a conclusion may rest on."

reproduce it
cargo run --release -p lights --features fit --bin lights-p4 -- --out-dir <DIR> --classes all --terms shared --max-atoms 8

VERIFIED 2026-09-14 that this invocation form is accepted by this tree: run from the repo root into a `mktemp -d` with `--classes 1 --terms shared --max-atoms 1`, and again with `--terms 64`; both built, parsed every flag, wrote the strip PNGs and exited 0. `--release` is MY choice and not a reconstruction: no cargo profile is recorded anywhere for the original runs.

PARTIALLY REPRODUCIBLE — the strips are, the numbers are not.

(1) The only committed producer is the strip binary above, re-run with `--terms 64` and `--terms 512`, and again with `--max-atoms 64`. That flag set is INFERRED from the three run logs, which record `rendered at 8/64/512 term(s)` and shared ceilings 8 vs 11/15/13/10/61/50/64/31 — no log records its own command line. It writes PNGs and a stdout manifest only: `grep -rn "hardware|banner" src/bin/lights_p4.rs` finds a caption banner and no hardware line, and lights-p4 emits no TSV (BURNDOWN.toml:448).

(2) The N-term table that every quoted rel-L2 and byte figure comes from was produced by an UNCOMMITTED harness — `aspect2_report_harness.rs`, a `#[test] fn nterm_table` reading `LIGHTS_MAX_ATOMS` (default 8) — whose recipe was: copy it to `crates/lights/tests/` and run
  LIGHTS_MAX_ATOMS=64 cargo test -p lights --features fit --test aspect2_report_harness -- --nocapture nterm_table
NOT VERIFIED, AND NOT VERIFIABLE TODAY: `git show --stat 251381a7` lists no such file, and the per-session scratchpad it lived in is gone — I looked on 2026-09-14 and every `<session scratchpad>/*/scratchpad/aspect2_report_harness.rs` path is absent. There is nothing left to copy, so that line is a recipe for a file that would have to be rewritten, not a command anyone can paste.

(3) The verify pass came from a second uncommitted harness, `audit_probe_harness.rs` (tests `audit_a`…`audit_f`, `audit_q_exactness_rung_and_overshoot`, `audit_q_how_exact_is_the_exact_rung`), same copy-and-run route, its output `audit_probes.log`. ALSO NOT VERIFIED and also gone from the scratchpad, checked the same way on the same day.

(4) The signed-atom numbers came from that same harness's `matched_search` test and CANNOT be reproduced from the crate at all — see `not_settled`.

RE-RUN AND PARTLY AUDITED 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-134). Aspect 2 now has artefacts on disk for the first time, under docs/lights/p4/, in the two-run shape docs/lights/p2/ already uses:

    cargo run --release -p lights --features fit --bin lights-p4 -- \
      --out-dir <REPO>/docs/lights/p4/run-8  --classes all --terms shared --max-atoms 8
    cargo run --release -p lights --features fit --bin lights-p4 -- \
      --out-dir <REPO>/docs/lights/p4/run-64 --classes all --terms shared --max-atoms 64

THE COMMAND ABOVE THIS NOTE IS THE 8-ATOM FORM AND IT REPRODUCES NONE OF THE PUBLISHED CEILINGS: at a budget of 8 every class is capped at 8. The 64-atom run is the one that measures reach, and its eight shared ceilings reproduce EXACTLY the list this record's own command note carries -- t2 11, t3 15, t5 13, t6 10, n1 61, n2 50, n3 64, n4 31 against '8 vs 11/15/13/10/61/50/64/31'. That is the FIRST aspect-2 figure ever reproduced from a committed binary, and it independently confirms number 9's reach figures, because at a 64-atom budget the shared ceiling IS the exhaustion point.

CORRECTED HERE: this record said 'lights-p4 emits no TSV (BURNDOWN.toml:448)'. It does, and has since the binary was written -- write_report at src/bin/lights_p4.rs:246-266 emits report.tsv through invocation::table, so the table opens with the command that wrote it. What lights-p4 does NOT emit is any error or byte column: report.tsv carries content, viewport, control, shared_ceiling, requested, zoom, columns and png, and nothing else.

SO THE LIMIT, PLAINLY: NO rel-L2 figure and NO byte figure below is auditable from anything in this repository. Numbers 1, 2, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 10, 11, 12, 13, 14 and 16 came from the uncommitted N-term and probe harnesses and they stay unauditable. What the committed tables DO pin down is number 9 (reach, via the ceilings), number 15's control claim (the analytic column is ABSENT on exactly n1..n4 and analytic-exact on exactly the four T classes), and the viewport column that number 14's level-count finding rests on (t6-glyph-like is the only 32x32 class; every other class is 64x64). Locked by crates/lights/tests/p4_artefacts.rs, seven tests, seen RED with docs/lights/p4 moved aside.
