what it settled
  • THE THREE HONEST AXES POINT DIFFERENT WAYS, so on content analytic cannot express there is no clear winner: on TERMS the atom wins all eight new-class cells needing 2.0x to 8.0x fewer at equal error (audit_byte_tables.txt Table A), on BYTES under the run's own convention the transform wins all eight by 2.43x to 9.74x, and on REACH the transform wins outright because the greedy fitter exhausted at 61 and 50 atoms and cannot go below rel L2 0.007717 and 0.020541 while CDF 9/7 continues to 0.000506 at 1024 coefficients — which is a fitter result, not a representation result.
  • THE SIGNED ATOM EARNS NOTHING, and the way it fails is the informative part: under a matched search whose shape set includes frequency zero — making gabor-colour a strict superset of the gaussian — the optimiser drove the carrier frequency to zero on all four new classes (9 of 16 atoms at exactly 0.000, none above 0.011), and at f=0 the crate's own test a_zero_frequency_gabor_is_the_gaussian_bit_for_bit (tests/gabor_kernel.rs:186) asserts the Gabor IS the gaussian bit for bit, so the 0.47–1.23% edge is the superset's extra polish freedom rather than oscillation.
  • WHERE A REAL CARRIER SURVIVES THE GABOR IS WORSE, WHICH A SUPERSET CANNOT BE: at pixel scale on t5-thin-stroke the Gabor lands at rel L2 0.475797 against the gaussian's 0.222139 (carriers 0.017 to 0.219), and t6-glyph-like goes the same way (0.595467 vs 0.566461) — so the one place the signed atom might have earned something is exactly the place where the search is not good enough to tell, which is a caveat rather than an acquittal.
  • GAUSS-HERMITE LOSES ON ALL EIGHT CLASSES IN BOTH SIGN PLACEMENTS — 16 of 16 cells in matched2.txt — and order 2 was selected in only 4 of 64 hermite atom slots, so the extra polynomial factor is bought and almost never used.
  • SIGN PLACEMENT IS RULED COLOUR, winning 4-0 on the four new classes and 5-3 over all eight, and it is the placement that keeps everything else: alpha stays non-negative so Over stays valid, the set stays depth-sortable, the early-out survives, the crate::bound tolerance argument survives and PixelsAndTolerance still accepts the arm, whereas density forfeits all of that and — because Additive::resolve ADDS the background — forces a black-background contract that would put a constant offset into that arm's error (src/kernel/gabor_rs_comment/sign_placement.md). It is implemented as one associated const plus a defaulted trait member, so for the six existing kernels the branch vanishes at monomorphisation.
  • HAAR REPRESENTS A STEP EXACTLY AND A SMOOTH BASIS CANNOT: Haar leads CDF 9/7 on the 30° edge at N=8, N=16 and N=512, and reaches the edge at 1024 coefficients (rel L2 0.000000, max-abs 0.000000 as printed; 2.295e-16 and 4.441e-16 measured) where CDF 9/7 is still at 0.011245 — a smooth basis has no finite exact representation of a step at any angle, a piecewise-constant one does.
  • CDF 9/7 RINGS AND OVERSHOOTS, AND ONLY THE SECOND METRIC SEES IT: its peak error reaches 1.5377 at N=16 on t2 against a truth whose range is at most 1.0, while Haar's worst peak on the same class is 1.0407 and its reconstruction never exceeds the step by more than that — relative L2 alone would have hidden the overshoot completely, which is why the criterion carries two metrics that are never combined.
  • AUDIT 1 — THE BYTE AXIS RESTS ON AN UNPINNED ASYMMETRIC PRECISION CONVENTION: a coefficient value is billed at VALUE_BYTES = 4.0 against an atom parameter at SCALAR_BYTES = 8.0 (src/fit/nterm.rs:23,25) with nothing fixing either — grep -rn &#x27;SCALAR_BYTES|VALUE_BYTES&#x27; crates/lights/tests/ returns nothing, the only atom-byte assertion (tests/nterm.rs:341-346) compares the 14-scalar column against the 9-scalar column and both scale identically with SCALAR_BYTES, and scripts/mutations.json names neither — so the verify pass's report that mutating SCALAR_BYTES to 4.0 passes the entire suite is exactly what the code allows.
  • AUDIT 2 — UNDER A SYMMETRIC CONVENTION THE HEADLINE CHANGES SIDES: bill both at f32 and the atom at its native nine scalars, which is what Atom2d::bits() actually returns, and 'transforms win on all four by 3.1x to 9.7x' becomes 3.60x, 2.44x and 1.83x with n2-soft-shadow flipping to the atoms in both its cells, while on the T-classes the atoms then sweep all eight cells; furthermore two of the three T-class cells the report awarded to the transform already flip inside the report's own bracket table depending on which pairing is used (t3-gaussian 3248 B vs 2944 B goes to the transform under explicit-atom-vs-explicit-coefficient but 2088 B vs 2944 B goes to the atoms under nine-scalar-atom-vs-explicit-coefficient, and t5-gaussian does the same), and only one pairing was reported.
  • AUDIT 3 — HALF THE NEW CONTENT HANDS THE TRANSFORM AN UNDECLARED STRUCTURAL ADVANTAGE: n3 and n4 are generated on value-noise lattices with periods 16 and 8 (N3_LATTICES) and 16, 8 and 4 (N4_LATTICES) anchored at pixel 0 (src/fit/content.rs:42,49), so cell boundaries at columns 0, 16, 32, 48 land exactly on the level-4, level-3 and level-2 dyadic block boundaries, and under a controlled shift test CDF 9/7 is 1.69x better in relative L2 aligned than misaligned at N=256 with the two groups not overlapping, while the atom arm is indifferent to the shift.
  • AUDIT 4 — EVERY TRANSFORM NUMBER RIDES ON ONE CONSTANT WHOSE ROLE NO REPORTED NUMBER STATES: MIN_COARSE_SIDE = 4 (src/fit/transform/mod.rs:13) is what gives four decomposition levels at 64x64, and n1's CDF 9/7 error at N=64 is 0.0303 at four levels, 0.6390 at three and 0.9720 at one — a 21x swing from four to three levels and 32x from four to one — while the same constant silently gives t6-glyph-like only three levels because it is 32x32.
  • WHAT SURVIVES UNCONDITIONALLY: both controls hold on independent re-measurement — a zero-frequency Gabor is bit-identical to the gaussian through the full render path on all eight classes and the check can fail (one perturbed coefficient gives max-abs 1.462419, and a f=0.05 carrier moves the image by 0.1030–1.9767), and both transforms round-trip to a worst max-abs of 3.553e-15 over sixteen cells; Haar preserves energy to 2.133e-14 relative so its exact-N-term-optimum label is earned, and CDF 9/7's coefficient energy does not, so the report was right to refuse it that label and to file it as basis-ranked instead.
  • THE WAVELET WAS DELIBERATELY KEPT OUT OF THE KERNEL TRAIT AND THE BAN IS MECHANICAL: src/fit/transform/mod_rs_comment/not_a_kernel.md rules that a wavelet's positions are not free, that it is filtered rather than evaluated, and that its basis functions are not independent, and tests/layering.rs::the_transform_arm_is_not_a_kernel scans the directory to enforce it — because bending a wavelet into Kernel would have given it free placement and free anisotropy, the two properties the atom side is on trial for, biasing the comparison toward the atoms.
  • THE BYTE AXIS UNDER ONE CURRENCY (2026-09-28, L-108): with an atom billed at nine f32 scalars (36 B) and a coefficient at an f32 value plus its index, a transform byte-win survives on exactly the three classes with no edge - n1, n3, n4 - and only for CDF 9/7 (1.04x to 3.13x on the transform's pessimistic bracket), while the atoms win n2 and all four T classes past their first two rungs. The earlier 'transform wins all eight by 2.43x to 9.74x' was the 4-vs-8-byte convention; the uncommitted 3.6x/2.4x/1.8x re-tabulation is NOT reproduced as a statistic (it was a different, unrecorded one), though its ordering n1 > n3 > n4 and its n2 flip to the atoms both reappear.
what it refuted
  • 'Haar should be poor on a 30-degree edge' — FALSE, and the failure is the finding: Haar leads t2 at N=8, N=16 and N=512 and reaches the edge exactly at 1024 coefficients where CDF 9/7 is still at 0.011245.
  • The signed atom the project had speculated about since its first session: built, measured, and it earns nothing on this content — the carrier is driven to zero wherever it would help, and the only cells that keep a carrier are the ones where it loses to its own superset.
  • THE RUN REFUTED ITS OWN EARLIER GABOR MEASUREMENT. An earlier search whose shape set did NOT contain frequency zero put gabor-colour at rel L2 0.597523 on t2 against the gaussian's 0.110055, 0.801109 on n1 against 0.170721, and 0.792420 on n4 against 0.178932 (.../scratchpad/matched.txt, 23:29). Adding CurveShape::gabor(0.0,0.0,0.0) to the shape set — making the search MATCHED — collapsed those to 0.101699, 0.169920 and 0.176847 (matched2.txt, 23:36). The dramatic 'Gabor is far worse than the gaussian' result was an artefact of an unmatched instrument, not a property of the atom.
  • 'Transforms win on all four new classes by 3.1x to 9.7x' — withdrawn by the run's own verify pass: under a symmetric f32 / nine-scalar convention it becomes 3.60x, 2.44x, 1.83x with n2 flipping to the atoms, and the T-class sweep reverses to the atoms in all eight cells.
  • Density sign placement, rejected: it forfeits Over's validity, depth sortability, the early-out and the bound tolerance argument, and forces a black-background contract, and it also loses 5-3 on the measurement.
  • CDF 9/7's claim to the exact-N-term-optimum label, refused on measurement: it is biorthogonal, Parseval fails, and its arm is labelled basis-ranked with the legend stating the N kept coefficients are NOT proven optimal because the synthesis functions are not mutually orthogonal.
  • The stage specification's byte rule, refuted before it was used: it billed the explicit index at ceil(log2(W*H)) = 12 bits at 64x64 while the combinatorial term named one of 3*W*H positions, which at N=1 puts the LOWER bracket (5.6981 B) ABOVE the upper one (5.50 B). Corrected to ceil(log2(3*W*H)) = 14 bits, which makes the bracket provable (C(M,N) <= M^N) and is asserted at every rung (src/fit/nterm_rs_comment/bytes_are_not_symmetric.md; audit_probes.log F block).
  • The specification's request for a closed-form lgamma binomial, refused in favour of a summed log2_choose checked against u128 integer binomials — no special function, no dependency, no approximation error to defend.
  • REFUTED LATER, BY THE PROJECT'S OWN BURN-DOWN AUDIT: the headline pair '0.4758 against 0.2221' is not reproducible from the crate. KernelFamily has four variants and dispatch_kernel matches exactly those four; KernelFamily::shape() never returns CurveShape::gabor; FitRequest carries no shape, frequency, orientation or phase field so greedy::fit cannot search a carrier; and REACHABLE_ARMS is four entries, which is what lights-p4 runs. The register's ruling is explicit: 'Do NOT re-quote 0.4758/0.2221 until' either the fitter limit is accepted in writing or KernelFamily is widened (crates/lights/BURNDOWN.toml:1694-1704).
  • 'Under the symmetric convention no transform byte-win survives' and its opposite 'transforms win on bytes everywhere' - both FALSE as of 2026-09-28: CDF 9/7 wins n1, n3, n4 with the win bracketed, and loses n2 and every T class (docs/lights/p4/nterm-64/crossings.tsv).
what it does NOT settle
  • THE ATOM ARM IS A GREEDY LOWER BOUND, NOT THE ATOM CEILING, and the shortfall was measured at the exact term counts the claims rest on: hand-built atoms polished on n1 reach 0.0561 where the greedy fitter reaches 0.1228 (the fitter leaves 4.794x the residual energy) and on n2 0.0870 against 0.1004 (1.332x); polishing the greedy 8-atom set on t2 moves it from 0.0598 to 0.0327 (3.349x residual energy) and moves the CDF 9/7 coefficients needed to match it from 384 to 768; t5 goes 0.1958 -> 0.1357 and 256 -> 384 (audit_probes.log, B block). Every 'the transform reaches this error in fewer bytes' statement therefore compares a transform optimum against an atom lower bound.
  • THE COMPARISON IS STRUCTURALLY ASYMMETRIC AND THE RUN SAYS SO ITSELF, verbatim, in its own banner: 'The two transform columns have NO fitter: keeping the N largest coefficients under the subband-weighted ranking is their optimum in that basis, obtained exactly and for free… A strip in which a transform column looks better is therefore ambiguous between the transform represents this content better and the transform got its optimum and the atoms did not, and no caption here resolves that.' The transform arm is additionally handed the TRUTH IMAGE itself as input (src/fit/aspect2.rs:249, truncate_and_reconstruct(*transform, truth, ...)), where the atom arm must fit.
  • NEITHER BYTE FAMILY IS QUANTISED. Coefficients are kept at full f64 and atom parameters are raw floats, and no measurement of either family's tolerance to parameter quantisation exists in the crate. The module's own prose states the consequence: every figure is an upper bound on a real encoding, none of them is a compression result, and 'Term count against atom count is the only axis a conclusion may rest on.' A real coder (EZW, SPIHT, EBCOT) can beat the combinatorial lower bracket by exploiting inter-scale zerotree structure that pure counting cannot see.
  • THE DYADIC-ALIGNMENT FACTOR IS N-DEPENDENT AND ONLY ITS BEST VALUE WAS REPORTED: 1.28x at N=128, 1.69x at N=256, 1.43x at N=512. The claim that 'the atom arm is indifferent' understates the direction by one step — the atom arm at n=32 was slightly BETTER off-lattice (0.0621 misaligned against 0.0716 aligned).
  • THE MIN_COARSE_SIDE SWING HAS TWO SIZES: 21x is the four-levels-to-three-levels step (0.0303 -> 0.6390) and 32x is the full four-to-one span (0.0303 -> 0.9720). The level count is also a function of viewport, not just of the constant — 48x48 and 40x40 both get three levels — so the same content at a different raster size gets a different transform.
  • 'HAAR REACHES THE EDGE EXACTLY' IS EXACT TO PRINTED PRECISION ONLY. The verify pass measured N=1024 at rel L2 2.295e-16 and max-abs 4.441e-16 with bit-identical = FALSE, on t2, t3, t5 and t6 alike (audit_probes.log, Q3). The published 0.0000 is a rounding of a floating-point residue, not a bit-for-bit identity.
  • THE SIGNED-ATOM NUMBERS ARE NOT REPRODUCIBLE FROM THE CRATE AT ALL. No committed code path can select a Gabor or a Gauss-Hermite: KernelFamily has four variants (src/kernel/mod.rs:118-123), dispatch_kernel matches exactly those four (src/dispatch.rs:82-89), FitRequest carries no shape/frequency/orientation/phase field (src/fit/greedy.rs:68-78), and REACHABLE_ARMS is four entries (src/fit/nterm.rs:181-186). The shipped binary states the limit verbatim in its own banner ('TWO OF THE SIX ARMS ARE MISSING AND THAT IS A FITTER LIMIT, NOT A RESULT'), but the corresponding note was never attached to the RESULT. The gap is not a one-line fix: KernelFamily derives Eq, so a variant cannot carry an f64 frequency (BURNDOWN.toml:1694-1705). CORRECTED 2026-09-28 (BURNDOWN L-115): the reachability half of this paragraph stopped being true on 2026-09-15, when L-56/L-58 gave KernelFamily the variants GaborColour(Carrier), GaborDensity(Carrier), GaussHermiteColour(Lobe) and GaussHermiteDensity(Lobe), with Carrier and Lobe as fieldless enums. That is how the Eq snag was solved (src/kernel/mod.rs:199-208), and dispatch_kernel now matches all eight families (src/dispatch.rs:111-117). The greedy fitter ACCEPTS every one of the ten modulated kernels, and each fit renders differently from the same atoms drawn as gaussians: crates/lights/tests/p4_signed_arms.rs::every_modulated_kernel_is_fitted_at_a_carrier_that_is_not_the_gaussian, run with cargo test -p lights --all-features --test p4_signed_arms, passed 3 of 3. WHAT STAYS TRUE, AND IS WHY THE PAIR IS STILL UNREPRODUCIBLE: each carrier is FIXED (all three at QUARTER_CYCLE_FREQUENCY; they differ only in orientation or phase), FitRequest has no carrier knob, and REACHABLE_ARMS is still the same four. The matched search that produced 0.4758 / 0.2221 moved a continuous CurveShape that included frequency zero, and no committed path can do that. The knob is L-135's to build (PM ruling 2026-09-16, option B). Do not quote the pair as a crate result.
  • TWO REPORTED FIGURES CANNOT BE RECONSTRUCTED FROM THE ARTEFACTS. (a) The headline byte range 'transforms win on all four by 3.1x to 9.7x': the audit's own table under the report convention spans 2.43x to 9.74x, so the 9.7x end matches (n1, b4 arm) and the 3.1x end does not — the smallest is 2.43x on n2. (b) The published page's 'on terms the atom wins all four classes, needing 3x–32x fewer at equal error': the audit's Table A pairings give 2.0x to 8.0x on the four new classes. Neither can be settled without re-running; the settling command is the uncommitted LIGHTS_MAX_ATOMS=64 … --test aspect2_report_harness -- --nocapture nterm_table plus the byte-table derivation in audit_byte_tables.txt.
  • THE 262x CDF 9/7 COEFFICIENT-ENERGY FIGURE IS IN THE COMMIT MESSAGE ONLY. The test that prints it (tests/transform_cdf97.rs:269) asserts only worst &gt; 0.1 and prints the value; no clean run of it survives on disk (the one capture in scratchpad/suite1.txt is a MUTATED run in which the orthonormal control itself failed at 0.7834). Settling command: cargo test -p lights --features fit --test transform_cdf97 the_biorthogonal_transform_does_not_preserve_energy_and_the_deviation_is_recorded -- --nocapture.
  • THE GABOR CARRIER RANGE AS PUBLISHED ('0.000 to 0.006') IS NARROWER THAN THE ARTEFACT. matched2.txt shows per-atom gabor-colour carriers on n1–n4 spanning 0.000 to 0.011 (n3 max 0.010, n4 max 0.011), with 9 of 16 exactly zero. Likewise 'order 2 was selected in 4 of 32 atom slots' is 4 of 64 in the artefact.
  • ASPECT 2 HAS NO RESULTS DOCUMENT. ls docs/lights/ returns BURNDOWN_LOOP.md, CORPUS_READING_MAP.md, P2_RESULTS.md, P5_RESULTS.md, TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md and p5/ — nothing for aspect 2. Everything it measured lives in the body of commit 251381a7 and in one published HTML page. A commit message is invisible to ls docs/lights/, is not linked from the plan, and carries the byte-axis correction that the re-tabulation backlog item exists to protect (BURNDOWN.toml:721-731).
  • NO ARTEFACT OF THE RUN IS IN THE REPOSITORY. lights-p4 writes PNGs and stdout only, no TSV, no hardware banner and no provenance stamp — so the run does not record the machine it was measured on, in a crate whose own convention is that every published number names its hardware (BURNDOWN.toml:448, 751-760). Every surviving number lives in a session scratchpad that a rm -rf /private/tmp would end.
  • SIX OF THE TWELVE ARM-CLASS COMBINATIONS WERE NEVER DRAWN and four of the eight classes have NO analytic control at all: on n1–n4 the control column is an ABSENT magenta hatch, refused rather than replaced by an invented weak arm, so those four classes carry no ceiling anything is measured against.
  • THE WAVELET ARM HAS NO ABSENT PATH: transform::validate hard-errors on any viewport it cannot dyadically decompose to MIN_COARSE_SIDE, so a content class at a non-power-of-two raster cannot enter this comparison at all (recorded as an open defect in BURNDOWN.toml).
  • THE PREFIX IS NOT A REFIT. Every atom-count curve in this run is a prefix slice of ONE greedy fit, not a fresh fit per rung; the counterexample showing that a prefix can RISE is measured only at max_atoms = 2 with one refinement pass and a tail of one, not at the six passes and eight-to-sixty-four atoms every published p4 table used (src/fit/nterm_rs_comment/the_prefix_is_not_a_refit.md; BURNDOWN.toml:716).
  • THE 2026-09-28 BYTE WINS ARE AGAINST A GREEDY ATOM LOWER BOUND. A transform-cheaper verdict is robust to the ladder (it uses the transform's pessimistic bracket) but NOT to a better fitter: the greedy fitter exhausted at 61, 50, 48 and 31 atoms on n1, n2, n4 (nterm.tsv), and polished atoms were measured earlier to leave up to 4.794x less residual. An atoms-cheaper verdict is the robust direction. Also unsettled: the low bracket assumes error does not rise with more terms, proven for Haar (orthonormal) but not for CDF 9/7 (biorthogonal); the analytic column is not in this table, and L-111's second half (one analytic byte formula instead of two) is still open.

Aspect 3 — does a description fitted at one sampling rate survive a zoom, and by how much does it lose?

representation2026-09-09copy-pasteable command8 refuted
the answer

Resolution independence is real for atoms on smooth and near-smooth content, marginal on a crisp edge, and it fails on text — one frozen 8-atom description of a 30-degree edge meets its scene-unit criterion at every rung of both ladders (0.25x to 8x) and beats a fresh fit at the small rungs, while on the 12 px glyph frozen atoms decay 0.22339 → 0.45811 rel L2 (decay 2.051) against an analytic bar at 0.01352 (0.09216 in the 2026-09-09 artefact, corrected by L-144) — and the machine-readable table said the opposite about the glyph until the emitter defect was fixed in the same commit.

the question it was built to answer

Hold a fitted description fixed, vary only the sampling rate, and measure how far quality decays away from the fitting scale — i.e. is fit-once-render-anywhere true, and what does freezing cost against a fresh fit at every rung, an analytic SDF, and two lattice-bound wavelet transforms?

criterion, and its convention

PRIMARY: SCENE UNITS. SECONDARY: DEVICE PIXELS. Both printed on every row (P5_RESULTS.md:14). Thresholds are stamped in both TSV headers: #criterion.max_transition 1, #criterion.target_fwhm 1, #criterion.max_fwhm_error 0.5. An edge/corner class is judged on the 10–90 transition width (meet = width < 1 scene unit); a glyph class is judged on FWHM (meet = |fwhm − 1| <= 0.5) and carries transition_10_90 = nan. The ruling is stated as load-bearing rather than cosmetic: the description is held fixed and only the sampling rate varies, so a scene criterion asks one question at every rung and a difference along the ladder is attributable to the description, whereas a pixel criterion re-asks a four-times-stricter question at 4x and "a decay curve measured that way cannot be separated from the goalposts moving" (P5_RESULTS.md:17-20). The analytic bar proves the point: its 10–90 transition is FLAT at 0.80–0.96 device px across a 32x span of scale and runs 0.12 → 3.38 in scene units — "same measurement, two conventions, opposite stories" (P5_RESULTS.md:23-27). Byte convention is explicitly provisional: #byte_convention symmetric-f32-provisional-see-C2. Fitter search convention: #fitter_search_convention scene-invariant.

reproduce it
cargo run --release -p lights --features fit --bin lights-p5 -- --out-dir <DIR> --classes t2,t3,n1,t6 --direction both --profile both

The mechanism of the error is worth keeping: the lower-bound bug's own error was a fixed-width artefact along the seam where two primitives meet, i.e. itself boundary-localised, so on the multi-primitive classes it MANUFACTURED the behaviour it was then cited as evidence for. The finding survives on t2 (single primitive, untouchable by the bug, 0.964–1.022 over eleven rungs) and on t3; it is refuted on t6. The overall median moves only 1.002 → 1.0035 magnifying and 1.000 → 1.0032 minifying, because t2 and t3 outvote t6 — so the median must not be quoted alone: the distribution is bimodal. Read at tests/aspect3.rs:1276-1330. It is in-process (no subprocess, no temp dir), so it is NOT one of the three race-prone tests named in L-16.
artefact inventory and run cost50 files in docs/lights/p5 — 2 TSVs (ladder-magnify.tsv 113 data rows, ladder-minify.tsv 169 data rows) and 48 PNG strips, ~18 MB, all committed in 70cb842a. Run cost measured separately: 21m32s wall for 6m59s of CPU on an 18-core machine (6 P + 12 E).ls -la on the directory and row counts by grep -vc &#x27;^#&#x27;. The timing is BURNDOWN.toml L-95 (open), which measured this exact invocation (PID 48395) and notes lights_p5.rs and aspect3.rs contain no concurrency at all.
what it settled
  • Fit-once-render-anywhere holds for a straight edge under contract(): one frozen 8-atom description of a 30-degree edge meets the scene-unit sharpness criterion at every rung of both ladders, 0.25x to 8x, a 32x span, with its 10–90 transition never leaving 0.383–0.947 scene units against a threshold of 1.
  • The refitted control is not a ceiling: the frozen set beat a fresh fit in 13 of 24 non-base magnification cells (13 of 32 counting base ties), and on the corner it beat it at every non-base rung under both profiles, 6 of 6 — so where the frozen set wins, the frozen-to-refitted gap is a MINIMUM statement about the cost of freezing and possibly zero, and greedy matching pursuit is a FLOOR rather than an optimum.
  • Recomputing the same comparison on the minification ladder, which the results document does not do, gives 17 of 40 non-base cells where frozen beats refitted — the ceiling breach is at least as widespread going down as going up.
  • The glyph is the product question and frozen atoms lose it: a frozen 12 px fit carries no detail to fill an 8x zoom, decaying 0.22339 → 0.45811 rel L2 (decay 2.051) while the analytic bar ends at 0.01352, a same-rung factor of 33.9. (The analytic figure read 0.09216 and the factor 4.97 in the 2026-09-09 artefact; corrected by L-144 on 2026-09-15, in the analytic arm's favour.)
  • The trap on the glyph is that the registered FWHM criterion reads MET in scene units at every rung (1.168, 0.657, 0.742, 1.147 against target 1 tolerance 0.5) while whole-field rel L2 doubles — the probe is local and the error is not, so criterion and rel L2 must be read together.
  • The corner is analytic-only at every scale: the analytic SDF meets at every magnification rung (0.800 → 0.120 scene) while every atom and wavelet arm fails at every rung under both profiles (0 of 24 non-analytic magnify rows meet).
  • The analytic bar is not automatically minification-safe: on the edge it meets down to 1x (transition 0.800) and then fails, reading 1.600 at 0.5x and 3.378 at 0.25x.
  • The decay column has a denominator, and this run found it: relative L2 divides by the norm of the truth at that rung, so an error confined to a boundary of fixed device-pixel width reads sqrt(base_side/side) — 0.354 at 8x, 5.657 at 0.25x — purely because it covers a shrinking share of a growing image.
  • Measured over three classes and both ladders the analytic arm's decay sits on that boundary reference with a median ratio of 1.002 magnifying and 1.000 minifying, and its max|e| never moves (0.0324–0.0383 on t2), so the one arm that is resolution-independent by construction posts the worst decay in the table in both directions and none of it is the description.
  • The run reports the denominator rather than correcting it: a bdry ref column sits immediately beside decay, and a third self-audit, decay_that_is_only_the_metric, names the rows where the decay is the metric — deliberately NOT correcting, because a single corrected column would be a third wrong number that made a perfectly flat colour-field result look like it decayed 2.8x.
  • Where the boundary reference stops predicting is the real signal: on the glyph minified below its own size the analytic arm departs from it, ratio 1.26 at a 16x16 render and 1.74 at 8x8, and max|e| finally falls from 0.4987 to 0.3253 as the glyph vanishes — a genuine representational failure visible only because the geometric part was accounted for first.
  • The prefilter trade is two-sided and both sides are reported: switching from contract() (screen_dilation = 0, no pixel prefilter) to prefiltered() lowers t2 frozen rel L2 at every minification rung and holds it flat into the small scales (0.0676→0.0564 against 0.0960→0.1132), while widening the 10–90 transition from 0.383–0.573 to 0.629–2.944 scene units so the edge stops meeting a sub-unit sharpness criterion from 1x down.
  • The colour field is the strongest pro-atom result and the document refuses to oversell it: a frozen set decays 1.0000 → 1.0019 over an 8x zoom, but CDF 9/7 holds equally flat and beats the atoms absolutely (0.03026 against 0.03793), because band-limited content has no detail above the base lattice for a lattice-bound arm to lose.
  • The search convention is a larger effect than the thing under test: refitting t2 at 8x gives 0.09603 with a scene-invariant search against 0.18628 with a pixel search, a difference of 0.09025, which is more than the entire frozen-to-refitted gap of 0.01617 being measured at that cell.
  • No setting can equalise the seed lattice — a refit at 8x chooses among 64x more distinct seed positions — so the refitted ceiling is genuinely stronger at large scales for a reason about the SEARCH, not about resolution independence, and the run reports that residual rather than arguing it away.
  • The wavelet arms post the flattest decays in the table (0.98–1.07) while carrying rel L2 0.19 against the atoms' 0.06 on t2 and failing the criterion at every single rung: a flat decay from a bad base is not resolution independence, and the base row's absolute rel L2 must be read before the decay column means anything.
  • THE INSTRUMENT LIED, and the fix is the other half of the same commit: row_cells emitted a single meets_scene/meets_device_px pair carrying the TRANSITION verdict gated only on whether quality was measured, so a glyph row — which carries transition_10_90 = nan, and nan &lt; criterion.max_transition is false in Rust — read no where the honest answer is that the target carries no transition probe, while judge_fwhm's verdict (src/fit/scale.rs:459-461) was never written to the file at all.
  • The printed table was right the whole time — met() at src/bin/lights_p5.rs:428 returns - when the value is not finite and prints all four verdicts — so the machine-readable artefact and the human-readable run disagreed, and the one a report generator consumes was the wrong one.
  • Read straight, the broken table said the analytic SDF never meets the glyph at any rung, contradicting P2 on the same published page; computed from the right probe it meets at every rung, FWHM 0.937, 0.947, 0.933, 0.950 against a target of 1 with a tolerance of 0.5.
  • The fix is verified rather than asserted: SCALE_COLUMNS went 27 → 29, the regression case the_tsv_names_which_probe_each_verdict_belongs_to_and_says_nothing_where_a_probe_is_absent (tests/aspect3.rs:1276) was RED on the unfixed tree and refuses to pass unless it saw both a probed and an unprobed target, and a mutation making the verdict ignore probe existence turns it red on the fixed tree (scripts/mutations.json 235 → 236 rows).
what it refuted
  • REFUTED BY ITS OWN AUTHORS, in the same commit that produced the table: the machine-readable ladder TSVs said the analytic SDF meets the glyph criterion at NO rung. It meets at every rung. Any copy of ladder-*.tsv older than the correction note of 2026-09-09 is wrong in one column (P5_RESULTS.md:229-231).
  • REFUTATION WITHDRAWN 2026-09-15 (L-144), and the original reading reinstated. This record USED to say: "REFUTED, the spec's own reconnaissance reading: 'the analytic bar IMPROVES 1.00 → 0.36' on the glyph is the metric, not a representational gain — 0.36 is 1/sqrt(8) = 0.354, the boundary reference." The arithmetic was right and the number was stale: 0.36 was the broken column's decay, and the arm's own decay on the glyph is 0.156, which is 2.3x past the boundary reference. The improvement IS a representational gain. The other half of the claim survives and grows: analytic ending better than frozen atoms in absolute error at the same rung is 34x (0.01352 against 0.45811), not ~5x. WORTH NAMING AS A FAILURE MODE: a check that is sound in form can still be pointed at the wrong number, and nothing in the record's shape catches that.
  • REFUTED, the reading that ranks arms by the decay column: it ranks the one provably scale-free arm (analytic SDF, max|e| flat to within a few percent along the whole ladder on the single- and two-primitive classes t2 and t3 — on t6 it is not flat, which L-144 established on 2026-09-15 and which makes the glyph's decay a real signal rather than a metric artefact; the point about decay carrying two facts is unaffected) as the LEAST resolution-independent. decay is one name carrying two facts — 'the description lost quality' and 'the metric's denominator moved' (aspect3_rs_comment/the_decay_column_has_a_denominator.md).
  • AMENDED BY ITS OWN AUTHORS after the verdict columns were fixed: the C3 claim "the apparent minification failure of atoms is the missing prefilter, not the representation" is true of ERROR and FALSE of SHARPNESS. The run had no way to say so before the fix. Prefiltered() lowers rel L2 at every rung and holds it flat into the small scales — that half stands — while the same prefilter pushes the 10–90 transition from 0.38–0.57 to 0.63–2.94, so the edge stops meeting the criterion from 1x down.
  • REFUTED, the assumption that the refitted arm is the atom-quality ceiling: it is one greedy trajectory that landed worse than the one carried up from the base scale in 13 of 24 non-base magnification cells, worst t3 prefiltered at 4x (frozen 0.196130 against refit 0.292385, +49.1%).
  • REFUTED, the reading of the corrected column that the fix invites: with the right probe Haar MEETS the glyph criterion while sitting at rel L2 0.676, beside the analytic arm at 0.0135 (0.092 before the L-144 correction). FWHM within ±0.5 of a one-unit stem is a loose gate — a stroke can be the right width and the wrong picture — so every glyph conclusion in the document is drawn from rel L2 ratios and never from that column.
  • REFUTED, that the colour-field result is a win for atoms: it proves atoms do not degrade (1.0000 → 1.0019 over 8x), and CDF 9/7 holds equally flat while beating them absolutely at every rung. Resolution independence only DISCRIMINATES on content with structure above the base lattice.
  • REFUTED, that the analytic bar is minification-safe by construction: it fails the edge criterion from the 0.5x rung down (1.600 and 3.378 scene against a threshold of 1).
what it does NOT settle
  • C2, the byte comparison, REMAINS OPEN. Every arm is billed in one symmetric f32 currency so the columns are at least in the same money, and the header is stamped symmetric-f32-provisional-see-C2. **No byte ranking is claimed from this run.** (P5_RESULTS.md:214-217; the arm-asymmetry comment says the same: "This run bills every arm in one symmetric f32 currency so the columns are at least comparable, and still does not claim the byte ranking.")
  • Absolute error is NOT comparable to aspect 2. The truth here is the pixel-box INTEGRAL of one fixed continuous field; aspect 2 used the POINT SAMPLE. Only ratios along a ladder carry across. (P5_RESULTS.md:218-220)
  • The base rung privileges three of four arms. At base the frozen arm IS the refitted arm and the wavelet lattice IS the truth lattice. Mitigated by running both ladders from both ends and scoring each arm against its own base — mitigated, not removed. (P5_RESULTS.md:221-223)
  • The four tables on the published page are NOT in one currency and must not be cross-quoted: P2 ran --max-atoms 48 against a pass criterion, aspect 3 ran 8 against probe widths. (added to docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md by this same commit, 70cb842a)
  • The prefilter is applied here as ONE constant, screen_dilation = 0.3, where Mip-Splatting (arXiv:2311.16493) states it is a SUM of 0.1 screen-space and 0.2 object-space. A 2D UI wants only the screen half, and it is plausibly the object half doing the blurring — "Nothing here measures that split — it is the next experiment, not a conclusion of this one." (P5_RESULTS.md:169-174)
  • SETTLED 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-144) — kept here because the withdrawal says more than the correction. This entry USED to open "THE INSTRUMENT MAY STILL BE LYING, and this one is OPEN, not fixed", and it was right: sdf_coverage (src/fit/analytic.rs:44) painted the analytic reference from Target::sdf, a distance LOWER BOUND over the primitives. The line changed to Target::combined on 2026-09-14 and the published tables were NOT re-measured for six days. They have been now, and the prediction this record made — "0.0335 and 0.1775 if line 44 used Target::combined" — is exactly what the re-run produced for max|e| at 1x on t3 and t6 (0.033467 and 0.177534). The predicted DIRECTION was also right: every figure moved in the analytic arm's favour, so the atom arms had been judged against a handicapped opponent. What the record did NOT predict is that one of its own findings rested on the defect: see number 9. The blast radius outside aspect 3 — P2, aspect 1, aspect 2 — is NOT re-measured here and remains owed.
  • The ceiling breach does not survive onto disk. BURNDOWN.toml L-7 (open): Aspect3Report::ceiling_violations() (src/fit/aspect3.rs:896) detects it and lights-p5 prints a loud 'THE REFITTED CEILING IS NOT A CEILING' block (src/bin/lights_p5.rs:657-677), but there is no ceiling_violation column in either TSV, so a reader of the table must recompute it — as I had to.
  • The pixel-search half of the search-convention table exists in NO committed artefact. search_probe (src/bin/lights_p5.rs:581-615) prints it to stdout, and L-7 records that "the one number that sizes the search convention's own contribution is recorded in prose only" (src/fit/greedy_rs_comment/the_search_is_gaussian_tuned.md:60-66). It also probes only selected.first() under profiles.first() — i.e. t2 under contract() — so the confound is sized on one class and one profile, not across the table.
  • The image manifest is stdout-only. P5_RESULTS.md:7-8 cites "manifest with absolute paths and captions printed by the run itself"; there is no manifest file in docs/lights/p5 (50 files: 2 TSVs + 48 PNGs). The captions that explain the 48 strips do not survive the run.
  • The frozen atom set itself is not serialised, so the frozen arm cannot be reproduced without a refit. BURNDOWN.toml L-47 (open): "nothing in the crate can save or load a scene — a fit exists only inside the process that produced it", with the explicit next step "make lights-p5 emit the frozen atom set alongside its TSVs so aspect 3's frozen arm becomes reproducible without a refit".
  • The 31/0 test result comes from a suite that contains a known race. BURNDOWN.toml L-14 and L-16 (both open): three aspect-3 tests build a FIXED process-global temp path and remove_dir_all it before spawning the binary (tests/aspect3.rs:828 lights-p5-banner, :1081 lights-p5-strips, :1181 lights-p5-audit-labels), so a second concurrent copy of the suite makes them fail — measured 26 passed / 3 failed at 06:26 with another job running against 30 passed / 0 failed at 06:44 idle. Worse, scripts/mutate_par.py isolates CARGO_TARGET_DIR but not TMPDIR and scores detected = out.returncode != 0, so a mutation naming one of those three can be scored CAUGHT because of the race rather than because the test detected anything. NOTE IN MITIGATION, checked by me: the L-119 regression case is in-process (no subprocess, no temp dir) and is not one of the three.
  • Re-running is expensive and single-threaded. BURNDOWN.toml L-95 (open) measured this exact invocation at 21m32s wall for 6m59s of CPU on an 18-core machine (6 P + 12 E) — under a third of one core while seventeen idle — because grep -cE &#x27;rayon|par_iter|Concurrency|sweep&#x27; returns 0 for src/fit/aspect3.rs, src/fit/scale.rs and src/bin/lights_p5.rs.
  • The hardware is named only as "macOS aarch64, release, f64" (P5_RESULTS.md:5). No machine model is given in the document, so the render_ns column and any timing read off these artefacts is unattributed except by L-95's separate 18-core note. Per the project's own convention, figures measured before Aug 2026 belong to a different machine; this run is dated 2026-09-09.
  • The edge headline carries a profile qualifier the summary sentence drops: "meets at every rung of both ladders" is true under contract(). Under prefiltered() the same frozen t2 arm reads no at magnify 1x (1.031975) and at minify 1x, 0.5x and 0.25x (1.418976, 1.816269, 2.943661).
  • "The bar fails below 0.5x on the edge" (commit 70cb842a, echoed into P2_RESULTS.md) is one rung loose: the analytic arm fails AT the 0.5x rung (transition 1.600), and meets at 1x.

Is a persistent patch/splat light-transport graph — with event-driven (neuromorphic) updates and purpose-built silicon — worth building for a 2.5D IDE canvas?

transport2026-09-08no command on record19 refuted
the answer

No: it "changes the algorithm, not the project — build nothing yet", because in a 2.5D card canvas all fronts face +z so co-facing surfaces exchange exactly zero, leaving ‖M‖∞ ≈ 0.01 and a second bounce ≈ 1e-4, so the only surviving signal is a rim contact shadow that a screen-space pass already delivers; the study is a literature-and-reasoning record, it ends on a one-day falsification gate, and as of 2026-09-09 that gate has never been run and the crate contains zero lines of transport code.

the question it was built to answer

Does a persistent, signed-delta transport graph over surface patches (or over gaussian splats), optionally accelerated by event-driven neuromorphic hardware, deliver any visible lighting phenomenon in fulgor's 2.5D node canvas that a screen-space AO pass cannot — and is it worth building?

criterion, and its convention

The pass criterion is VISUAL AND HUMAN-JUDGED, not numeric: two renders of the same real canvas side by side at 100% and 200% zoom, judged by eye. Falsification, verbatim (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:292-296): "if you cannot tell them apart, or the only difference is a rim tint you would not notice unpaired, the line is dead and it cost a day instead of three weeks"; passing means naming "a named visual phenomenon that transport delivers and a screen-space pass cannot". TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:455-456 restates the gate as "unchanged, plus: name the effect SSAO cannot produce". Four kill criteria are stated (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:314-316): indistinguishable images; OR required patch size ≤3px; OR measured out-degree 4-8 (the design collapses into ordinary dirty-tile invalidation); OR IDE renderer power delta < 2 W. The baseline convention is explicit and adverse to the proposal — "freeze the baseline in writing as the traditional renderer *with* dirty-rect, idle-skip and viewport culling" (:311-312), not against a full re-solve nobody ships. The internal analytic criterion is signal against the 8-bit display quantum 1/256 and operator convergence ‖M‖∞ < 1.

reproduce it
NONE — no reproducing invocation exists, and that is deliberate rather than a gap in the record. The study is a literature-and-reasoning workflow, self-described at docs/lights/TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:349-354 as "Five verification workflows, ~2.5M subagent tokens", whose raw per-agent returns lived in `subagents/workflows/wf_*/journal.jsonl` and are declared "not durable across machines, so the conclusions above are the record". Writing a cargo line here would be a reconstruction of something that never ran as code, so this field says NONE on purpose and the counter of runnable experiments should not count it.

THE ABSENCE IS RE-CHECKABLE, and I re-checked it on 2026-09-14; both of these returned exactly what the record claims:
  grep -rniE "radiosity|form_factor|form factor|enlighten|ssao" crates/lights/src crates/lights/tests --include="*.rs" | wc -l   ->  0
  grep -rn "BroadbandPatch" crates/lights/src --include="*.rs"   ->  only Sampler::BroadbandPatch (src/fit/scale.rs) and the n4-broadband-patch content class (src/fit/content.rs), both aspect-2/3 noise samplers unrelated to transport

THE RECORD IS REPRODUCED BY READING, and all four of these were run on 2026-09-14 and returned what the record says they return:
  sed -n '1,354p' docs/lights/TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md          (354 lines — the record itself)
  git show 84311742                                          (the commit that introduced the doc)
  sed -n '443,458p' docs/plan/TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md            (the backlog item, its gate and its deciders)
  sed -n '1582,1600p' crates/lights/BURNDOWN.toml            (entry L-107, the 2026-09-09 audit of that item)

THE COMMAND THAT WOULD SETTLE IT does not exist as code and is specified in prose at TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:283-291: render one real atrium canvas (~60 node cards at real z-offsets over the real background, real albedos) twice — (1) an offline path-traced reference, an unattended overnight run, and (2) SSAO plus a contact-hardened drop shadow in `egui::Painter` with no transport graph — and put them side by side at 100% and 200%. Until someone writes that, this record is not reproducible by running anything.
what it measured
measurementvaluewhere it comes from
‖M‖∞ on a visible card face≈ 0.01TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:25. Follows from F_sky > 0.99 on a visible face; this is the number the whole verdict rests on.
F_sky on a visible face> 0.99TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:25. Nearly all of a card front's view is sky, so nearly nothing is exchanged with other surfaces.
Second-bounce magnitude≈ 1e-4TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:25. Doc says this is 'four orders of magnitude below the 1/256 display quantum'; 1/256 = 0.00390625, so the true ratio is 39× (~1.6 orders) — see not_settled.
Sealed back-face cavity (the one strongly coupled block)~4px gap, F ≈ 0.98, ρ(M) ≈ 0.88TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:26-28. The only place real transport happens — and 'nobody can see into' it.
Patch size: affordable vs required20px used vs ≤3px neededTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:121-122 (F8). 'The affordable arithmetic used 20px patches; resolving the 4px contact gradient needs ≤3px.' Also a standing kill criterion (:315).
Contact-shadow gradient width4pxTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:122. The feature the entire surviving signal consists of, and the feature the affordable discretisation cannot resolve.
Cost of refining to resolve the band~8e4 patches (3.5× the whole estimated scene), ~5e6 links (≈25×), drag path 12-25 msTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:122-125 (F8), against a stated 16.6 ms frame budget.
Occlusion locality — energy by shell63% within λ, 86% within 2λ, 95% within 3λTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:97-100 (F4). Energy per shell ~ e^(−r/λ); occlusion is the sole source of locality.
Occlusion locality — link count by shell8% within λ, 32% within 2λ, 58% within 3λTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:97-100 (F4). Links are mostly long-and-weak: 'weight any partition by event volume, never by edge count.'
Drettakis & Sillion '97 update breakdown0.08 s find (15%) of a 0.53 s update; 0.40 s apply (75%)TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:81-83 (F2) and the correction row at :148. Refutes the study's own prediction that finding dirty edges dominates.
Naive partition cut without clustering1 − 1/k = 94% at k=16TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:86-88 (F3). Why hierarchical clustering is a precondition, not an optimisation.
Structural-plasticity requirement vs demonstrated rate1e7-1e8 edge creations/s required vs 1e1-1e3/s demonstratedTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:106-107 (F5); demonstrated only on SpiNNaker / BrainScaleS PPU, whose cores are small CPUs, never on Loihi's fixed synapse pipeline.
Rim-band link scaling≈3.3× before glyphsTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:116-118 (F6). Rim-band links scale with total perimeter, not card count, and 'text is the highest-perimeter geometry in a UI'.
Splat overlap multiplicity on reconstructed content3-10, so Σ_j F_ij ≫ 1TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:135 (F9). Non-partition is structural for unparameterised splats — the operator cannot be made stochastic by construction.
Energy per RGB edge-event: custom silicon vs one CPU core10-30 pJ (7nm SRAM-adjacent) vs ~0.5-3 nJ per irregular edge-visit on one M5 Max P-core = 30-100×, central ~50×, ±3×TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:164-167. Anchored on three measured chips: TrueNorth 26 pJ, Loihi 23.6 pJ, ODIN 12.7 pJ.
Fabric crossover pointbreak-even at ~1e5 edge-events/frame; 8.7× at 1e6; 42× at 1e7TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:169-173, at IDE scale N=1e5, K=32, 12 B/edge = 38 MB, SRAM retention leakage ~9 mW = 153 µJ/frame at 60 Hz. 'The high static fraction is the fabric's worst case, not its best.'
Repair : propagation cost ratio8 nJ : 40 pJ = 200:1, independent of the dirty fraction fTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:175-176. Yields an Amdahl cap of 1.005× on transport and ~1.001× on frame energy.
Whole-system power win from custom silicon~58 mW → ~15 mW, i.e. 20-60 mW saved on a 5-15 W machine (0.2-1.2%)TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:176-178. Against a stated kill criterion of 'IDE renderer power delta < 2 W' (:316).
Multi-chip SerDes penalty100-400 mW/lane static; 4 lanes = 0.4-1.6 W against 1-10 mW dynamic = 400-1600× regression per added unitTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:180-184. Bandwidth is never binding — one 5 Gb/s lane carries 840k 12-byte events per frame.
FPGA capacity (12 B/edge, 32 B/node, degree 32, 80% usable)ECP5-85F 921 nodes; Artix-7 200T 3,225; ZU7EV 9,578; Alveo U250 108,890TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:188-191. 'The largest fully-open part holds a unit test; the smallest part fitting 1e5 splats idles at 30-40 W.'
Partition cut law vs effective dimension dc ≈ 1.1·R/L: d=2.0 → 2.5%; d=2.5 → 9.8%; d=3.0 → 24%TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:204-207. Target is c ≤ 3%; c=10% is already a 1.9× penalty. d is box-countable from an existing .ply 'in an afternoon' — never measured.
Bit-quantised visibility saving (ESTIMATE)38 MB → ~4 MB; leakage 9 mW → ~1 mW; 2-4 bits still buys 8-16×TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:231-235, explicitly labelled ESTIMATE, i.e. arithmetic and not measurement (:353-354).
The surviving work boundtotal firings ≤ |E_injected| / (ε·(1−ρ)), converging in ~8 generations at mean reflectance 0.5TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:321-323. The one result the study says survives with no hardware required — a directly dialable per-frame work cap for a 16.6 ms budget.
Convex-card internal transportidentically zero; only concave relief (bevels, insets) contributes, ~5% of areaTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:251-256. The segment between two points on a convex opaque surface passes through the body.
Study costfive verification workflows, ~2.5M subagent tokens; 354 lines of docTRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:349-350 for the workflow count; git show 84311742 --stat shows docs/lights/TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md at 354 insertions.
what it settled
  • F1 — a gaussian splat is a bad transport node: it has no unique normal, no area and no front/back, so operator row sums exceed 1 and a signed solve DIVERGES, presenting as a slow scene brightening that 'gets misdiagnosed' as a tone-mapping bug, and SH colour is view-dependent by construction (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:73-79).
  • F1's repair collapses the proposal's own justification: patch nodes fix divergence because Σ_j F_ij = 1 by construction giving ‖M‖∞ ≤ ρ_max < 1 — 'but a one-sided row clamp on the splat operator does the same in one line' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:77-79), a point carried into the backlog gate at TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:445-447.
  • F2 — edge DISCOVERY is not the cost; APPLICATION is: Drettakis & Sillion measured 0.08 s identifying affected links out of a 0.53 s update (15%) against 0.40 s applying it (75%), so the expense is building the index and re-evaluating visibility (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:80-83).
  • F3 — hierarchical clustering is a precondition, not an optimisation (without it the far field is dense and the naive cut is 1 − 1/k = 94% at k=16), but clustering is an APPROXIMATION and therefore destroys the exactness that was supposed to distinguish this from ReSTIR (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:85-90).
  • F4 — inverse-square falloff does NOT make transport local, it makes it scale-invariant: r² growth in partner count exactly cancels 1/r² falloff so energy is equal per distance octave, and occlusion is the sole source of locality, giving 63/86/95% of energy within λ/2λ/3λ against only 8/32/58% of the links (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:92-104).
  • F5 — structural plasticity was the hardware disqualifier at 1e7-1e8 edge creations/s required against 1e1-1e3/s demonstrated, and dissolving it with a static topology is self-defeating because zero edge-events during relighting sits permanently below the fabric's own break-even and the kernel degenerates to SpMV (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:105-110).
  • F6 — a dragged node churns its whole incident edge set every frame, and worse than first assessed: tooltips, dropdowns, context menus, drag ghosts, hover scrollbars and resize handles add and delete occluders at HOVER frequency, in exactly the 0-10px z-band where all the surviving transport lives (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:112-118).
  • F8 — the discretisation is self-contradictory at the only scale that matters: the affordable arithmetic used 20px patches while the 4px contact gradient needs ≤3px, and refining makes the rim band alone ~8e4 patches (3.5× the whole estimated scene) with ~5e6 links (≈25×) and a 12-25 ms drag path against a 16.6 ms budget (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:119-125).
  • F8's second horn — patch size is under two incompatible criteria at once, scene-fixed (or canvas zoom churns the graph) and pixel-tracking (or it bands visibly), and 'zoom is continuous' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:125-128).
  • F9 — the scope premise is a scissors: patches are near-free only for authored parametric rectangles, which need no splat renderer because egui::Painter already draws them, while content that would justify a splat substrate is reconstructed and unparameterised where meshing returns whole, albedo extraction from fitted SH is ill-posed and overlap multiplicity 3-10 makes non-partition structural — so 'no scope justifies the splat substrate and the patch transport at once' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:130-136).
  • The hardware verdict is a crossover, not a difficulty argument: custom silicon beats one CPU core by 30-100× per edge-event, but only above ~1e5 edge-events/frame, so 'the high static fraction is the fabric's worst case, not its best' — and system-level the repair:propagation ratio of 200:1 caps the whole thing at 1.005× (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:162-178).
  • The FPGA prototype path is unrepresentative in exactly the dimension of interest: multi-chip SerDes static power (100-400 mW/lane, link-up regardless of traffic, ungateable at 60 Hz) causes a 400-1600× regression per added unit, and the die-to-die interposer that would invert it (UCIe-Advanced ~0.25 pJ/bit) requires the custom silicon the prototype exists to avoid (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:179-186).
  • Two of Dacian's design contributions were verified sound and one was reframed: the operator/source split B = (I − RF)⁻¹E is exact for any BRDF, bit-quantised visibility plus an activity mask correctly turns edge creation into a bit flip in a preallocated mask (1e8 bit flips/s trivial where 1e8 allocations/s is impossible), and LOD-on-patch-size is independently the hierarchical refinement oracle F3 says is mandatory (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:212-241).
  • The operator/source split lands the wrong way round for an IDE: it makes recolour, theme, selection and hover free — 'and they were already free' — while drag, resize, expand/collapse, zoom and the popover layer all touch the operator, and those are what an IDE does (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:222-226).
  • Post-denoising was reframed rather than accepted: OptiX, OIDN and DLSS Ray Reconstruction remove zero-mean Monte Carlo noise, whereas coarse-LOD and 1-bit visibility produce STRUCTURED BIAS — banding and hard shadow terraces — which a denoiser 'smooths into plausible wrongness rather than removes'; the salvage is a learned corrector trained on (coarse solve, converged reference) pairs, for which the lights ray-cast method is a free unlimited reference generator (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:243-249).
  • Three things survive the verdict: the work bound total firings ≤ |E_injected| / (ε·(1−ρ)) as a dialable per-frame cap requiring no hardware; the 2.5D closed forms (Hottel algebra for axis-aligned rectangles at discrete depths, occlusion as rectangle boolean intersection, discovery as an R-tree range query O(log N + c)) — which nonetheless break on rotation, rounded corners, the hex layout already in this codebase, and curved Bezier wires; and the framing that a transport graph is a dataflow graph with signed deltas, which fulgor already is (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:319-337).
  • The honest shape if built anyway is not a crate: a bounded, non-divergent, deterministic SINGLE-BOUNCE indirect term over a geometry-derived link set, recomputed from scratch each frame with no cached solution, no signed deltas, no fabric and no silicon, transporting IRRADIANCE rather than radiosity — 'That is a shader. A page of code plus an R-tree, living in optical next to the painter — not a crate, and not a revived lights' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:259-278).
  • A specific silent-failure trap is named for anyone who builds it: never ship the unoccluded form factor 'as an optimisation', because F must always be F_unoccluded × V̄ — the unoccluded factor is exactly wrong at the shadow boundary where the entire visible signal lives, and 'it fails silently as a plausible, energy-conserving, wrong image' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:272-276).
  • THE GATE, which is where the study ends and where it still stands: one day, no crate — render one real atrium canvas (~60 node cards, real z-offsets, real background, real albedos) twice, an offline path-traced reference against SSAO plus a contact-hardened drop shadow in egui::Painter, side by side at 100% and 200%; if indistinguishable the line is dead at a cost of one day instead of three weeks, and only if distinguishable does anything else become worth measuring, because 'every other measurement presumes the effect is worth having, and that presumption is the one that was never tested' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:281-302).
  • The gate has not been run and the salvage has not been taken up, measured 2026-09-09 and recorded as BURNDOWN entry L-107 (state=absent, status=open): grep -rniE &#x27;radiosity|form_factor|form factor|enlighten|ssao&#x27; crates/lights/src crates/lights/tests --include=&#x27;*.rs&#x27; | wc -l returns 0, the only patch identifiers in the crate are the aspect-2/3 content samplers Sampler::BroadbandPatch (src/fit/scale.rs:221,234,235,251,315) and n4-broadband-patch (src/fit/content.rs:18,383,405), 'there is no atrium canvas render pair anywhere in docs/ and no path-traced reference on disk', and 'nothing measured today contradicts the 2026-09-08 verdict build nothing yet' (crates/lights/BURNDOWN.toml:1582-1600).
  • The record for this study is three places and no published page: docs/lights/TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md, the message of commit 84311742, and the 16-line backlog item at docs/plan/TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:443-458 — Artifact action:list shows the only two lights pages are 'What Crisp Pixels Cost in Gaussians' (aspect 3) and 'lights burn-down', neither of which covers transport.
what it refuted
  • Negative-energy shooting is Chen '90 — REFUTED by its own authors: it predates Chen, appearing in Buckalew & Fussell '89 (Illumination Networks, SIGGRAPH, CG 23(3):89-98) and Puech, Sillion & Vedel '90 (I3D, CG 24(2):51-57), which both 1990 papers cite as the source (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:146, :47-48).
  • The update fires negative energy first and then re-shoots — REFUTED: both signs fire together, and the polarity is the opposite of the intuition — POSITIVE energy erases a stale shadow, NEGATIVE energy creates a new one (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:147).
  • Finding the dirty edges dominates the update cost — REFUTED by Drettakis & Sillion's measurement: 15% find, 75% apply (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:148).
  • ~1.6 GB at 100 links/node — REFUTED as presuming a refinement oracle the design lacks; without it 1M nodes is 16 TB, realistically 6-13 GB, and 1M nodes is itself 2-3 orders too many, since at 1e4 surfaces even the DENSE matrix is only ~1.2 GB (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:149).
  • 5-20× realized speedup — REFUTED as measured against a full re-solve, which nobody ships; against a dirty-rect baseline it is 1.5-3×, and 0-to-negative on pan/zoom (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:150).
  • A globally dynamic scene is slower for an incremental method — REFUTED: Chen measured 1.15× and 1.16×, still faster; it goes negative only when paying graph maintenance (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:151).
  • Diffuse-only limitation is what killed radiosity — REFUTED: MESHING killed it first, and splats delete that cause (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:152).
  • Catastrophic cancellation is the numerical hazard of signed deltas — REFUTED: it is avoidable by construction via a per-link last_contribution and writing new−old, which is order-free and idempotent; the real hazard is Chen's Fig. 5 ordering bug, which needs per-node version stamps (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:153).
  • Splats-as-transport-nodes is novel — REFUTED as already published: RadiosityGS (SIGGRAPH Asia 2025, arXiv:2509.18497, with code) does radiosity over Gaussian surfels and PRTGS (ACM MM 2024) does per-splat transfer (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:154, :60-61).
  • A splat is a view-independent point of light — REFUTED: it has no unique normal, no area and no front/back, and its SH colour is view-dependent by construction (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:155, F1 at :73-79).
  • Exactness differentiates this from ReSTIR — REFUTED as never deliverable: the graph is exact only with respect to its own truncation, a systematic bias that does not average away, while resampling noise does; the real differentiators are determinism on thin features, zero idle cost and provenance (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:156, F3 at :88-90).
  • A 99% static fraction favours the approach — REFUTED as conflating static geometry, static illumination and static screen; the distribution is bimodal with little mass where a graph wins, and with draw_node at 3-8 ms the static fraction of WORK is 0% (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:157).
  • 1-10 pJ/bit is the chip-crossing cost — REFUTED: that figure is long-reach SerDes; die-to-die is 0.25-2 pJ/bit (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:158).
  • The patch-graph reformulation is a new design — REFUTED as an existing shipped product: cluster-to-cluster form factors precomputed with direct light, sky cubemap, albedo and emissive streamed per frame IS Geomerics Enlighten, shipped ~2011, and the operator/source split must be 'state[d] as a discipline, never as a discovery' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:58, :218-220; echoed at TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:444-445).
  • 'Event-based' is the right name for the idea — REFUTED on naming grounds: that phrase plus 'gaussian splatting' already means event CAMERAS as input across 8+ papers; use 'incremental' or 'signed-delta' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:65-67).
  • The bit-quantised-visibility 'jitter' worry — the artifact was misnamed: the real artifact at LOD level transitions is POPPING, and the C^∞ gaussian substrate is its mitigation because it cross-fades smoothly where polygonal patches snap (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:238-241).
  • Post-denoising will clean up the coarse solve — REFUTED as a category error: OptiX/OIDN/DLSS RR remove zero-mean Monte Carlo noise, while coarse LOD and 1-bit visibility produce structured bias that a denoiser turns into 'plausible wrongness' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:243-247).
  • The hardware analysis was run against the wrong machine: CLAUDE.md's '2014 MacBook Pro, 16GB' is stale — the machine is an Apple M5 Max with 137 GB — and the doc states plainly that this 'misled part of the analysis' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:196-198).
  • The line is new work — REFUTED by the project's own war map: docs/war_map.toml:66-70 already marks lights group = "Legacy", status = "Deferred", "Archived — not carried forward into current architecture", so this is a reformulation of an experiment the project had already retired (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:337-343, verified against docs/war_map.toml).
what it does NOT settle
  • The gate itself has never been run. The entire verdict rests on a reasoning argument about a workload; the ONE measurement that would confirm or overturn it — the path-traced vs SSAO image pair — does not exist. BURNDOWN L-107 (crates/lights/BURNDOWN.toml:1582-1600) records this on 2026-09-09: 'That measurement has not been made: there is no atrium canvas render pair anywhere in docs/ and no path-traced reference on disk.'
  • The study says this of itself, verbatim, and it is the strongest caveat in the record: 'Every other measurement presumes the effect is worth having, and that presumption is the one that was never tested' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:301-302).
  • THE INSTRUMENT IS ARITHMETIC, NOT MEASUREMENT, wherever marked: 'anything labelled ESTIMATE is arithmetic, not measurement' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:353-354). That covers the bit-quantised-visibility saving (38 MB → ~4 MB, 9 mW → ~1 mW, TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:232-233) and by extension the whole hardware section, which is anchored on three published chip figures rather than on anything run here.
  • The headline sentence's own arithmetic overstates by ~2.4 orders of magnitude. TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:25-26 says the second bounce at ≈ 1e-4 is 'four orders of magnitude below the 1/256 display quantum'; 1/256 = 0.00390625, so 1e-4 is 39× below it — about 1.6 orders. The CONCLUSION survives (39× below the display quantum is still invisible), but the number as written does not, and this is the one place in a correction log where a figure was not itself checked.
  • The raw evidence is gone. 'Raw per-agent returns are in the session transcript directory under subagents/workflows/wf_*/journal.jsonl — they are not durable across machines, so the conclusions above are the record' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:350-353). Nothing in the five workflows can be re-audited; only the summary survives.
  • 1-bit visibility gives HARD shadow boundaries where classical radiosity carries a fractional term, because patch-to-patch visibility is usually PARTIAL; 2-4 bits still buys 8-16×, but the study does not say which is correct for the workload (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:233-236).
  • The effective dimension d of the splat cloud — the controlling parameter of the whole partitioning analysis, swinging the cut from 2.5% at d=2.0 to 24% at d=3.0 — was never measured. It is 'box-countable from an existing .ply in an afternoon' and nobody did it (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:204-207).
  • The learned-corrector reframing of post-denoising is a proposal, not a result: no corrector was trained and no (coarse solve, converged reference) pair set exists, though the study notes the lights ray-cast method would be a free unlimited reference generator (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:247-249).
  • The salvageable 2.5D closed forms are narrower than they sound: Hottel algebra plus rectangle boolean occlusion holds only for axis-aligned rectangles at discrete depths, and 'breaks on rotation, rounded corners, the hex layout already in this codebase, and curved Bezier wires' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:326-332).
  • The verdict does NOT bear on the splat renderer: 'It does not bear on the splat renderer itself, which is a separate deliverable under Horizon 2' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:38-39), a separation the commit message repeats and TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:31-38 has since hardened by making lights a UI renderer AND a 3D game engine.
  • Three of the four kill criteria are unmeasured, not just the image test: measured out-degree (the 4-8 threshold at which the design collapses into ordinary dirty-tile invalidation), the per-frame changed-fraction histogram across real sessions, and the IDE renderer power delta against a 2 W threshold (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:308-316).
  • The baseline the study demands has never been frozen in writing: 'freeze the baseline in writing as the traditional renderer with dirty-rect, idle-skip and viewport culling' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:311-312) — no such document exists in docs/.
  • Confidence marking is by the author's own hand: 'Status of everything below: VERIFIED unless marked ESTIMATE or OPEN' and 'Confidence is marked where it is not high' (TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md:10, :353). No independent party re-derived any figure; the verification was five subagent workflows in the same session as the proposals they judged.
  • The prior-art table is not cross-referenced by the crate's own paper triage: docs/lights/CORPUS_READING_MAP.md contains no hit for 'transport', 'radiosity', 'Enlighten', 'Drettakis', 'Sloan' or 'PRT', so nothing links the transport bibliography to the reading map that governs the rest of the crate's research.
  • BURNDOWN L-107's own supporting count is slightly off, which matters only as a reminder that the audit is itself an instrument: it reports grep -rni &#x27;patch&#x27; crates/lights/src as '5 hits, all Sampler::BroadbandPatch in src/fit/scale.rs:221,234,251,315'; re-running it today (excluding dispatch) gives 10 hits across src/fit/scale.rs:221,234,235,251,315, src/fit/content.rs:18,383,405 and src/bin/lights_p4.rs:197. The substance is unchanged — every one is a content sampler, none is transport.
artefacts on disk

Is the thirteen-paper gaussian-splatting corpus well chosen — judged first against the lights renderer alone, then re-judged against renderer + iris + compression

prior-art2026-09-08no command on record16 refuted
the answer

Re-judged against the three objectives the papers were actually collected for, the corpus is well chosen — 4 of 13 papers earn at least one USE, 0 of 13 are SKIP on all three objectives, and the first reading's "assembled for a different project" verdict was a product of its single-objective frame rather than of the papers.

the question it was built to answer

Do the 13 PDFs in docs/gaussian_splatting/ serve this project, and if the first triage said no, was that a fact about the corpus or about the question it was asked?

criterion, and its convention

A four-valued verdict assigned per paper PER OBJECTIVE, not per paper: USE / REFERENCE / SKIP, with two one-off values used once each (NOTE, DO NOT CITE for SWAGSplatting under lights; SPLIT for AVS under compression). Convention: 13 papers × 3 objective columns (lights renderer, iris decoration extraction, compression) = 39 verdict cells, plus a fifth free-text "other" column carrying no verdict vocabulary (CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:23-37). The FIRST reading used a different, single criterion — "which renderer phase does this paper serve?" — which the second reading rules unsatisfiable by construction for an analysis/inverse paper, "so the frame could only ever return SKIP" (map:50-53). There is no numeric threshold anywhere; the pass criterion is a human judgement per cell, and the second reading's guard against over-correction is procedural, not numeric: "a critic was pointed specifically at over-correction" (map:8).

reproduce it
NONE — this experiment is a reading, not a program. There is no binary, no script, no cargo target and no recorded prompt: `ls scripts/` contains nothing that touches the corpus and `grep -rln "gaussian_splatting" scripts/ .claude/` returns nothing, both re-checked 2026-09-14. Inventing a cargo line for it would be a fabrication, so this field says NONE on purpose and the counter of runnable experiments should not count it.
