what it settled
  • The second reading reversed the first's verdict on the collection outright: from 'this corpus was assembled for a different project' — reached with ten of thirteen marked SKIP or REFERENCE under a renderer-only frame (commit 47946dca) — to 'it was well chosen', with 4 of 13 papers earning at least one USE and 0 of 13 SKIPped on all three objectives (counted from CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:25-37).
  • The reversal is attributed to the question, not the papers: 'It was asked which renderer phase does this paper serve? An analysis / inverse paper serves no rendering phase, so the frame could only return SKIP' (map:50-52), and the objective set handed to the first reader was incomplete because two of the three real objectives — iris decoration extraction and compression — are written down elsewhere in the repository (map:52-53).
  • Four specific verdicts moved between the readings: Image-GS went from peripheral to the centre, USE on all three objectives at once ('the renderer frame asked what it renders; it renders nothing new — it fits, which is the whole of the iris objective', map:55-58); VALA went from dismissed-as-a-language-paper to USE for lights, because its two mechanisms contain no language content and CLIP merely supplies the vectors being aggregated (map:59-62); MEGS², CoRe-GS, GeoSplat and GPSToken were all filtered on domain when their mechanisms are the segmentation, allocation and compaction machinery iris needs (map:63-67); and the map's own conclusion is that 'domain was never the right filter for any paper here' (map:72).
  • One paper was missed by BOTH readings: Particle Merging-and-Splitting, 'the only paper of thirteen about primitives changing over time, against a settled plan requirement that every decoration declare static|animated' (map:68-70) — recorded as a failure of the second reading as well as the first.
  • The second reading was explicitly guarded against over-correction — 'a critic was pointed specifically at over-correction' (map:8) — and six SKIPs are recorded unsoftened with their reasons (map:39-44), so the correction did not become a blanket rehabilitation.
  • The Image-GS reading refuted a claim the project had been steering on: Image-GS normalises rather than alpha-composites (its Eq. 5 divides the top-K weighted colour sum by the sum of weights, a partition of unity), under which a straight edge costs exactly two atoms with a 10–90 transition width of 4.394·σ²/d independent of edge length — so 'a step function costs an unbounded number of gaussians' is false, and the accurate statement is that gaussians are cheap on flat fills and straight boundaries of any length and expensive per unit of CURVATURE (map:82-98).
  • That result re-drew the hybrid split line the project had been planning against: text and borders had been bucketed together as the expensive case, and borders are in fact the cheap case at 2–5 atoms, length-independent, against ~20 atoms for a 12 px glyph (map:96-98, 108).
  • The second commit (8d6bda7a) answered whether the corpus needed supplementing: a four-front search returned 24 papers with exactly ONE overlap against the 13 owned (Image-GS), so the corpus did need supplementing — but only outside the 3DGS vocabulary it was gathered by (map:131-133, commit message).
  • Two of the acquisition list's rulings correct the project rather than the papers: the signed/alpha constraint is narrower than had been asserted (3DGabSplat and NegGS put the sign in the colour while opacity stays non-negative, so front-to-back alpha compositing stays valid and sortable, and the crate's Additive path is needed for iris dictionary fitting rather than necessarily for the UI, map:187-193); and StopThePop does not apply to 2D orthographic pan/zoom at all, because its popping mechanism is order inversion under camera rotation, so OrderingModel::CentreDepth 'is provably sufficient for the node-graph target — assert it as a theorem in the suite rather than paying for a resort window' (map:195-200).
  • The reading found two of the corpus papers' own headlines unsupported by their own tables: SWAGSplatting computes its headline against the arithmetic mean of six baselines rather than the best (+2.70/+3.48 dB, falling to +0.34/+0.28 dB against the method it extends, with its own ablation pricing the headline mechanism at 0.91% PSNR, map:119-123), and AVS claims 40% against a measured 25.64% (map:124-125) — the AVS pair I re-verified directly from the PDF (abstract 'a 40% storage reduction'; results '3.343 MB (25.64%)').
  • The iris design derived from the reading is additive rather than a rewrite, and lands on two hooks that already exist in committed code: Image-GS's initialiser wants the Sobel map cv_extract.rs already computes, and cv_extract's Saliency method 'is already declared and falls through to Dominant' — verified at crates/iris/src/ai_extract/cv_extract.rs:106-118 ('Saliency-weighted K-means. Placeholder for P4.3e iteration 2; currently routes to Dominant') with the fallback threshold at :93, and the ThemeExtractor trait it would register through at crates/iris/src/ai_extract/client.rs:103.
  • The reading's central formula survived contact with the code: the 4.394·σ²/d edge width is now enforced by tests that did not exist when it was written — tests/normalised_blend.rs:479-556 (two_atoms_render_the_closed_form_logistic, asserting the measured width against 2·ln9·σ²/d to 1e-6, and that 4.394 is 2 ln 9 to 5e-4) and tests/fit_edge.rs:91 (the literal 4.394449154672439) — and tests/fit_greedy.rs:314 sets the closed-form Image-GS pair beside the fitter's two atoms on the same target.
  • One sentence of the map went stale within hours of being written and propagated: map:147 says Mip-Splatting's Eq. 10 prefactor 'is character-for-character the gain computed at kernel/gaussian.rs and discarded at project.rs'; the gain half is confirmed (crates/lights/src/kernel/gaussian.rs:72 via moment_prefilter, gain = sqrt(det Σ / det(Σ+sI))), but the 'discarded' half stopped being true the same day at commit 6ac92f33, which added prefiltered() with energy_compensation: true (settings.rs:73-77), after which project.rs:151-155 applies view.opacity * gain — and the sentence was then copied into docs/plan/TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:459-462 a day later, where BURNDOWN.toml entries L-37 and L-101 now record it as uncorrected in both places.
what it refuted
  • 'This corpus was assembled for a different project' — the first triage's own conclusion, withdrawn by the second reading as a framing error, not a finding about the papers (CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:4-8, 50-53).
  • 'A step function costs an unbounded number of gaussians' — REFUTED (map:105). Under Eq. 5's partition of unity a straight edge is two atoms at any length.
  • 'Gaussians are terrible at hard edges' — SPLIT, not refuted: 'true only of Image-GS as configured (σ∈[5,10], fp16 means)' (map:104).
  • '30dB/160KB is on photographs, the favourable case' — REFUTED: it is a stylised illustration, and photographs are the paper's UNFAVOURABLE case (map:106).
  • 'A UI is ~90% crisp chrome' — no source; 'by area a node graph is mostly flat fill, the cheapest content there is' (map:107).
  • 'Hybrid split at text, borders, 1px strokes' — right conclusion, WRONG SPLIT LINE: borders are cheap, text is the expensive one (map:108).
  • 'Authored rects don't need a splat renderer' — CONFIRMED, 'the strongest of the set, and untouched' (map:109) — recorded here because the same table that killed five claims left this one standing.
  • VALA is a 'language' paper — refuted: its two mechanisms contain no language content (map:59-62).
  • Domain is a usable filter for this corpus — refuted for every paper: 'every one of them manipulates a fitted anisotropic gaussian field' (map:72-74).
  • The project's own signed/alpha assertion — narrowed: only sign in the DENSITY forces an additive signed buffer; sign in the colour keeps alpha compositing valid and sortable (map:187-193).
  • That StopThePop's popping applies to the node-graph target — refuted: its mechanism is order inversion under camera ROTATION, absent under orthographic parallel rays (map:195-200).
  • SWAGSplatting's headline as a comparison against the best baseline — refuted against its own tables (map:119-123).
  • AVS's abstract claim of 40% — refuted against its own measured 25.64% (map:124-125); I re-confirmed both numbers from the PDF.
  • The map's OWN line 147, 'the gain … discarded at project.rs' — refuted the same day by commit 6ac92f33 (prefiltered(), energy_compensation: true) and still uncorrected; BURNDOWN.toml L-101 calls it 'the traceable case of the project's dominant register fault' and shows it propagating into TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md:459-462. [Corrected 2026-09-28 by L-101: map:147 now says the gain is applied at method/splat/project.rs, carries a visible Withdrawn paragraph, and the three top-three papers are marked not on disk; the TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md copy had already been corrected by L-37.]
  • The map's premise that this crate implements Image-GS Eq. 5 — refuted downstream by P2: 'The Image-GS premise did not reproduce. This crate's Normalised is a coverage mix, background·(1−c) + (weighted/weight)·c with c = min(weight,1) — not Eq. 5's pure top-K ratio … Normalised lost to Over on all five non-fill targets' (docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:58-63).
  • The reading's own claim that VALA's visibility gate transfers to iris — refuted by the same run that made it: 'Image-GS atoms have no opacity and aggregate regardless of order, so VALA's visibility gate degenerates — T is identically 1 and the gate becomes a top-K that Image-GS already performs at K=10. The gate belongs to lights; only the streaming median transfers to iris' (docs/iris/DECORATION_EXTRACTION.md:97-101).
what it does NOT settle
  • The first triage's own verdicts do not survive anywhere: grep -rln &#x27;assembled for a different project&#x27; docs/ returns only CORPUS_READING_MAP.md itself, so what the first reading said per paper cannot be checked — only §2's summary of it, written by the reading that overturned it.
  • The map records no provenance at all. It names 'three verification workflows' (map:4) and gives no token counts, no agent journals, no prompts, and no date beyond 2026-09-08 — unlike its sibling docs/lights/TRANSPORT_RESEARCH.md §10, which at least names subagents/workflows/wf_*/journal.jsonl and states those returns 'are not durable across machines, so the conclusions above are the record'.
  • The map never gives a filename. Its short names (Image-GS, AVS, GeoSplat, MEGS², GS-TG…) map onto the 13 PDF titles only by reader inference; nothing in either document states the correspondence.
  • Two of the map's own counts do not match its own lists: 'Six SKIPs survived full scrutiny' (map:39, repeated in commit 47946dca) enumerates five papers across eight paper×objective cells; 'Eleven of thirteen track one coherent thesis' (map:10) names ten papers, leaving the eleventh unidentified among SWAG, Particle Merging and Distributed.
  • The inpainting paper's '1.27 atoms/pixel, ~2,500× denser than Image-GS' (map:40-42) — the load-bearing number under one of the surviving SKIPs — is not printed in that PDF (pdftotext + grep -in &#x27;1.27&#x27; returns nothing) and its derivation is not shown.
  • Nothing here was measured. Every per-paper verdict is a judgement over a PDF, and the map says so of its central case: Image-GS 'does not address hard edges or text … but its Eq. (5) settles the question anyway' (map:79-82) — an analytic argument, not an experiment.
  • The map's own consequence for P2 is that no atom count means anything yet: 'atom counts are renderer-dependent by ~10×, so the blend rule must be settled before any count means anything, and the content classes must be measured separately' (map:111-113). P2's later caveat compounds it: 'in 20 of 24 cells the fitter stopped, not the budget … every count is an upper bound, and a statement about greedy matching pursuit rather than about gaussians' (P2_RESULTS.md:73-88).
  • The top three acquisitions the map's own rulings lean on are not on disk: Mip-Splatting, EWA Volume Splatting and GaussianImage are named at map:147-149 and none is among the 13 PDFs, 'although CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:145-149 lists all three as the Top three acquisitions' (BURNDOWN.toml L-37 evidence; L-101 next-step says to acquire them or mark the list unacquired, 'because the decompose-the-prefilter item cites two of them by identifier as its authority').
  • The instrument (this document) is itself known-stale in one place and uncorrected: git status --porcelain docs/lights/CORPUS_READING_MAP.md is clean, i.e. line 147 has never been fixed, and BURNDOWN.toml L-101 prescribes correcting it in place 'keeping the withdrawal visible rather than silently rewriting'. Its neighbour, line 148 (EWA: 'the prefilter is integral-preserving, not peak-preserving — so energy_compensation is the framework, not an option'), is judged 'still exactly right' and is now pinned by tests/prefilter.rs:97. [Corrected 2026-09-28 by L-101: map:147 now says the gain is applied at method/splat/project.rs, carries a visible Withdrawn paragraph, and the three top-three papers are marked not on disk; the TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md copy had already been corrected by L-37.]
  • The iris design derived from the reading names four limits of its own (DECORATION_EXTRACTION.md:88-105), copied unsoftened: fit cost is 'a third latency tier' that cannot meet cv_extract's <500 ms contract; the VALA visibility gate contradiction above; 'shadows are named in the objective and Image-GS cannot express them — that needs intrinsic-image decomposition, which the corpus lacks'; and 'nothing here extrapolates past the input canvas. Resizing a decoration to an arbitrary widget is a retiling problem and the corpus has no answer.'
  • The gap the whole exercise points at is unmeasured by design: 'No head-to-head of gaussians versus SDF/vector on text or UI content exists. Searched five ways across two independent passes … P2, run properly, is a first measurement, not a replication' (map:206-211), plus five further questions each marked measure-do-not-read (map:215-226).
  • Citation status of six entries in the acquisition list is explicitly incomplete — 3DGabSplat (equations read, authors not captured), Greene & Heckbert 1986, Heckbert's 1989 thesis, Bergeaud & Mallat 1995, Figueras i Ventura 2006, Octree-GS — and two entries are marked DO NOT CITE as unverifiable (map:230-240). The 3DGabSplat ruling in §6 therefore rests on a partially verified source by the map's own admission.
  • Whether the corpus is well chosen for the SECOND target is untested: the burn-down loop states lights has two requirement-level targets, the optical UI and 'a simple 3D game engine' (PM, 2026-09-09; docs/lights/BURNDOWN_LOOP.md:8-10), and the three objectives judged here are renderer, iris and compression — the game target postdates the reading and no column exists for it.

Where does the time go inside a lights fit, and which level of parallelism — the job sweep or the tiles inside one render — is worth taking?

engineering2026-09-09copy-pasteable command10 refuted
the answer

REBUILT 2026-09-28: parallelise the sweep of whole fits and nothing smaller. The rebuilt sweep::map buys 5.30x-6.29x on lights-p2 and 5.60x-5.82x on lights-p4 (medians of five reps each way, three runs, release, M5 Max 18 cores) with byte-identical outputs. THE 2026-09-09 VERDICT, KEPT: Parallelise the sweep of whole fits and nothing smaller: mapping independent fits across 18 cores buys 6.01x–7.00x wall against an 8.83x critical-path ceiling with bit-identical decisions, while tile parallelism inside one render is bit-exact but 1.7x–4.3x SLOWER at the 32x32–64x64 viewports the fitter works at, and inside a fit the time is 65–91% render, 8–10% residual_energy, 2–4% framebuffer allocation and ~1.2–1.9% timer syscalls.

the question it was built to answer

Inside one greedy fit, which functions actually consume the wall clock, and at which granularity — the job sweep over whole fits, or the tiles inside a single render — does adding cores pay, given that no published atom may move?

criterion, and its convention

Correctness gate, and it is an identity test, not a tolerance: a parallel sweep is allowed only if the answer does not move. Concretely, lights-p2 --sweep parallel must write a report.tsv whose data cells are byte-for-byte those of --sweep serial in EVERY column except render_ns_mean and render_ns_ci95, plus byte-identical sweep.tsv, sensitivity.tsv, probes.tsv, bins.tsv and all 66 PNGs, with rows in JOB order rather than completion order. The two timing columns are exempt because the worktree moves them into a separate SERIAL phase run after every fit has finished. Speed is judged in wall-clock seconds against the critical-path ceiling set by the longest single fit (serial-job-sum / longest-job), not against core count. Units: seconds wall, nanoseconds per render, percent of main-thread samples.

reproduce it
bash -c 'CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target cargo build --release -p lights --all-features --bin lights-p2 --bin lights-p4 && rm -rf /tmp/l96_rep && for rep in 1 2 3 4 5; do for bin in lights-p2 lights-p4; do for mode in serial parallel; do /usr/bin/time -p target/release/$bin --out-dir /tmp/l96_rep/$bin-$mode-$rep --sweep $mode 2>&1 >/dev/null | awk -v b=$bin -v m=$mode -v r=$rep "/^real/{print b, m, r, \$2}"; done; done; done'

REBUILT AND RE-MEASURED 2026-09-28 (L-96). The line above is the exact string run from the repository root to produce the 2026-09-28 numbers below. lights-p2 exits 1 on every run (cells that never meet the criterion inside --max-atoms 8 — a result the binary reports, not a failure). The identity half of the criterion is `cargo test -p lights --all-features --test fit_parallel` (13 tests) plus `cmp` on every PNG and on sweep/sensitivity/probes/bins.tsv (with `#` lines dropped) between /tmp/l96_rep/<bin>-serial-<rep> and -parallel-<rep>.

THE 2026-09-09 COMMAND FIELD, KEPT AS HISTORY — every path in it died with the RAM disk:

/Volumes/RamBuild/par-target/release/deps/fit_parallel-b04deeff3a2f5b2a

VERIFIED 2026-09-14: that is the exact binary L-96 ran, it is still on disk (2,872,144 bytes, mtime Sep 9 05:43), and it still passes — `test result: ok. 7 passed; 0 failed`. Five consecutive runs finished in 1.71 / 1.72 / 1.83 / 2.12 / 1.77 s. The record's own 17.34 s for the same binary is about ten times slower than the slowest of those five, well outside this machine's known 2.4x P-core/E-core spread; I do not know what the 2026-09-09 run was competing with, so read neither figure as a measurement of the code.

ALSO VERIFIED 2026-09-14, the source form, 7 passed in 23.40 s (debug):
  cd /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work && CARGO_TARGET_DIR=/Volumes/RamBuild/par-target cargo test -p lights --features fit --test fit_parallel
The explicit CARGO_TARGET_DIR matters: without it the global `build.target-dir` would build that different source tree into the shared /Volumes/RamBuild/cargo-target and invalidate the main worktree's fingerprints.

THE WORK IS NOT IN THE REPOSITORY, which is the real reproducibility risk and is worse than "no argv was recorded". /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work is a detached-HEAD git worktree at b3d82ae4 whose lights changes are UNCOMMITTED: `git -C /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work status --porcelain` shows 8 modified files and 5 untracked ones (src/fit/sweep.rs, src/fit/sweep_rs_comment/, src/fit/harness_rs_comment/search_is_parallel_timing_is_not.md, tests/fit_parallel.rs, scripts/mutate_par.py), and `git -C /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work diff` is 1,132 lines, 8 files, +560 -317 (all measured 2026-09-14). /Volumes/RamBuild is a RAM disk: one reboot and this experiment stops being reproducible by any command at all. The one-line rescue, verified to emit those 1,132 lines, is
  git -C /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work diff > lights-parallel.patch
plus a copy of the five untracked paths.

NO SINGLE RECORDED INVOCATION EXISTS for the experiment as a whole. No file in the worktree, the artefact directories, or any commit message records the argv of any run. What is reconstructible, in descending order of confidence:

(1) The regression suite — the only reproducer that is code — is the pair of commands above (`tests/fit_parallel.rs:1` is `#![cfg(feature = "fit")]`, and `crates/lights/Cargo.toml:49-50` defines `fit = []` as non-default).

(2) The end-to-end speedup pair, RECONSTRUCTED and NOT RUNNABLE IN THIS REPOSITORY: `cargo run --release -p lights --features fit --bin lights-p2 -- --out-dir /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep/serial-1 --sweep serial` and the same with `--sweep parallel`, five repetitions each (default --max-atoms 8, sensitivity on, no --tiled-field). MEASURED 2026-09-14: `--sweep` does not exist in this repository — `grep -n "sweep" crates/lights/src/bin/lights_p2.rs` finds only the word inside the --out-dir and --blends help text, while /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work's copy of the same file carries the flag at :24 and parses it at :146. So this command runs ONLY inside par-work, i.e. only until the RAM disk is cleared. The output directories it wrote do survive: /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep/{serial,parallel}-{1..5}. The identical flag exists on that worktree's lights-p3 and lights-p4; lights-p5 does not have it at all.

(3) The profile — the "where does the time go" half: `/usr/bin/sample <pid-of-spin> ...` against a throwaway binary `spin <content-name>` that looped `nterm_curve` forever, then `python3 parse.py s_t6-glyph-like.txt`. NOT VERIFIABLE TODAY: the per-session scratchpad that held prof/src/bin/spin.rs is gone (checked 2026-09-14, no such path survives), so neither the spin binary nor its source can be produced without rewriting them.

(4) The nested / tile / crossover figures: `nestx <serial|parallel> <render_threads>` and the argument-free `grid`, `scale`, `share`, `axes`, `broad` binaries from the same vanished scratchpad. `nestx.rs:64` set `request.render_threads`, a FitRequest field that DOES NOT EXIST in `crates/lights/src/fit/greedy.rs` in either tree — so that measurement cannot be re-run without first re-applying a source edit nobody kept.

L-95, ASPECT 3 ON THE SAME SWEEP, 2026-09-28. The line below is the exact string run from the repository root (with its stdout redirected to /tmp/l95_times.txt) to produce the lights-p5 numbers:

bash -c 'CARGO_TARGET_DIR=target cargo build --release -p lights --all-features --bin lights-p5 && rm -rf /tmp/l95_rep && for rep in 1 2 3 4 5; do for mode in serial parallel; do /usr/bin/time -p target/release/lights-p5 --out-dir /tmp/l95_rep/$mode-$rep --classes t2,t3,n1,t6 --direction magnify --profile contract --no-strips --no-search-probe --sweep $mode 2>&1 >/dev/null | awk -v m=$mode -v r=$rep "/^real/{print m, r, \$2}"; done; done'

The identity half: a python comparison of every /tmp/l95_rep/serial-<rep>/ladder-magnify.tsv against parallel-<rep> with the #invocation line and the render_ns column dropped, and `cmp` on the four frozen-*.fit files, all five reps. The library half is `cargo test -p lights --all-features --test fit_parallel --test aspect3_sweep_wall`.

what it settled
  • REBUILT 2026-09-28 under L-96: sweep::map exists again, in src/fit/sweep.rs, committed, and the sweep figures are live again at 5.30x-6.29x on lights-p2 and 5.60x-5.82x on lights-p4 (medians of five reps each way, three runs) — below the lost 7.00x and 6.01x. The rebuilt serial arm is itself faster (7.11 s against the lost 10.75 s for lights-p2), so part of the fall is a cheaper serial denominator, and that is not separated here. Serial and parallel outputs are byte-identical in every file but the two wall-clock columns.
  • The unit of work worth parallelising is a whole fit and nothing smaller: sweep::map (44 lines, /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work/crates/lights/src/fit/sweep.rs) wraps one par_iter and buys 7.00x on lights-p2, 6.01x on lights-p4 and 6.59x on the 32 aspect-2 jobs, which is 75% of the 8.83x ceiling that the longest single 856 ms fit imposes.
  • Inside a fit the time is overwhelmingly the render: greedy::render_or_background is 65.15% of main-thread samples on the glyph class and 91.31% on the colour field, with render_tile self-time 25.73% / 37.05% and, on the colour field, libm exp alone at 29.22% self — re-derived by me from the two /usr/bin/sample captures on disk with the parse.py that shipped beside them.
  • The diff supports the 9,520-renders claim as a near-invariant but not as an absolute: /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep/serial-1/report.tsv's fit_renders column is exactly 9520 in 23 of the 24 atom cells across six different content classes and two blends and two profiles, 0 in the 12 analytic rows, and 3029 in exactly one cell — t1-flat-fill / gaussian-normalised / prefiltered, the only row whose improvement_exhausted is yes and whose atoms_available is 2 instead of 8.
  • The diff supports the sweep-parallelism-pays claim and the tile-parallelism-does-not claim, and it supports them with the right kind of evidence: order_is_the_whole_contract.md:30-44 states outright that tile parallelism is BIT EXACT and still must not be used, that the pin is 'a speed decision, not a correctness one, which is the only honest reason to keep it', and tests/fit_parallel.rs:533 asserts the bit-exactness rather than assuming it.
  • The three-phase rewrite of harness::run is the substance of the diff, not the one-line par_iter: target_head (heads, parallel over targets), cell_bundle (cells, parallel over ONE FLAT (target,blend,profile) list), and measure_timings (SERIAL, after every fit has finished, the only writer of render_ns_mean/render_ns_ci95, harness.rs in the worktree at the fn measure_timings block) — a 684-line change to one file, of which the concurrency is a small part.
  • The identity gate holds when I test it against the surviving artefacts rather than against the prose: serial-1 and parallel-1 of /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep agree on all 66 PNGs and on 37 of 39 report.tsv columns over 36 rows, differing only in render_ns_mean and render_ns_ci95.
  • The per-render rayon ThreadPoolBuilder is real, is in fn render itself (crates/lights/src/method/splat/implementation.rs, the else arm after the threads==1 and threads==0 branches), and is byte-identical in both trees — diff between /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work/crates/lights/src/method/splat/implementation.rs and the main tree's produces no output, so this parallelism work never touched it.
  • It is latent for exactly one reason: settings.threads = 1 in greedy::fit (worktree greedy.rs:433, main tree greedy.rs:471) overwrites the caller's choice and takes the serial branch, so the explicit-N branch that builds a pool is never reached from a fit — and the test that claims to guard that line (tests/fit_parallel.rs:489) asserts only that the returned ATOM BITS are equal at threads 1, 0 and 8, which its own sibling at :533 proves is true even with the pin removed.
  • The three serial optimisations were identified and NONE was implemented in either tree: the profile I re-derived puts framebuffer allocation and resolution at Framebuffer::new 3.90%/2.26% plus resolve_float 11.36%/11.34% inclusive, residual_energy at 9.75%/8.31% inclusive with its difference collect inlined, and mach_absolute_time — Instant::now's landing point on macOS — at 1.90% and 1.15% self.
  • The --sweep serial|parallel flag was added to lights-p2, lights-p3 and lights-p4 and to nothing else; lights-p5 (aspect 3) did not exist when this worktree was written and is absent from it entirely, which is why the aspect-3 ladder still runs a fresh whole fit per rung on one core (BURNDOWN.toml records PID 48395 at 21m32s wall for 6m59s of CPU on an 18-core machine).
  • The worktree is now BEHIND the main tree: both were at b3d82ae4 when L-96 was written, but the main tree has advanced to ef4e6f8a with aspect 3 committed. Of the files that commit range touched under crates/lights (mod.rs, greedy.rs, edge.rs and new files), only src/fit/mod.rs intersects the worktree's eight modified files, so L-96's landing recipe — copy seven files straight over, git merge-file mod.rs — still applies.
  • L-95, 2026-09-28: aspect 3 is on the sweep. lights-p5 fits every (class, profile, frozen-or-rung) job as one flat list and assembles serially; 3.01x on the medians (2.82x-3.32x) for a 4-class magnify run, byte-identical outputs but the render_ns column. That is well under the 5.3x-6.3x of lights-p2 because the ladder is dominated by a few very large fits: the 8x rung renders at 64x the pixels of the 1x rung, so each class carries one job far heavier than its others and four of them bound the critical path. A refitted row's render_ns no longer includes its fit (header line render_ns_convention).
what it refuted
  • 'A fit does exactly 9,520 renders REGARDLESS OF CONTENT' — refuted in its universal form by the artefact it came from. /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep/serial-1/report.tsv gives 9520 for 23 of 24 atom cells and 3029 for t1-flat-fill / gaussian-normalised / prefiltered, the one cell that exhausted improvement after 2 atoms. The number is a property of running the full 8-atom budget, not of the fitter.
  • 'Parallelising the job sweep is worth 7.84x' — refuted. grep -rn &#x27;7\.84&#x27; over /Volumes/RamBuild/par-work finds one hit and it is a Gaussian cutoff radius in src/kernel/supergaussian_rs_comment/shape.md:28. The recorded speedups are 7.00x, 6.01x and 6.59x.
  • 'Tile parallelism inside a fit is a 2-11x slowdown below about 1024x1024' — refuted on both halves. The record says 0.23x-0.58x of serial, which it itself converts to 'a 1.7x to 4.3x slowdown' (order_is_the_whole_contract.md:36-37), and it puts the crossover near 128x128 for the colour field and near 512x512 for the glyph (:39-41). The strings '1024' and '2048' do not occur anywhere in the parallelism prose.
  • 'End-to-end 5.7-7.0x' — refuted at the low end. The three recorded figures are 6.01x, 6.59x and 7.00x, i.e. 6.0-7.0x. BURNDOWN.toml L-64 already caught this and its correction stands.
  • 'at 1.5-1.8x more CPU' — refuted as a description of the sweep. The only CPU-multiplier in the entire record is '68 percent more CPU burned to finish 14 percent later' (order_is_the_whole_contract.md:42-43 and tests/fit_parallel.rs:525), and it describes tile parallelism NESTED inside a parallel sweep — the configuration the work concluded against — not the plain sweep it recommends.
  • REFUTED BY ITS OWN AUTHORS, in the harness comment doc against the harness's own output: search_is_parallel_timing_is_not.md:34-36 claims 'a serial and a parallel lights-p2 run write byte-identical sweep.tsv, sensitivity.tsv, probes.tsv, bins.tsv'. I ran cmp: all four DIFFER, at line 8, because the same commit added a search_phase header line that prints 'ran serial over 1 worker(s)' vs 'ran parallel over 18 worker(s)'. The DATA rows are byte-identical; the FILES are not. The doc's own honesty feature falsified its own sentence.
  • REFUTED BY A LATER READER, and I confirm the refutation with a different instrument: BURNDOWN.toml L-34 kills the premise that lights-p2's published timings were single-threaded. Header.threads is a hardcoded literal 1, while every timed render inherited threads: 0 (= every core) from SharedSettings::reference(). docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:4 repeats 'single-threaded' in prose. The worktree fixes the measurement but leaves the literal, so /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep/serial-1/out.txt now prints 'threads 1' on one line and 'renderer threads 0 (0 means every core)' on the next.
  • REFUTED BY A LATER READER: BURNDOWN.toml L-36 shows that the_fitter_ignores_the_callers_renderer_thread_count_... (tests/fit_parallel.rs:489) CANNOT FAIL on the mutation its own panic message names. Delete settings.threads = 1 from greedy.rs and the fit at threads 0 or 8 returns the same atoms — because the sibling test at :533 establishes that threaded renders are bit-exact on exactly the content (T6) and kernel (Gaussian) the guard uses. Both tests pass today; one of them is measuring nothing.
  • I REFUTE ONE OF THE REGISTER'S OWN CORRECTIONS. BURNDOWN.toml L-64 states that '9,520' has 'no hit in either tree' and that '1.2-1.9' has exactly one irrelevant hit. Both greps were scoped to --include='*.rs' --include='*.md' --include='*.py'. 9520 is sitting in the fit_renders column of every surviving report.tsv under /Volumes/RamBuild/p2rep and /Volumes/RamBuild/rep0, and 1.15% / 1.90% are the mach_absolute_time self-times I re-derived from /Volumes/RamBuild/par-target-prof/s_n1-colour-field.txt and s_t6-glyph-like.txt. Two of the five 'unsourced' figures have sources; they are .tsv and .txt artefacts on the RAM disk, which the grep never looked at.
  • 'The measurement instruments are gone' — refuted. Nine throwaway probe binaries survive in source at <session scratchpad>/959574de-b7ef-49fa-8b42-9a8e0e211b3c/scratchpad/prof/src/bin/ (spin, drift, sweep, share, grid, scale, axes, nestx, broad), together with their Cargo.toml pointing at the worktree crate. What is gone is their OUTPUT, not the instruments.
what it does NOT settle
  • Not re-measured on 2026-09-28: the 8.83x critical-path ceiling (it needs the per-job times, which the rebuilt sweep does not print), the flat-versus-nested topology pair, the tile-parallelism slowdown band and the profile shares. Those figures stay the dead worktree's until someone re-runs them. --sweep on lights-p5 was built under L-95 the same day; see its numbers.
  • The raw stdout of every probe binary was never written to disk. I searched <session scratchpad>/.../scratchpad and the RAM disk for 'render_share', 'pool-per-render', 'sweep serial=' and 't0_over_t1' and found nothing; the session transcript (959574de-b7ef-49fa-8b42-9a8e0e211b3c.jsonl, 4,062 lines) has been compacted and contains none of them either. Every speedup, crossover and spread figure in the two comment docs is therefore a prose summary with no surviving primary record.
  • The framebuffer-reuse figure '17-27% of a fit' cannot be checked because its denominator and its bucket are undefined. The profile I re-derived gives Framebuffer::new + resolve_float = 15.26% (t6) and 13.60% (n1) inclusive of the main thread; adding memset/bzero and the Vec collect reaches roughly 24% and 16%; renormalising to greedy::fit's own inclusive time moves it again. The band is in the right region for one plausible bucketing and no file says which one was used.
  • The residual_energy fusion figure '~4%' is likewise unrecorded. residual_energy is 9.75% (t6) and 8.31% (n1) inclusive; how much of that a fused single pass would recover was never measured, and BURNDOWN.toml L-97 warns that reassociating the float sum would move every published residual_energy figure including the 0.09603 / 0.18628 pair in greedy_rs_comment/the_search_is_gaussian_tuned.md:62.
  • Timer cost is attributed, not isolated. mach_absolute_time self-time (1.90% / 1.15%) covers ALL of the splat renderer's phase timers — Instant::now at implementation.rs:115, :129, :161 and elapsed at :122, :149, :232 — not the single per-render pair the claim names. Removing one Instant::now would recover a fraction of that, unmeasured.
  • The '2.4x spread as work moves between P and E cores' has no surviving measurement and no causal evidence. The instrument written for it (drift.rs, 14 repetitions of nterm_curve on n1-colour-field) produced no saved output. What I CAN show from the artefacts is a run-to-run spread of the same cell's render_ns_mean of up to 2.86x across the five p2rep repetitions and 2.58x between the two rep0 runs — but those renders were taken at timing_threads = 0, i.e. on every core, so they do not establish a single-thread P/E story; they establish only that the machine's timing is unreliable to that factor.
  • Not one mutation was added for any of the seven new tests (BURNDOWN.toml L-66). Only the_bit_comparator_fails_on_a_single_flipped_bit_of_a_single_atom has ever been shown able to fail, and it breaks the comparator rather than the subject. Nothing proves that replacing sweep.rs:37's par_iter().map(&amp;f).collect() with a for_each pushing into a shared Vec — the exact mistake the module exists to make unavailable — would be caught.
  • The thread-pin guard test cannot fail (L-36), so 'the fitter ignores the caller's thread count' is an unverified property despite having a green test named after it.
  • The per-render ThreadPoolBuilder is UNFIXED in both trees (L-35). Anyone who removes the pin to try nested parallelism, or who calls render_or_background with an explicit N from a new harness — aspect 3's ladder is the obvious candidate — pays thousands of pool constructions and will read the resulting slowdown as evidence against tile parallelism rather than against that one line.
  • The nested-parallelism measurement is not re-runnable as the tree stands: nestx.rs:64 sets request.render_threads, and FitRequest (greedy.rs:54-63) has no such field in either tree. That figure was taken against a source state nobody kept.
  • The tile-parallelism crossover was probed on 2 of 8 content classes (T6 glyph-like and N1 colour-field, grid.rs:37 for index in [3usize, 4usize]) and one kernel (KernelFamily::Gaussian, grid.rs:35). Generalising '128x128 for the colour field, 512x512 for the glyph' to other content or other kernels is not supported.
  • All figures are from one machine: macOS 26.5 aarch64, 18 cores as 6 performance + 12 efficiency, release, f64, tile 16, and the report header records 18 rayon workers. Nothing here transfers to another machine, and per the project's own convention any figure measured before Aug 2026 belongs to a different machine entirely.
  • The whole body of work is UNCOMMITTED working-tree state in a worktree on a RAM disk, one git clean or one reboot from gone: 8 modified files and 5 untracked paths at base commit b3d82ae4, while the main tree has since advanced to ef4e6f8a.
  • The p2rep and rep0 runs used the DEFAULT --max-atoms 8, not the --max-atoms 48 that docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:4 records for the published P2 figures, so the 9,520 count is a property of the 8-atom budget and does not describe the published run.
  • Undocumented behaviour observed in passing and not written down anywhere: --repeats 0 produces NaN in render_ns_mean and render_ns_ci95 for every row (/Volumes/RamBuild/rep0/r0-1/report.tsv), while leaving all 37 other columns byte-identical to a --repeats 5 run. The usage block does not mention it.
  • The worktree's scripts/mutate_par.py is the STALE copy (5,879 bytes, git-based, refuses uncommitted files) against the main tree's newer one (snapshot-based, per-mutation features, committed in the b3d82ae4..ef4e6f8a range). It must be excluded from any landing, as BURNDOWN.toml already rules.
  • L-95: the 21-minute lights-p5 --classes t2,t3,n1,t6 --direction both --profile both run that motivated the entry was NOT re-timed, in either mode — five reps each way would cost hours. The measured run is its magnify/contract quarter. The aspect-3 critical path (the longest single fit, presumably an 8x-rung refit) was not measured, so how far 3.01x sits from its ceiling is unknown; neither was the share of the serial prepare and assembly phases. The --search-probe side fits in lights-p5 are still serial and were excluded by --no-search-probe.

Does one fresh Claude Code session per task cost less than resuming one session, or does the per-session cache-creation floor outweigh the context accumulation it avoids?

engineering2026-09-15copy-pasteable command3 refuted
the answer

NO, AND IT IS THE OPPOSITE AT THIS SCALE. Resuming one session across five read-only tasks cost $0.458 against $1.209 for a fresh session per task - isolation was 2.64x MORE EXPENSIVE, and also 31% slower. The mechanism is a ~40,000-token cache-creation floor paid at every session start: isolated wrote 217,505 cache-creation tokens against persistent's 16,166, a 92.6% difference, while the accumulation isolation exists to avoid showed up as only 416,364 extra cache READS - and a cache read is roughly an order of magnitude cheaper per token than a cache write. Isolation buys a smaller context and pays for it in re-priming.

the question it was built to answer

Does giving every task its own fresh Claude Code session reduce token consumption and cost, as claimed, or does the per-session fixed overhead outweigh the context accumulation it avoids?

criterion, and its convention

Reported cost in USD from claude -p --output-format json, which is the figure Claude Code itself bills; token columns are diagnostic. Both phases ran the identical five-task sequence, the same fixed model (sonnet), the same permission mode, in the same working tree. The persistent phase is verified to have actually resumed ONE session: all five tasks report session_id 392d8495. Turn counts match between phases per task (2/2/11/4/4 isolated against 2/2/11/5/4 persistent), so the two phases did comparable work rather than one phase quietly doing less.

reproduce it
python3 scripts/claude_task_host.py verify crates/lights/scripts/burndown_tasks.json --model sonnet --permission-mode acceptEdits --unset-env ANTHROPIC_API_KEY --json-out docs/lights/benchmarks/session_isolation_verify.json

RUN 2026-09-15 from the repository root, exit 0, and the full per-task JSON is committed at docs/lights/benchmarks/session_isolation_verify.json.

--unset-env ANTHROPIC_API_KEY IS LOAD-BEARING AND WAS FOUND THE HARD WAY. ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set in this shell and scripts/claude_task_host.py copies the environment to the child, so the first smoke run billed every spawned session to an API account and returned `Credit balance is too low` with ok=False and zero usage on every task. The flag was added to the host for this. Without it the benchmark reports a clean-looking run of five failures.

The five tasks are READ-ONLY on purpose (crates/lights/scripts/burndown_tasks.json): `verify` runs the same sequence twice, so a task that writes makes phase 2 start from a tree phase 1 already changed and the comparison measures the edit rather than the session boundary.
what it measured
measurementvaluewhere it comes from
reported cost, five tasks, fresh session each$1.209183Sum of total_cost_usd over the five isolated results. Per task: 0.183284, 0.186537, 0.386831, 0.228439, 0.224091.
reported cost, five tasks, one resumed session$0.458424Per task: 0.027885, 0.035781, 0.207930, 0.097268, 0.089560. The first task in the persistent phase still pays a full session start; the saving is entirely in tasks 2-5.
cost of isolation over persistence2.64x more expensive (+$0.750759, i.e. persistent is -62.1%)Divided over the four avoided session starts, one session start costs $0.188.
cache-creation tokens, isolated against persistent217,505 against 16,166 (-92.6%)THE MECHANISM. Isolated averages 43,501 cache-creation tokens per session; persistent's marginal average after the first is 3,233. The floor is the system prompt plus CLAUDE.md plus the MCP tool surface, re-written at every session start.
cache-read tokens, isolated against persistent1,305,155 against 1,721,519 (+31.9%)The accumulation effect is REAL and measurable - the persistent session re-reads a growing conversation. It is simply much cheaper per token than re-writing the cache, so it loses to the floor at this scale.
output tokens, isolated against persistent7,804 against 4,936 (-36.8% for persistent)The resumed session answered in fewer tokens because earlier tasks had already established the context; that is a second, smaller saving in the same direction.
wall clock, five tasks116.8 s isolated against 80.9 s persistent (-30.8%)Sum of elapsed_seconds. Session start is not free in time either.
the fixed floor, measured on a no-op43,717 cache-creation + 18,534 cache-read tokens, $0.1786, for a prompt of 2 input tokens and an answer of 4claude -p &#x27;Reply with exactly: OK&#x27; --model sonnet --output-format json. 0.01% of the billed tokens were the task. This is what every session start costs before any work happens.
the same floor inside a real one-task session126,259 billed tokens of which 117 were the task (0.09%)The smoke run of burndown.py --check: 4 input + 113 output against 39,225 cache-creation + 86,917 cache-read.
what it settled
  • ISOLATION LOST, 2.64x. Five read-only tasks cost $1.209183 with a fresh session each and $0.458424 resuming one session. It was also 30.8% slower in wall clock. The direction is the opposite of the claim the experiment was built to test.
  • THE MECHANISM IS A CACHE-CREATION FLOOR, NOT ACCUMULATION. Every session start re-writes roughly 40,000 cache-creation tokens - system prompt, CLAUDE.md, the MCP tool surface - before any work happens. Isolated wrote 217,505 of them; persistent wrote 16,166. Divided over the four avoided starts, one session start costs $0.188.
  • THE ACCUMULATION EFFECT IS REAL AND IS THE SMALLER TERM. The resumed session did re-read a growing conversation: 1,721,519 cache reads against 1,305,155, up 31.9%. A cache read is roughly an order of magnitude cheaper per token than a cache write, so the growing side lost to the constant side at this scale.
  • A SESSION START IS ALMOST ENTIRELY OVERHEAD. claude -p &#x27;Reply with exactly: OK&#x27; billed 43,717 cache-creation and 18,534 cache-read tokens and $0.1786 for a two-token prompt and a four-token answer. In the smallest real task measured here, 117 of 126,259 billed tokens were the work - 0.09%.
  • THE BENCHMARK'S FIRST RUN MEASURED NOTHING AND LOOKED FINE. ANTHROPIC_API_KEY is set in this shell and the host copied the environment to the child, so all five tasks returned Credit balance is too low with ok=False and zero usage. A reader skimming the aggregate would have seen a $0.00 run. The host grew an explicit --unset-env flag; check authentication before trusting any figure from a spawned session.
  • PERSISTENCE ALSO ANSWERED IN FEWER WORDS: 4,936 output tokens against 7,804, down 36.8%, because earlier tasks had already established what later ones needed.
what it refuted
  • THE CENTRAL CLAIM THIS EXPERIMENT WAS BUILT TO TEST - that one task per fresh session reduces consumption - is refuted for this workload, in the measured direction and by a wide margin: 2.64x more expensive and 31% slower. The claim is not absurd; it is simply outweighed here by a per-session cache-creation floor the claim does not account for.
  • The intuition that 'context accumulation' is the dominant cost. It is a real effect and it is visible - persistent read 31.9% more cached tokens - but it is the SMALLER term. The larger term is re-priming a fresh context, and it is paid in cache WRITES, which are the expensive kind.
  • The idea that a benchmark like this can be run without checking authentication first. The first run produced five clean-looking failures with zero usage because the child inherited ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
what it does NOT settle
  • WHERE THE CROSSOVER IS. Isolation must eventually win: cache-read cost grows with accumulated context while the session-start floor is constant. These five tasks peaked at 770,840 cache reads in one task and persistence still won by 62%, so the crossover is above that - but it was not measured and is not estimated here. A ten-entry burn-down context is far heavier than five read-only lookups and could plausibly sit on the other side.
  • WHETHER THIS TRANSFERS TO THE WRITE-HEAVY LOOP. The five tasks are short reads. The real loop edits source, runs cargo, commits and redeploys, which produces far more tool output per entry. Answering that needs a disposable git worktree per phase, which this run did not have.
  • MODEL DEPENDENCE. Everything here is sonnet. The ratio of cache-write to cache-read price is what decides the direction, and it is not necessarily the same across models.
  • STATISTICAL WEIGHT. n=1 run of 5 tasks. The direction is large enough (2.64x) that noise is unlikely to explain it, but no repetition was done and no spread is reported. This machine has a measured 2.4x wall-clock spread over identical calls, so the -30.8% time figure in particular should not be quoted as precise.
  • HOW MUCH OF THE 40k FLOOR IS THE MCP SURFACE. Two MCP servers are configured for this repository and nothing in it uses either (L-137). Their tool names are in the system prompt of every session and therefore in every one of those cache-creation tokens, but the experiment did not run a with/without comparison to size that share.

Is the ray caster's density gradient at the closest approach a usable normal — can it light a sphere with no normal field on the atom?

engineering2026-09-15copy-pasteable command2 refuted
the answer

Yes for a side light, and it is the SILHOUETTE normal, not a surface normal: under a light from +x with ambient 0.2 and diffuse 0.8 the red sphere's lit half is 3.5426x its dark half, within 0.11% of the 3.5465 that a normal radial in the image plane predicts (2/pi over a half disc) and far from the 2.70 a front-facing sphere normal would give; a light at the camera keeps only 0.2556 of the flat luminance (ambient is 0.2) because the closest-approach vector is perpendicular to every ray. No field was added to Atom. The splat path refuses a light.

the question it was built to answer

BURNDOWN L-122 asks whether an atom needs a normal before a 3D scene can be lit. The ray caster already stands at each ray's closest approach x* = o + t* d to each atom and carries Lambda = Sigma^-1, so Lambda (x* - mu) is the outward normal of the level set through x* at zero cost. Is that normal usable — does a Lambert term built from it light a one-atom sphere on one side and not the other — and what does it get wrong?

criterion, and its convention

Written before the run, in BURNDOWN L-141. Scene: the red sphere of scene::demo::SPHERE_SCENE alone (centre (0,0,3) in the lights frame, radius 0.5, sigma = radius/3, opacity 0.9), through the ray caster under SharedSettings::contract with a black background, sphere_camera at 640x360, f64, gaussian kernel. Mask: pixels the flat render leaves above zero. Sides: screen x below and above (w-1)/2. Number: mean luminance (0.2126 R + 0.7152 G + 0.0722 B) of the lit-side pixels over the dark-side pixels, the lit side being the screen half where a probe atom displaced to +x lands. FLAT must give 1.000 within 1e-9 (the sphere is symmetric; this guards that the mask measures the sphere). LIT must exceed 2.0 under ambient 0.2 and diffuse 0.8. Changed before any lit render was made: the scene is the red sphere ALONE rather than demo::spheres, because in the full scene four nearer spheres overlap the red one's footprint. Added after the first number and before the second run: the ratio must match the silhouette-normal prediction (0.2 + 0.8 * 2/pi) / 0.2 = 3.5465 within 2%, and a light at the camera must keep between 0.2 and 0.30 of the flat luminance.

reproduce it
cargo test -p lights --all-features --test shading -- --nocapture

Prints two lines: `shading: flat right/left 1.000000000000; lit +x: lit-half mean 0.042610 over dark-half mean 0.012028 = 3.542557; pixels left 1772 right 1772; plus-x is screen side 1` and `shading: headlight keeps 0.255577 of the flat luminance (ambient is 0.2)`. Run 2026-09-15 on the M5 Max, 6 passed in 0.07 s; the numbers are deterministic, not timings. The panels: `cargo run -p lights --all-features --bin lights-demo -- --out-dir <DIR> --width 640 --height 360 --frames 32 --reps 5`, group `shading`, ids shading-flat and shading-lit (the demo object, not the sphere scene: the layering guard forbids the demo binary from reaching the path-traced reference scene).
what it measured
measurementvaluewhere it comes from
flat render, right/left luminance ratio of the red sphere1.000000000000 (1772 pixels each side)tests/shading.rs::a_light_from_plus_x_lights_the_plus_x_half_of_the_red_sphere_and_the_flat_render_is_symmetric, printed under --nocapture. Exact symmetry: pixel x and w-1-x see mirror-image rays and every term is even in x.
light from +x, lit-half mean over dark-half mean0.042610 / 0.012028 = 3.542557Same test. Criterion floor 2.0; cleared. +x is screen side 1 (right), established by rendering a probe atom at x = +0.6 and seeing which half holds it, not assumed from the camera convention.
silhouette-normal prediction for that ratio(0.2 + 0.8 * 2/pi) / 0.2 = 3.5465; measured is 0.11% below itThe closest-approach vector x* - mu is perpendicular to the ray, so for a camera on the axis the normal is radial in the image plane and n.l = cos(theta); max(0, cos theta) averages 2/pi over the lit half disc. A front-facing sphere normal would average 4/(3 pi) = 0.424 and give 2.70. tests/shading.rs::the_gradient_normal_is_the_silhouette_normal_... asserts the 2% band.
light at the camera (headlight), lit luminance as a fraction of flat0.255577 (ambient 0.2, ceiling 0.30)Same test. A true sphere under a headlight keeps about 0.73 of flat (brightest at the centre); the gradient normal keeps a faint rim only, because n_z = -|u|/sqrt(9 + u^2) is small for every ray. This is the limitation, and it is structural: the normal cannot have a component along the ray.
mirror symmetry under a light flipped to -xright/left under +x equals left/right under -x within 1e-9 relativetests/shading.rs::flipping_the_light_to_minus_x_mirrors_the_render_exactly; the same test asserts the ratio is not 1, so it cannot pass by both renders being flat.
mutations, both CAUGHT through all four gates2 of 2, 50.6 s on 2 workers, dev profilescripts/mutations.json rows 344-345: the normal taken at the ray origin (constant across an atom; ratio collapses to 1) and the back-hemisphere clamp dropped (a pixel goes negative). MUT_ONLY on the two test names with MUT_MERGE=1, merged into docs/lights/mutations/results.tsv.
demo panel cost at 640x360, ray caster, the demo objectshading-flat 15.693 ms min; shading-lit 17.253 ms min (spread 2.5 and 4.9 ms over 5 reps)lights-demo run 2026-09-15, 18 threads. Five reps with the spread reported per the measurement rule; the 1.6 ms difference is inside the spread and is NOT a measured cost of the Lambert term.
what it settled
  • THE GRADIENT NORMAL IS THE SILHOUETTE NORMAL. Lambda (x* - mu) at the closest approach is perpendicular to the ray by construction, so it is the normal of the level set where the ray grazes it — radial in the image plane for a camera on the axis — and never a normal that faces the camera. The measured 3.5426 against a predicted 3.5465 confirms the model to 0.11%.
  • A SIDE LIGHT WORKS AND A HEADLIGHT DOES NOT. Lit/dark 3.54x from the side; 0.256 of flat from the camera, where a real sphere would keep 0.73. A game whose light is not at the camera gets shading for free; one whose light is at the camera gets a rim and nothing else.
  • NO FIELD ON ATOM WAS NEEDED. The normal is a property of Sigma at the sample point. L-122's premise — an atom carries no normal — is true of the struct and false of the ray caster.
  • THE SPLAT PATH CANNOT DO THIS AND SAYS SO. Its conic is 2-D and it has no closest-approach point; SplatRaster::new returns LightsError::LightingUnsupported, and assert_contract refuses any settings carrying a light (ContractViolation::LightPresent) rather than comparing under it. Shading is a ray-caster-only capability and the champion decision has to weigh that.
  • THE FLAT RENDER IS EXACTLY SYMMETRIC, to twelve printed digits, which is the guard that the mask measures the sphere and not the background or a ground atom.
what it refuted
  • L-122's prescribed experiment: 'the largest-eigenvector direction of Sigma is the nearest thing to a normal an atom has' — a surfel's normal is its smallest axis, and no per-atom axis can light one side of a one-atom sphere; the per-pixel gradient can.
  • The naive expectation written into L-141's own criterion that an ideal Lambert hemisphere pair gives 3.0 — that assumed a surface normal; the silhouette normal gives 3.5465 and the measurement sided with the silhouette.
what it does NOT settle
  • Material and emission — nothing here touches them; they stay owed on L-122.
  • Whether a game target can live with a normal that cannot face the camera, or needs a second primitive that carries one — a PM scope call, recorded on L-122.
  • The cost of the Lambert term: the demo's 1.6 ms panel difference is inside the run spread; lights-bench has no shading column and none was added.
  • Shadows, multiple lights, specular terms — none built; a directional light is the smallest experiment that answers the normal question.
artefacts on disk

Does a surfel that stores its normal light a sphere the way a surface does, where the gradient normal could only light a silhouette?

engineering2026-09-28copy-pasteable command1 refuted
the answer

Yes. A camera-facing surfel under a headlight keeps 1.000000000000 of its flat luminance (the gradient normal kept 0.255577); a tilted surfel is lit by 0.2+0.8cos(theta) to 1e-12; and on an opaque 900-surfel sphere per-pixel lit/flat matches the ideal Lambert sphere with mean absolute error 0.0208 under a side light and 0.0074 under a headlight. The half-mean side ratio pre-registered at 2.2-3.2 read 2.155 and is kept as MISSED: the instrument weights the soft limb by its coverage.

the question it was built to answer

BURNDOWN L-122, ruled (B) by the PM on 2026-09-16: add a surfel primitive that carries a stored normal, lit per pixel by the same directional term. L-141 had shown the ray caster's free gradient normal is the silhouette normal and fails a headlight. Does a stored normal fix that, and does a shell of surfels shade like a sphere?

criterion, and its convention

Written before each run. Camera look_at eye (0,0,-5), target origin, up (0,-1,0), 30 degree fov, 160x160, f64; ambient 0.2, diffuse 0.8; light directions derived from the camera's own axes so image-right is +x by construction. (1) A facing surfel under a light along its normal: lit/flat at the centre pixel equals 1 within 1e-12. (2) Tilt theta: lit/flat equals 0.2+0.8cos(theta) within 1e-12. (3) Sphere of 900 Fibonacci surfels, radius 1: headlight keep in (0.60, 0.85) around the ideal 0.733; side-light half-mean ratio (luminance = mean of RGB over non-black pixels, split at x = w/2) in (2.2, 3.2) around the front-facing 2.70 and away from the silhouette 3.55 - THIS ONE WAS MISSED. (4) Written after (3) missed and before its own run: over pixels with coverage >= 0.99, per-pixel lit/flat against 0.2+0.8max(0, n.l) of the analytic sphere hit, mean absolute error below 0.05 for both lights. Precondition added after the first shell run: interior coverage (60x60 centre block) above 0.99, or the test refuses to measure.

reproduce it
cargo test -p lights --all-features --test surfel -- --nocapture

Prints `facing surfel under a headlight keeps 1.000000000000 of flat`, three `tilt ... lit/flat ... predicted ...` lines, `surfel sphere, 900 surfels: flat right/left 0.999993; headlight keeps 0.795144 of flat ...; +x light lit/dark 2.155239 ...; pixels left 6068 right 6068`, `interior coverage 0.997498` and `per-pixel lit/flat against the ideal sphere: side light mean abs error 0.020805 over 11672 px, headlight 0.007417 over 11672 px`. Run 2026-09-28 on the M5 Max, 7 passed in 3.2 s; every number is deterministic, none is a timing. The panels: `cargo run -p lights --all-features --bin lights-demo -- --out-dir <DIR> --width 640 --height 360 --frames 32 --reps 5`, group `surfel`.

what it settled
  • A STORED NORMAL SERVES THE HEADLIGHT THE GRADIENT NORMAL COULD NOT: 1.000 against 0.256 on the same question, exactly.
  • A TWO-SIDED SURFEL SHELL MUST BE OPAQUE TO READ AS A SPHERE. At 0.68-0.96 coverage the back hemisphere, lit with its normals turned toward the viewer, dragged the side ratio from about 2.2 to 1.30. Open surfaces need two-sidedness; closed shells would want back-face culling, which is not built.
  • THE HALF-MEAN INSTRUMENT MEASURES COVERAGE AS WELL AS SHADING. L-141 used it on one atom where the two coincide; on a shell whose limb is partially covered it under-reads the lit side. Per-pixel lit/flat divides the coverage out and is the instrument that measures the quantity named.
what it refuted
  • The pre-registered 2.2-3.2 band for the side-light half ratio of a surfel sphere - as an instrument of shading, not as a claim about the surfel: the per-pixel error of 0.021 says the shading is the sphere's.
what it does NOT settle
  • Material and emission - split to L-154.
  • Cost: the surfel pass ray-casts every disc at every pixel with no binning; no timing was taken and none is published.
  • Interpenetration with atoms: composite_over carries no depth; L-153 measures whether that matters.
  • Faceting: the shell is visibly faceted at surfel spacing in the demo; no measurement of how the per-pixel error falls with surfel count.

Can a central-difference gradient of the fit loss be trusted at this crate's curvature, and which step size does the sweep choose?

engineering2026-09-15copy-pasteable command3 refuted
the answer

Yes, at eps = 1e-6, and the plateau was MEASURED rather than assumed: |g| agrees to seven digits across 1e-5/1e-6/1e-7 and the directional-derivative identity holds to 8.2e-10 of |g| there. The module's first choice, 1e-4, was REFUSED by its own sweep. The failure modes are not the textbook two: besides curvature at large eps and cancellation below 1e-8 there is a third, and it is NOT monotone in eps. A support-edge straddle made 1e-4 (identity error 8.3e-3) worse than both its neighbours (3.6e-5 at 1e-3, 9.2e-9 at 1e-5), through the angle partial alone. The vector is also usable: from the point where the coordinate ladder is stuck on t5-thin-stroke it still descends, by 1.004e-2 relative in 20 steps, so the mechanism L-131 named is real in sign and small on this target.

the question it was built to answer

BURNDOWN L-131. The crate's only search is a coordinate ladder (greedy::refine) that accepts a step only when ONE knob lowers the energy on its own, and P2_RESULTS.md:124 reports 'in 22 of 24 cells the fitter stopped, not the budget' (L-131's own why still quotes the withdrawn 20; the document was corrected to 22 on 2026-09-15 and this record follows the document). Before any optimiser is written: can the nine per-parameter partials of the loss be assembled into one vector by finite differences and trusted? Too large a step measures curvature, too small measures floating-point cancellation, and both produce a plausible-looking vector pointing somewhere else. So: which step, chosen from a measured table rather than guessed, and is the resulting direction one the ladder's stuck point actually has?

criterion, and its convention

Written into L-131 before the module existed, as two checks that can fail plus one discriminating case. (a) THE IDENTITY: for a unit direction d with every component non-zero, (loss(x + eps d) - loss(x - eps d)) / (2 eps) must agree with g . d to a stated tolerance, 1e-3 of |g| here, which catches a wrong sign, a wrong scaling and a missing partial at once. (b) THE STEP SWEEP: the gradient is taken at ten decades, 1e-1 to 1e-10, on one real atom, and the chosen step must be a row of that table whose change from its neighbour is below 1e-6 relative while BOTH ends of the sweep change more, so the flat middle is measured and not assumed. (c) THE DISCRIMINATING CASE: converge the ladder on Target::t5_thin_stroke until an extra sweep returns the energy bit-identical, then require that a line search along the projected gradient still lowers it, and that 20 steps of steepest descent gain more than 1e-3 relative. Viewport 64x64 from t5_thin_stroke, gaussian kernel, SharedSettings::prefiltered, tile 16, truth from exact_coverage at quadrature depth 10.

reproduce it
cargo test -p lights --all-features --test fit_descent -- --test-threads 1 --nocapture

Run 2026-09-15 on the M5 Max: 6 passed, 0 failed, finished in 1.05 s. Every figure below is printed by that one invocation and is deterministic (render counts and residual energies, not timings). The sweep table comes from the_step_sweep_measures_a_flat_middle_and_the_chosen_step_names_its_row; the stuck-ladder figures from the_ladder_stops_where_the_descent_direction_still_lowers_the_energy; the identity errors from the_directional_derivative_identity_holds_at_the_chosen_step.
what it measured
measurementvaluewhere it comes from
the flat middle: gradient norm across the sweep4.025793e1 at 1e-5, 1e-6 and 1e-7, identical to seven digits; 3.232351e0 at 1e-1 and 4.025794e1 at 1e-10Sweep table, column |g|, on the one-atom fit of t5-thin-stroke. The 1e-1 row is a factor of 12 low because at that step the angle probe swings the 15 px atom right off its own support: its angle partial is -2.046 against the plateau's -40.18.
the chosen step names its roweps = 1e-6, change from the decade above 3.394e-8, the smallest in the table; 1e-2 changes by 9.453e-1 and 1e-10 by 7.121e-6Sweep table, column change_vs_prev. CHOSEN_STEP in src/fit/descent.rs is 1e-6 and tests/fit_descent.rs asserts that row is flatter than both ends, so the constant cannot drift away from the measurement silently.
identity error at the chosen step8.175e-10 of |g| on the fitted atom (|g| = 4.025793e1); 2.301e-10 on an independent interior atom (|g| = 4.917810e1); tolerance 1e-3the_directional_derivative_identity_holds_at_the_chosen_step. The same test first asserts every one of the nine partials is non-zero on the interior atom, so a gradient that skipped a parameter could not pass by cancellation.
THE THIRD FAILURE MODE: a support-edge straddle at 1e-4, non-monotone in epsidentity error 8.297e-3 at 1e-4, against 3.642e-5 at 1e-3 and 9.224e-9 at 1e-5: worse than BOTH neighboursSweep table. The per-parameter rows localise it to one partial: angle is -4.04017e1 at 1e-4 against -4.01766e1 on the plateau, while the other eight equal the plateau to six digits. The fitted atom is 15 px long and a third of a pixel thin (log-scales 2.728975 and -1.117325), so 1e-4 rad tilts its tips by 4.5e-3 px and one probe crossed a pixel centre through the splat's hard alpha-cutoff support edge.
the cancellation sidechange_vs_prev 1.164e-7 at 1e-8, 6.856e-7 at 1e-9, 7.121e-6 at 1e-10Sweep table. The per-parameter rows show it arriving in the smallest partials first: the three colour partials sit at -9.16955e-3 through the plateau and split to -9.04166e-3 and -9.07718e-3 at 1e-10, while centre.x still holds five digits.
cost of one gradient18 renders, exactly 2 per parameter; the ten-decade sweep costs 200a_gradient_costs_eighteen_renders_and_bills_its_own_site asserts budget.used() rises by RENDERS_PER_GRADIENT = 2 * P and that the site string is fit::descent::gradient, so gradient renders are never attributed to fit::greedy::fit.
the ladder's stuck point, and that the gradient is not zero thereenergy 23.072884 after 5 extra full sweeps that changed nothing; |g| = 3.908354e1, of which the angle partial is -3.900833e1the_ladder_stops_where_the_descent_direction_still_lowers_the_energy. Stuck is asserted, not assumed: one more refine() call must return the energy bit-identical before the gradient is taken. No component of the projected direction is clipped by the box, so this is not a constrained stationary point.
how far the descent direction walks out of the ladder's stopone line-searched step of length 2^-9 = 1.953125e-3 reaches 23.024982 (2.076e-3 relative); 20 steps of steepest descent reach 22.841347 (1.004e-2 relative) for 721 rendersSame test, floor 1e-3 relative. The atom moves 0.1298 px in centre.x and 5.157e-3 rad in angle, both far below the ladder's smallest centre step of 8 * 2^-7 = 0.0625, which is why no single knob could see the improvement.
what it settled
  • THE STEP SIZE IS NOT A FREE PARAMETER AND THE SWEEP CAUGHT A WRONG ONE. src/fit/descent.rs first shipped CHOSEN_STEP = 1e-4, chosen before the sweep was run; the sweep's identity column refused it at 8.3e-3 and the table moved the constant to 1e-6. This is the instrument catching its own author, which is the whole reason L-131 demanded the sweep before the optimiser.
  • THERE IS A THIRD FAILURE MODE AND IT IS NOT MONOTONE IN EPS. The textbook picture is curvature above and cancellation below with a valley between, so a bisection on eps would find a good step. On this loss it would not: 1e-4 is a spike between two agreeing rows. The cause is that the splat's alpha cutoff gives every pixel a hard support edge, so the loss is piecewise-smooth, and a central difference is essentially exact BETWEEN crossings and wrong ACROSS one. The good step is the one that rarely straddles, not one that never does.
  • THE IDENTITY CHECK IS THE STRADDLE DETECTOR, which is why it is run at every row of the sweep rather than once at the chosen step. A straddled axis probe moves g . d without moving the directional probe, so the two estimates separate; the gradient norm alone would not have flagged 1e-4 (4.048259e1 against 4.025793e1, half a percent).
  • THE MECHANISM L-131 NAMED IS REAL. The ladder converges to a point where the gradient is 39 in norm and unclipped by the box, and a line search along it immediately lowers the energy. A coordinate ladder and a gradient are demonstrably not the same search on this loss.
  • AND IT IS SHALLOW ON THIS TARGET. 1.004e-2 relative for 721 renders. The entry's sentence that the ladder stops 'at a point a descent direction would walk straight out of' is true in sign and about one percent in size on t5-thin-stroke, so L-132's case must be made on the classes P2 reported stopping early, not on this one.
  • THE PARAMETER VECTOR IS RAW. pack/unpack carry no clamp and no angle wrap; the box enters only through projected_descent and clamp_to. A difference quotient is therefore always taken about the point that was asked for, and a boundary is handled by projecting the direction rather than by silently shortening the probe, which would have made the partial a lie about the loss.
what it refuted
  • CHOSEN_STEP = 1e-4, the module's own first choice, refused by its own step sweep at an identity error of 8.297e-3 against a 1e-3 tolerance.
  • The two-sided picture of finite-difference error implied by L-131's own next field, 'too large a step measures curvature instead of slope, too small a step measures floating-point cancellation', as a COMPLETE account. It is complete for a smooth loss; this loss has hard support edges and the error is not monotone on either side of the plateau.
  • That the gradient norm is a sufficient diagnostic for step choice. It is flat to half a percent across the bad row.
what it does NOT settle
  • Whether eps = 1e-6 transfers. The plateau was measured on ONE atom (the one-atom fit of t5-thin-stroke) at ONE viewport (64x64) with ONE kernel (gaussian). The straddle argument says the plateau's width scales with the support perimeter, so a much larger atom or a much larger viewport may move it; nothing here measures that.
  • The straddle explanation itself is a mechanism, not a measurement: no test isolates a pixel crossing. It is consistent with every column of the table and with the atom's geometry, and it is written down so it can be refuted.
  • Multi-atom gradients. gradient() takes an index and perturbs one atom; the cross terms between atoms are never formed. Joint optimisation and the prefix contract are L-132's.
  • Whether descent beats the ladder on cost. 18 renders per gradient against the ladder's 2 per knob per halving is a comparison nobody has tabulated, and 721 renders for 1e-2 relative is not obviously a good trade.
  • Whether the gain is material on the classes P2 reported stopping early. That is exactly L-132's close bar and then L-43's measurement.
  • descend() is steepest descent with a halving line search, built to measure the gap. It is not the optimiser L-132 owes: no momentum, no Adam, no seeding, no prefix contract, one atom.

The per-atom-count curves are prefix curves. How much worse does the first n atoms of a longer fit render than the same fitter's own n-atom answer?

engineering2026-09-15copy-pasteable command3 refuted
the answer

Much worse, and worst exactly where the published atom counts are read off. Across all six content classes at the five sub-full rungs of the published ladder, 26 of 30 cells read WORSE than the rung's own fit, mean +23.6%, and the worst is t2-straight-edge at n=1: the prefix renders at residual energy 1747.49 against the one-atom fit's 617.02, which is 2.83 times the energy. The shortfall falls with n, which is what the mechanism predicts: refine_tail re-tunes up to three already-placed atoms after every placement, so a short prefix has been re-tuned for a configuration it is being read without. Four of thirty cells read slightly BETTER (0.9% to 6.9%, all at high n), so 'a prefix is always worse' is false and 'a prefix is worse on average and much worse at low n' is what holds. Three readers are affected (harness.rs, nterm.rs, aspect2.rs) and the demo is NOT: it refits at every count.

the question it was built to answer

BURNDOWN L-142, piece 1. Five sites slice a shorter prefix out of a longer greedy fit and treat it as the n-atom answer, and three of them build published curves that way, including the one whose first passing rung IS the published atom cost of a class (harness.rs:583-589, first_pass). A two-target probe found 15% to 41%. What is the shortfall across the classes and rungs the published ladder actually emits, and is its sign uniform?

criterion, and its convention

Written into L-142's next before the run. Six classes t1..t6, gaussian kernel, SharedSettings::prefiltered, tile 16, truth from exact_coverage at depth 10, viewport each target's own. For each class fit once to nterm::MAX_ATOMS = 8, then for every rung of nterm::ladder_upto below the placed count, fit that rung ALONE and render both; the number is the relative difference in residual energy against the truth. A rung whose own fit stops short of its requested count is REFUSED and printed rather than compared. Vacuity guard: at least 12 (class, rung) cells must be measured, so the sweep cannot pass by reading almost nothing. The finding stands only if the mean shortfall is positive and the worst exceeds 1e-2; either failing means L-142 is a footnote and must be rewritten rather than the assertion loosened.

reproduce it
cargo test -p lights --all-features --test fit_greedy -- the_published_ladder_reads_a_prefix_at_every_rung_and_the_shortfall_is_tabulated --exact --nocapture

Run 2026-09-15 on the M5 Max: 1 passed in 44.49 s. Prints one row per (class, rung) with both energies and the shortfall, then the summary line and every cell that read better. Deterministic: residual energies, not timings. The narrower two-target probe is the sibling test the_first_n_atoms_of_a_longer_fit_are_not_the_n_atom_answer in the same file.
