(2) docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:9-15 — the page that indexes the whole record — summarises aspect 3 as "one frozen atom description of a 30° edge holds its criterion across a 32× zoom span", with no profile qualifier at all; under prefiltered() that same frozen t2 arm reads no at magnify 1x and at minify 1x, 0.5x and 0.25x. The headline is true only under contract(), which is the profile a shipping UI does not use (BURNDOWN L-13: "the profile a ui must ship — prefiltered() — is the one configuration the agreement instrument structurally refuses to certify").

"Nothing but analytic meets the 12 px glyph" (P2, aspect 1) against "Haar meets the glyph criterion" (aspect 3) — the two experiments judge the same target with different predicates, and I confirmed it in the code

contradictionp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

P2: no splat arm produced a passing glyph result in any of four configurations at any budget up to --max-atoms 48. Aspect 1: 0 of 7 kernels meet t6 at 8 atoms. Aspect 3: "with the right probe Haar MEETS the glyph criterion while sitting at rel L2 0.676." These are opposite statements about the same target in the same crate, and the cause is a dropped sub-criterion, verified in source. P2's per-probe rule for a Stroke probe (crates/lights/src/fit/edge.rs:359-363) is peak &gt;= criterion.min_peak_frac * target_contrast &amp;&amp; (fwhm - target_fwhm).abs() &lt;= max_fwhm_error — peak AND FWHM. Aspect 3's judge_fwhm (crates/lights/src/fit/scale.rs:454-462) is (scene - criterion.target_fwhm).abs() &lt;= criterion.max_fwhm_error — FWHM only, no peak term anywhere in the function. The same asymmetry exists on the edge classes: P2's Edge probe (edge.rs:354-358) requires transition_10_90 &lt; max_transition &amp;&amp; contrast &gt;= min_contrast_frac * target_contrast, aspect 3's judge_transition (scale.rs:443-452) requires the transition alone. The artefact confesses it in its own header: docs/lights/p5/ladder-magnify.tsv lines 9-11 stamp only #criterion.max_transition 1, #criterion.target_fwhm 1, #criterion.max_fwhm_error 0.5 — min_peak_frac and min_contrast_frac are absent, because nothing in that run reads them. Aspect 3's own prose half-sees this ("FWHM within ±0.5 of a one-unit stem is a loose gate — a stroke can be the right width and the wrong picture") and correctly refuses to draw glyph conclusions from the column; but the consolidated document must not put an aspect-3 meets cell in the same table as a P2 meets cell, because aspect 3's predicate is a strict relaxation of P2's on BOTH classes it judges.

P2 publishes its timings as "single-threaded" while the header literal that says so measures nothing and the timed renders ran on every core

contradictionp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsl96-sweep-parallelisma1-atom-curve
what it says

docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:4-6 records the run as "macOS aarch64, release, f64, splat-raster, global sort, tile 16, single-threaded", and P2's own caveat repeats it ("RENDER TIMES ARE RELATIVE... single-threaded f64 on a 64x64 field"). L-96 and BURNDOWN L-34 refute it, and I confirmed the mechanism directly: crates/lights/src/fit/harness.rs:737 is the literal threads: 1 written into the Header struct, and header_lines (src/fit/report.rs:400) prints it unconditionally as threads\t{}, while the timed renders inherit threads: 0 (= every core) from SharedSettings::reference(). The header is not a measurement, it is a constant. Two things follow that the record does not say. First, the 10.2-38.9 µs analytic figures and the 54.9-120.7 µs splat figures in P2's headline are not on the stated machine configuration, and P2's own "render times are relative" caveat understates the problem — they are not even relative to a stated thread count. Second, harness.rs is shared with lights-p3, so every aspect-1 report.tsv carries the same false threads 1 line; aspect 1's cost axis is kernel evaluations rather than time, so its ranking is unaffected, but its wall-clock table (gaussian 143,108 ± 41,031 ns against β=4's 151,717 ± 44,420 ns) inherits it whole.

The corpus triage's predicted atom counts against what P2 and aspect 1 measured — 2 atoms for an edge, ~20 for a glyph, 2-5 for a border; measured 8-10, never, and 2-12

contradictioncorpus-gaussian-splatting-triagep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curve
what it says

docs/lights/CORPUS_READING_MAP.md:90-98 states, as the reading's central derived result: "an edge of **any length** is still two atoms"; "A 12px glyph is ~20 atoms by the flattening bound (terminals, junctions, 2-4px counters). A 1px panel border is 2-5 atoms and length-independent." P2 measured 8-10 atoms for a straight edge, 2-12 for a 1 px stroke, 17-25 for a corner, and NO passing fit for the glyph at any budget up to 48 in any of four configurations; aspect 1 confirms 0 of 7 kernels meet t6 at 8. The glyph is the one that matters: "~20" and "never within 48" are not a refinement of each other, they are opposite answers, and the ~20 figure is the one that set the project's hybrid split line. The map hedged the class of error in advance (map:111-113, "atom counts are renderer-dependent by ~10x, so the blend rule must be settled before any count means anything") but the numbers were never struck from the map, and the corrections table at map:104-109 corrects the split LINE ("borders are cheap; text is the expensive one") while leaving both quantities standing. Whoever consolidates must carry the map's prediction and the measurement in the same row, with the measurement winning.

"The reading's central formula survived contact with the code" (corpus) against "the Image-GS premise did not reproduce" (P2) — the test that pins the formula bypasses the exact step P2 says breaks it

contradictioncorpus-gaussian-splatting-triagep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atoms
what it says

The corpus record claims the 4.394·σ²/d edge width "is now enforced by tests that did not exist when it was written — tests/normalised_blend.rs:479-556". P2 claims the premise did not reproduce because "this crate's Normalised is a coverage MIX, background·(1−c) + (weighted/weight)·c with c = min(weight,1)". Both are true of different quantities, and the code says which one is the renderer. ink_fraction (crates/lights/tests/normalised_blend.rs:74-81) returns (acc.weighted * acc.weight.recip()).x — the pure Eq. 5 top-K ratio, with the accumulator read directly. The shipped Normalised::resolve (crates/lights/src/blend.rs) returns background * (T::ONE - c) + mean * c where c = coverage(acc). The test never calls resolve. So two_atoms_render_the_closed_form_logistic proves the closed form holds for a quantity the renderer computes and then discards, and it would stay green under any change to the coverage mix — which is the thing P2 found decisive ("an edge sharp enough to meet a sub-pixel criterion has σ²/d < 0.23, so midpoint weight is far below a half, c collapses, and the mix fades toward the background exactly at the edge"). It is also the crate's recurring defect shape: a green test named after a claim, measuring a neighbouring quantity.

docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md is the record's index and it carries two sibling-experiment errors that the siblings' own artefacts refute

contradictionp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

P2_RESULTS.md is where a reader lands and it summarises aspects 1 and 3 on the same page. Two of those summaries are wrong against the artefacts of the experiments they describe. (1) P2_RESULTS.md:113-116: "β=8 was eliminated on evidence rather than headline numbers: it is **the only kernel tripping the sample-grid overfit flag** at 0.51 px". Aspect 1's own stdout reports SAMPLE-GRID OVERFIT FLAGGED on 3 cells — t3/β8 at 0.5097, t4-curve/β0.5 at 0.2029, and t4-curve/β2 at 0.5749, which is the LARGEST overfit gap in the run and belongs to β=2, the arm aspect 1 kept as its conservative pick. The published table's narrower wording (corner column only) is true; this sentence is not. (2) P2_RESULTS.md:13: "the analytic bar itself fails below 0.5×". Aspect 3's ladder has it failing AT the 0.5x rung (transition 1.600 scene) and meeting at 1x — one rung loose, in the direction that flatters the bar. Both errors point the same way, toward the conclusion the page argues. The consolidated document inherits this page's role, so this is the specific failure mode it exists to stop: a summary page that re-states sibling results without re-deriving them from their artefacts.

The four published tables on one page are in four different currencies — and the difference is not only --max-atoms, it is the truth definition, the criterion predicate and the unit

not-comparablep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

P2_RESULTS.md:9-15 warns about one axis ("P2 ran --max-atoms 48 against a pass criterion, aspect 3 ran 8 against probe widths"). There are four more, each verified. (a) TRUTH: aspect 2 fits the POINT SAMPLE of the continuous field; aspect 3 fits the pixel-box INTEGRAL of the same field (P5_RESULTS.md:218-220) — so no absolute error crosses between them, only ratios along a ladder. (b) PREDICATE: P2 and aspect 1 judge meets as transition AND contrast (edge.rs:354-358) or peak AND FWHM (edge.rs:359-363); aspect 3 judges transition alone and FWHM alone (scale.rs:443-462), and its TSV header stamps only three of the six criterion values. (c) CONVENTION: P2 and aspect 1 judge in DEVICE PIXELS at one natural viewport; aspect 3's primary is SCENE UNITS, and its own analytic row proves the two are not interconvertible by a constant — flat at 0.80-0.96 device px across a 32x span while running 0.12 -> 3.38 in scene units, "same measurement, two conventions, opposite stories" (P5_RESULTS.md:23-27). (d) COST UNIT: P2's cost is µs/frame; aspect 1's is kernel EVALUATIONS, and aspect 1's only wall-clock table separates nothing (β=4 nominally SLOWER than the gaussian with every 95% CI overlapping). So "β=4 is 2.39x cheaper" and "analytic-sdf renders at 10.2-38.9 µs" cannot appear in one column, and neither can any pair of meets cells drawn from different aspects.

Three incompatible byte currencies across P2, aspect 2 and aspect 3 — the same six-primitive glyph description is billed at 274 B and at 150 B, and the same eight atoms at ~896 B and at 288 B

not-comparablep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsp4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

Read off the committed artefact and the constants. Aspect 3 (docs/lights/p5/ladder-magnify.tsv, description_bytes): t2 analytic-sdf 51.000000 at 1 primitive; t3 70.000000 at 2; t6 150.000000 at 6; every 8-atom arm 288.000000, which is exactly 8 × ATOM_SCALARS_NATIVE(9) × SYMMETRIC_VALUE_BYTES(4.0) (crates/lights/src/fit/aspect3.rs:30-32); wavelet 64 terms 368 B (352 on the 32x32 t6). P2 bills the same six-primitive glyph description at 274 B and its 8-atom f64 fits at 1,344-2,464 B for 12-22 atoms (~112 B/atom, the 14-scalar 3D convention), noting f16 would be 28 B/atom. Aspect 2 bills a coefficient VALUE at 4.0 bytes against an atom PARAMETER at 8.0 (src/fit/nterm.rs:23,25) — asymmetric on purpose and unpinned by any test, which is the convention its own verify pass then overturned ("transforms win on all four by 3.1x-9.7x" becomes 3.60x/2.44x/1.83x with n2 flipping to the atoms). BURNDOWN L-108 confirms the split is still live: "the symmetric currency was built for aspect 3 only; aspect 2 still bills 4-vs-8", and grep finds the tests reference only ATOM_SCALARS_* and SYMMETRIC_VALUE_BYTES, never VALUE_BYTES or SCALAR_BYTES. Consequence: NO byte statement in this record survives cross-quoting. Aspect 3 says so of itself (#byte_convention symmetric-f32-provisional-see-C2, "No byte ranking is claimed from this run"); P2 and aspect 2 do not, and both put byte figures in their headlines.

Five different numbers name "the tile-binning waste", they are cells of a covariance-anisotropy ratio rather than one quantity, and a sixth is method-specific

not-comparablep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curve
what it says

Published as: 123.4x (P2's headline, a cross-cell maximum on a 512x512 field of 40 copies); "up to 89.5x" (src/kernel/compact_rs_comment/support.md:38, citing the same P2 result); 67.787 (aspect 1's own maximum, t5/β0.5) with the corner gaussian at 14.379 and the compact bump at 48.884; and 68.784901 reproduced today by BURNDOWN L-21 on t2 with --targets 2,6 --tiled-field --max-atoms 22 on a 512x512 field of 400 atoms, mean_tile_list 254.107422 of 400. Aspect 1 settled why none of them is comparable to any other: the ratio is 4r²√(sxx·syy)/(πr²√det), r² cancels, so it measures the FITTED COVARIANCE ANISOTROPY of whatever atoms that particular fit happened to place — it is a function of target, kernel, atom count and field population, not a property of the binner or the kernel. So P2's "232 of 320 atoms in the average tile" and L-21's "254.1 of 400" are not a replication and not a disagreement; they are different scenes. Add one more axis the record never crosses: BURNDOWN L-20 measured the raycast binner (a projected bounding sphere) at 2.15x the splat binner's tile-atom pairs on the same scene, so every binning figure above was splat-only. WITHDRAWN 2026-09-15: that 2.15x is now 1.0003x. The raycast binner bins the exact silhouette conic of the support ellipsoid (src/extent.rs, shared below both renderers), and cargo run -p lights --all-features --bin lights-bench -- --quick --out-dir &lt;DIR&gt; reports 46,717 raycast tile pairs against 46,704 splat pairs at 10,000 atoms 1920x1080 f32, from 109,188 against 50,735 on 2026-09-09. Kernel evaluations are unchanged at 3,407,788, which is what makes it a prune and not a change of answer. Tile-pair counts are therefore now comparable ACROSS methods; the covariance-anisotropy algebra above is unaffected, because it is a statement about what the ratio measures and not about which binner measured it. The consolidated document should carry the ALGEBRA (anisotropy, r² cancels, the fix is an OBB/Mahalanobis tile test) as the finding, and the numbers only as per-cell instances. WITHDRAWN AND RESOLVED 2026-09-15 (L-48). The five numbers were never in conflict about the ratio, and the record can now say why in one sentence: box_over_ellipse_area is a per-cell mean over atoms of an AREA ratio, it is a function of target, kernel, atom count and field population, and NO BINNER CHANGE MOVES IT — 123.406101 reproduced bit-exactly on 2026-09-15 across a binner rewrite. The number that DID move is the one nobody listed as part of this family: mean_tile_list, the average tile's candidate list, which is the only one of them that measures WORK. It went 232.023438 -> 25.747070 on the same cell when the per-tile ellipse rejection landed. So the correct reading is that this family has two quantities in it, one about the atoms and one about the binner, and quoting the first as evidence for the second is the mistake. Both are now committed at docs/lights/p2/run-8-tiled/report.tsv.

L-96's parallelism figures describe a budget the published P2 run did not use, on a machine P2 does not name

not-comparablel96-sweep-parallelismp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsp5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

L-96's own record states it: the p2rep and rep0 runs used the DEFAULT --max-atoms 8, while docs/lights/P2_RESULTS.md:4 records --max-atoms 48 for the published figures. So "a fit does 9,520 renders" is a property of the 8-atom budget (and not even of that: 9520 in 23 of 24 atom cells, 3029 in t1-flat-fill/gaussian-normalised/prefiltered, the one cell that exhausted at 2 atoms), and the 7.00x / 6.01x / 6.59x speedups are measured over a job mix that is not the published one. Two further gaps: the 65-91% render / 8-10% residual_energy / 2-4% framebuffer profile is from two content classes (t6, n1) and one kernel, and the tile-parallelism crossover ("128x128 for the colour field, 512x512 for the glyph") from the same two classes and one kernel — neither generalises to the six-target, seven-kernel sweeps P2 and aspect 1 ran. And nothing in L-96 reaches aspect 3 at all: the --sweep flag was added to lights-p2, p3 and p4 and lights-p5 did not yet exist, so the only auditable experiment in the record is also the only one still running single-threaded at 21m32s wall for 6m59s CPU on 18 cores (BURNDOWN L-95).

THE GREEDY FITTER — every atom count, every kernel ranking, every atom-versus-transform ratio and every frozen-versus-refit gap in this record is a statement about one greedy matching pursuit, and the four experiments size the confound at 1.33x to 4.79x residual energy

shared-confoundp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independencel96-sweep-parallelismcorpus-gaussian-splatting-triage
what it says

Findings that inherit it and would move if the fitter changed. P2: every atom count (2 fill, 8-10 edge, 14 curve, 2-12 stroke, 17-25 corner, never glyph) is an UPPER bound — 22 of 24 cells stopped on the fitter, not the budget; the hand-placed needle collapses T5 from never/12/never/never to 2/1/2/1; the four-atom glyph passes at residual 68.19 against an empty scene's 50.74; the greedy-myopia column (T5 meets at 2 and not at 3 or 4). Aspect 1: the whole seven-arm ranking, because "the comparison measures the kernel AND the search's affinity for that kernel, together, and they cannot be separated by running the same fitter harder" (src/fit/greedy_rs_comment/the_search_is_gaussian_tuned.md:31-40) — including the 11.5x corner result, the pixel-scale reversal, and the 27-of-42 never-met count; the calibration is against exp(-r²/2), so aspect 1's "the confound cuts in the winner's disfavour" is itself a fitter claim, and BURNDOWN L-8 records that its supporting numbers (0.93/255 vs 1.38/255) are PROSE — the test asserts only a boolean tight/loose split and SEARCH_LIMITED_RECOVERY is never compared against a measured gap. Aspect 2: the atom arm is a greedy LOWER bound while the transform arm gets its exact optimum for free AND is handed the truth image (src/fit/aspect2.rs:249) — measured shortfall 4.794x residual energy on n1, 1.332x on n2, 3.349x on t2 with the CDF 9/7 match point moving 384 -> 768 coefficients, so every "the transform reaches this error in fewer bytes" is an optimum against a floor; the Gabor result is a search failure by its own reasoning (a strict superset that loses); and the REACH axis (fitter exhausted at 61 and 50 atoms) is purely a fitter result. Aspect 3: the refitted "ceiling" is beaten by the frozen set in 13 of 24 non-base magnify cells (BURNDOWN L-7 counts 13 of 32 including base, worst t3 prefiltered 4x at +49.1%) and, recomputed, 17 of 40 minify cells; and the SEARCH CONVENTION alone (0.09603 scene-invariant against 0.18628 pixel, a gap of 0.09025) is larger than the entire frozen-to-refit effect under test (0.01617). L-96: the parallelism decision and the 9,520-render figure are shapes of this fitter. The corpus triage predicted the class of error at map:111-113. What does NOT inherit it: the analytic arms' own numbers, Haar's exactness at 1024 coefficients, the bit-identity controls (β=1 SHA-256 identical, zero-frequency Gabor bit-for-bit), and the binning anisotropy algebra. The closer is named by all four and exists nowhere: BURNDOWN L-43, "no gradient-descent fitter exists anywhere in the crate; the plan names it the highest-value item and the layering guard blocks its recipe."

The analytic-sdf arm painted coverage from a distance LOWER BOUND — fixed 2026-09-14, aspect 3 re-measured 2026-09-15, and three other experiments still owe it

shared-confoundp5-aspect3-resolution-independencep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets
what it says

sdf_coverage (crates/lights/src/fit/analytic.rs:35-49, line 44) computes clamp(0.5 - target.sdf(centre), 0, 1), and Target::sdf is sign × clearance = min-of-|sdf| — a certified lower bound built for the coverage subdivision, never for shading; at an interior seam of a multi-primitive target it reads 0.5 where truth is 1.0. BURNDOWN L-6 (open, raised 2026-09-09) measured worst |exact − sdf| of 0.4869 on t3-right-angle-corner (1 part + 1 hole) and 0.4940 on t6-glyph-like (5 parts + 1 hole), against 0.0335 and 0.1775 if line 44 used Target::combined, and states the blast radius as "the analytic bar of aspect 3 (src/fit/aspect3.rs:457) and of tests/scale.rs:335,360". That undercounts by three experiments. grep for render_analytic finds it called from src/fit/harness.rs:483 — the harness that lights-p2 AND lights-p3 run — and from src/fit/aspect2.rs:285, aspect 2's analytic control column. At harness.rs:485-486 the returned image goes straight into measure_target(&amp;image, target, &amp;request.criterion) and at :504 into row.met_criterion_at, so the analytic-sdf meets verdict in P2's and aspect 1's tables is computed FROM the defective coverage image. That places the defect under P2's headline ("meets every registered criterion on all six targets", the t6 cell at 6 primitives / 274 B / 38.9 µs, and "analytic wins the corner"), under aspect 1's delta_to_control_px sdf row, under aspect 2's analytic control on the T-classes, and under aspect 3's "the corner is analytic-only at every scale" and "analytic 5x better on the glyph". DIRECTION, stated honestly: the defect DEPRESSES the analytic arm's coverage at seams, so fixing it would most likely strengthen the analytic conclusions rather than overturn them — corroborated by the analytic arm's max|e| on t6 sitting at 0.4987/0.4910/0.4670/0.4940 along aspect 3's ladder, the same magnitude as the 0.4940 seam error. But t1/t2/t4/t5 are single-primitive and unaffected, so the defect moves t3 and t6 relative to the other four, which is exactly the axis P2's "cost scales with corners and curvature" finding is drawn on. The control arm gate is safe: control_arm_valid (harness.rs:511) is fed by AnalyticArm::Exact, which already got the fix. Nothing can catch this today — tests/fit_edge.rs:346 asserts the 0.8 px ramp exactly only on t2 and attributes every departure elsewhere to "that target's own scatter quantisation". — CLOSED FOR ASPECT 3 ON 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-144), STILL OPEN FOR THE OTHER THREE. Line 44 now reads target.combined(centre). The published aspect-3 ladders were re-measured with cargo run -p lights --all-features --release --bin lights-p5 -- --out-dir &lt;REPO&gt;/docs/lights/p5 --classes t2,t3,n1,t6 --direction both --profile both: twenty of 336 rows changed on the 26 deterministic columns, all of them analytic-sdf on t3 or t6, and t1/t2/t4/t5/n1 and every atom, wavelet and criterion column are bit-identical — which is the control this audit predicted. The predicted magnitudes were right to six decimals: max|e| at 1x went 0.486860 → 0.033467 on t3 and 0.494030 → 0.177534 on t6, against this audit's "0.0335 and 0.1775". The predicted DIRECTION was right too — every figure moved in the analytic arm's favour. What this audit got wrong is "fixing it would most likely strengthen the analytic conclusions rather than overturn them": it strengthened two of them (the corner goes from 7x to 84x better than frozen atoms at 8x, the glyph from 5x to 34x) and OVERTURNED a third, because the bug's error was itself a fixed-width seam artefact and therefore boundary-localised, so it manufactured the very decay behaviour that P5_RESULTS.md's 'the decay column has a denominator' finding cited the multi-primitive classes as evidence for. STILL OWED, and not measured by L-144: P2's and aspect 1's analytic-sdf meets verdicts through harness.rs:483, and aspect 2's analytic control through aspect2.rs:285. Aspect 3's own verdict columns did NOT move, which does not license assuming the others will not.

Aspect 3's wavelet arm changes its own basis depth along the ladder — 2 levels at the bottom rung to 7 at the top — and aspect 2 measured that one level costs up to 21x in error

shared-confoundp5-aspect3-resolution-independencep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets
what it says

Nobody has crossed these two. Aspect 2's AUDIT 4 found that MIN_COARSE_SIDE = 4 (src/fit/transform/mod.rs:13) silently sets the decomposition depth, and that n1's CDF 9/7 error at N=64 is 0.0303 at four levels, 0.6390 at three and 0.9720 at one — 21x from one level fewer, 32x across the span — with the level count also a function of viewport, not just of the constant. Aspect 3 then ran a ZOOM LADDER, i.e. it varied the viewport by 32x, at a fixed 64 terms. I computed the consequence from the shipped max_levels (transform/mod.rs:113-125, width / step &gt;= MIN_COARSE_SIDE): 16x16 -> 2 levels, 64x64 -> 4, 512x512 -> 7. The committed artefact confirms the viewports: docs/lights/p5/ladder-minify.tsv gives t2 wavelet-haar rows at 512x512, 256x256, 128x128, 64x64, 32x32, 16x16, all terms = 64. So aspect 3's wavelet arm is a DIFFERENT basis at every rung, and its rel L2 moves 0.139118 (16x16, 2 levels) to 0.205888 (512x512, 7 levels) with that change folded in. Two aspect-3 findings inherit it unstated: "the wavelet arms post the flattest decays in the table (0.98-1.07)" — flatness measured while the basis depth changes by up to five levels — and "CDF 9/7 holds equally flat and beats the atoms absolutely (0.03026 against 0.03793)" on the colour field, which is the run's one pro-transform absolute claim. A third, smaller one: t6 runs from a 32x32 base and so gets one level fewer than t2/t3/n1 at every matching rung, which is visible in the artefact as its 352 B wavelet description against the others' 368 B. Aspect 3 declares its arm as lattice-bound and warns that a flat decay from a bad base is not resolution independence — it does not declare that the lattice's DEPTH is itself a ladder variable.

P2's and aspect 2's atom-count curves are prefix slices of one fit rather than fits at that budget; aspect 3 is the only experiment that refits

shared-confoundp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsp4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independence
what it says

BURNDOWN L-110 names the three consumers and I confirmed the split: atoms[..n] at src/fit/harness.rs:548,609,683 (the P2/aspect-1 sweep), src/fit/nterm.rs:351 and src/fit/aspect2.rs:237 (aspect 2's every N-term atom curve) — and NOTHING in aspect3.rs, whose fit_at returns outcome.atoms whole (aspect3.rs:436) and refits at every rung, with its own test. Because refine_tail re-refines earlier atoms after later ones land (greedy.rs:518-524), the first atom of a three-atom fit is not the atom a one-atom fit places — asserted bit-for-bit at tests/nterm.rs:512. Consequences that cross experiments: P2's own caveat measured it (the corner reads 8/8/8/8 at --max-atoms 8, 23/17/24/24 at 24, 25/17/48/48 at 48), so every P2 sweep row and every aspect-2 rung is conditioned on the RUN'S --max-atoms, a flag that header_lines (report.rs:393-421) does not record. And the counterexample proving a prefix can RISE is measured only at max_atoms = 2 with one refinement pass and a tail of one (BURNDOWN L-45), not at the six passes and 8-to-64 atoms every published aspect-2 table used — so the size of this confound is unmeasured at the effort where it is being relied on.

The oriented tile test — P2 named the fix, aspect 1 proved algebraically that no kernel can substitute for it, and it SHIPPED for the splat binner on 2026-09-14 without the record noticing

owedp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curve
what it says

CORRECTED 2026-09-15 (BURNDOWN L-48). The body below was true when written and the last sentence of it was already false: the fix is built for the rasteriser. bin_and_sort (src/method/splat/bin.rs) still takes span_of_box as its CANDIDATE RECTANGLE, then rejects each tile in it whose distance to the atom under the atom's own whitened quadratic form exceeds its support — min_quadratic_over_rect(form, atom.centre, lo, hi) &gt; reach — which is exactly the Mahalanobis tile test L-21 specified, using l_inv already on ScreenAtom. It landed in commit 6b1b9ab8 on 2026-09-14. The ray caster got the same treatment from the other direction in L-20 (src/extent.rs, the exact silhouette conic of the support ellipsoid). MEASURED CONSEQUENCE: P2's most-cited tile figure moves 9.01x. The cell that published 232.023438 of 320 atoms in the average tile now measures 25.747070, and the old number returns bit-exactly when that one rejection is removed. WHAT REMAINS OPEN is narrower than the title suggested and worth stating exactly: the SPAN is still axis-aligned, so the binner still ENUMERATES tiles the ellipse cannot reach and pays a rejection test for each; an oriented span would not enumerate them at all. Nobody has measured what that second step is worth, and after a 9x prune it is no longer obvious it is worth anything. The original entry, kept because the withdrawal says more than the correction: P2 found the binning result INVERTS its own atom-count win (the needles that make an edge cheap in atoms are catastrophic in tile work) and named the OBB/ellipse test as the fix. Aspect 1 then tried the obvious kernel-side substitute and refuted it by its own authors: exact finite support made the corner WORSE (48.884 against the gaussian's 14.379), because r² cancels out of the ratio and no atom curve can move it. So the question is closed on the science and open in the code. BURNDOWN L-48 confirms grep -rni &#x27;obb|oriented box|ellipse test&#x27; crates/lights/src returns nothing and span_of_box (src/tiling.rs:77) is still the only binner, used by src/method/splat/bin.rs:49 and src/method/raycast/prepare.rs:287. L-21 gives the exact patch — reject a tile inside the existing for ty / for tx loops with the same Mahalanobis test the pixel loop already runs, using l_inv already on ScreenAtom — plus the regression (a 45° needle case asserting the emitted key count DROPS while the rendered image stays bit-identical) and the mutation. One counter-note the record should carry: the ratio is instrumented as box_over_ellipse_area only under the fit feature, and BURNDOWN L-61 says the splat path never charges candidates_tested at all, so P2's "one instrumented run away" is optimistic by one small fix.

DECOMPOSE THE PREFILTER — aspect 1 deferred its size to "a later aspect", aspect 3 was that aspect and explicitly did not discharge it, and the backlog item that carries it rests on a sentence that was false the day it was written

oweda1-atom-curvep5-aspect3-resolution-independencecorpus-gaussian-splatting-triagep2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atoms
what it says

Three records hand this forward and none of them lands it. Aspect 1 ran contract only, on the stated ground that the moment dilation is exact for a gaussian and a moment-matched approximation for β≠1 and the compact bump, so running with it on "would have measured a gaussian identity applied to non-gaussian atoms and called the result a property of the curve" — and deferred the SIZE of that confound to a later aspect. Aspect 3 ran both profiles and produced the sharpest evidence yet (the prefilter buys error and sells sharpness), then said in its own words: screen_dilation = 0.3 is applied as ONE constant where Mip-Splatting (arXiv:2311.16493) states it is a SUM of 0.1 screen-space and 0.2 object-space, a 2D UI wants only the screen half, "and it is plausibly the object half doing the blurring. Nothing here measures that split — it is the next experiment, not a conclusion of this one" (P5_RESULTS.md:169-174). So aspect 1's deferral is still outstanding: no run anywhere measures a non-gaussian kernel under the prefilter, which is the configuration a shipping UI uses (BURNDOWN L-13). [Corrected 2026-09-28 by L-101: map:147 now says the gain is applied at method/splat/project.rs, carries a visible Withdrawn paragraph, and the three top-three papers are marked not on disk; the TWO_HORIZON_PLAN.md copy had already been corrected by L-37.]

n2-soft-shadow — the one content class that would discriminate, blocked out of the resolution ladder, and the one class aspect 2's byte correction flipped

owedp5-aspect3-resolution-independencep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-wavelets
what it says

Aspect 3's own conclusion is that resolution independence "only DISCRIMINATES on content with structure above the base lattice" — the colour field proves nothing because it has none, the glyph fails, the edge is marginal. n2-soft-shadow is the crate's only class that composites a crisp rounded rectangle over a blurred copy of itself, i.e. a hard and a soft edge in one image, which is exactly the discriminating case. It cannot enter aspect 3: BURNDOWN L-42 shows Sampler has exactly three variants — ColourField (n1), BandLimitedNoise (n3), BroadbandPatch (n4) at src/fit/scale.rs:218-222 — and ScaleClass::Continuous can only be built from one of them, so n2 exists only inside the aspect-2 N-term run. Independently, n2 is the class aspect 2's own verify pass flipped: under a symmetric f32 / nine-scalar convention "n2-soft-shadow flip[s] to the atoms in both its cells", making it the single cell where the atom arm wins the byte axis. Both experiments therefore point at the same missing run from opposite directions, and neither says so. L-42 gives the recipe and warns against the shortcut: add Sampler::SoftShadow evaluating n2_rounded_rect_sdf analytically with the blur in SCENE units — do not reuse n2_shadow_plane(), which is a fixed 64x64 raster convolution with sigma pinned in PIXELS and would make the ladder measure the raster rather than the description.

The transport falsification gate — one day, four kill criteria, never run; three of the four are unmeasured and the baseline it demands was never written down

owedtransport-patch-graph
what it says

The transport study reaches a verdict entirely by reasoning and then names, in its own strongest caveat, the one measurement that would test it: render one real atrium canvas (~60 node cards at real z-offsets over the real background, real albedos) twice — an offline path-traced reference against SSAO plus a contact-hardened drop shadow in egui::Painter — side by side at 100% and 200%; if indistinguishable, "the line is dead and it cost a day instead of three weeks". The study's own sentence is the one the consolidated document should quote: "Every other measurement presumes the effect is worth having, and that presumption is the one that was never tested."

The case FOR splats has still never been tested by anybody — P2 said so, aspect 2 opened four classes toward it and gave all four no control arm, and the corpus reading says the head-to-head does not exist in the literature either

owedp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsp4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletscorpus-gaussian-splatting-triage
what it says

P2 states it as its first unsettled item: every target tested was a shape an SDF renders exactly from a description you already hold, and "the content splats exist for — photographs, soft shadows, band-limited noise, fitted textures, continuous fields — has NO ANALYTIC ARM AT ALL and is entirely unmeasured. This work bounds where splats lose. It says nothing about where they win." Aspect 2 took the obvious next step and added exactly that content (n1-n4) — and then, correctly, refused to invent a weak analytic arm for it: "on n1-n4 the control column is an ABSENT magenta hatch", so those four classes carry no ceiling anything is measured against, and six of the twelve arm-class combinations were never drawn. Aspect 3 inherited three of the four and could only compare atoms against wavelets. Meanwhile the corpus triage searched for prior art five ways across two independent passes and concluded "No head-to-head of gaussians versus SDF/vector on text or UI content exists" — so the comparison is missing from the literature AND from the crate, in the same direction. The consolidated document's honest headline is therefore narrower than any single experiment's verdict: the record bounds where splats lose on crisp authored chrome, at one scale, under one fitter, and it contains no measurement at all of the content splats were invented for. That gap is the largest owed item after the fitter, and unlike the fitter nobody has named it as a planned run.

L-159's 0.7 px rule is a bound and not a break point, and on real glyphs it needs the paper between strokes

contradictionl159-glyph-inflation-over-scalel168-real-glyph-inflation-over-scale
what it says

l159 found both of its outlines breaking where the thinnest feature fell below about 0.7 px and carried that forward as the rule: holds above, breaks below. l168 ran the same harness and criterion on nine real glyphs. Neither half survives as stated. BELOW: six of nine glyphs hold one to three rungs under the size where their thinnest feature crosses 0.7 px, and three never break down to 8 px/em. ABOVE: on ink alone 鬱 breaks at 24 px/em with its thinnest stroke at 0.798 px. What reconciles them is the paper: 鬱's thinnest gap is 0.38 px at that size and its worst pixel sits on it, and counting the thinnest parallel-walled gap alongside the thinnest stroke makes 0.7 px a bound on all 108 rows. Quote the rule as a sufficient condition on both sides of the outline, never as the size where a face breaks.

the premise that CoreText and FreeType hint and stem-darken below about 32 px is wrong in both halves

contradictionl168-real-glyph-inflation-over-scalel173-system-rasteriser-oracle
what it says

l168 listed APPEARANCE as not settled on the premise that CoreText and FreeType hint and stem-darken below about 32 px, and L-173 was filed on it. l173 measured both. CoreText applies no hinting to the calibration stems: with smoothing off its glyph renderer draws them within 2.6 8-bit steps of box coverage at every rung from 8 to 128 px/em. Its smoothing is a dilation at every size, 0.0117 px of width per px/em for dark text up to a 0.6 px cap reached at 64 px/em (0.032 per px/em, capped from 20, for light text). FreeType 2.13.2 ships with stem darkening off in all four drivers and hints only under a hinting load flag. Quote neither system's darkening as a small-size effect.

the optical baseline is at 1920x1080 POINTS, so only its 1x rows share a pixel count with the lights frame figures

not-comparablel174-optical-egui-baselinel150-cpu-pack-terml123-frame-rate-1080p
what it says

l150's 7.444 ms pack and l123's frame column are measured at 1920x1080 pixels. l174 fixes the LAYOUT at 1920x1080 points and renders it at 1 and 2 pixels per point, so its 2x rows are 3840x2160 pixels, four times the pixel count. Quote an l174 row beside a lights figure only at matching pixels, and say which. The same care applies inside l174: its CPU column is per frame whatever the scale, its GPU column is not.

the pack is 7.444 ms in l150 and 5.7430-6.0190 ms in l176 for MORE work, so quote the figure beside the frame it was measured on

not-comparablel150-cpu-pack-terml176-renderer-arms
what it says

l150 timed FloatImage::to_clamped_u8 alone on a synthetic 1920x1080 f32 frame and read 7.444 ms. l176 times the same call PLUS an RGBA expansion on the premultiplied planes of eleven real optical frames and reads 5.7430-6.0190 ms serially at 1x. The two differ by 1.24-1.30x in the unexpected direction, at the edge of this machine's 1.3x noise; neither is wrong, and neither should be quoted beside the other's frame. l176's parallel fused pass, 0.3872-0.4969 ms at 1x, is the figure to set beside a parallel render.

l150's 7.444 ms pack does not reproduce: its own unchanged command reads 4.643-4.658 ms today

unreproduciblel150-cpu-pack-terml190-fused-bgra8-handoffl176-renderer-arms
what it says

On 2026-09-29, cargo test -p lights --release --all-features --test handoff_pack -- --nocapture --test-threads 1 was re-run three times. The test file and to_clamped_u8 are byte-identical to 7a4eb243, where l150 was measured, and the release profile is unchanged. It read 4.643-4.658 ms median at a 1.02-1.03x spread. On 2026-09-16 it read 7.444 ms at a 1.50x spread. The gap is 1.59x, outside the 1.3x noise rule, so one of the two days measured a machine in another state, and neither recorded that state. The re-run also resolves the note above that l176's pack was faster than l150's for more work: l176's 5.7430-6.0190 ms sits beside today's 4.64-4.66 ms as expected. Quote l150 as 'measured 7.444 ms on 2026-09-16, 4.64-4.66 ms on re-run', and the pack as 28% of a 16.67 ms budget, not 45%. The rows are in docs/lights/benchmarks/l190/handoff.tsv.

kernel/gaussian

KEEPprovenance: 3d-scenetransfers: yes142 Rust lines · 3 mutation rowsl123-frame-rate-1080pl197-gallery-frame-timesl141-gradient-normal-shadingl153-interpenetration-compositel163-bandwidth-crossovera1-atom-curve
why this verdict

The atom the surviving mission actually draws: the gallery holds a Renderer<f32, Gaussian> and nothing else (crates/lights-mac/src/bin/gallery/mod.rs:47), and every 3D figure in the record is a gaussian one - 60 fps at 1080p to about 30k atoms on splat once the pack is paid (l123), 4.910-5.063 ms of CPU per warm gallery frame (l197), the gradient normal (l141), interpenetration (l153), the f32/f64 ladder (l163). kernel/gaussian-normalised

RE-JUDGEprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: no777 Rust lines · 3 mutation rowsp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsl96-sweep-parallelism
why this verdict

It has no role in a 3D scene, by algebra rather than by measurement: Normalised::resolve mixes a weighted mean over the background by coverage and carries no depth term (src/blend.rs:269), so nothing behind anything is hidden, and the certified path refuses it (BlendClassNotOver, src/agreement.rs:285-290). Its one measured role is inside the fit arm - P2's blend pair on crisp UI content (lights-p2 sweeps KernelFamily::BLEND_PAIR) and the fit jobs of the sweep-parallelism tests (tests/fit_parallel.rs) - so it stands or falls with fit/arm, which is itself RE-JUDGE. It still costs the DEFAULT build 12 of the 96 render-loop bodies (l67), although nothing outside the fit feature needs it.

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Is the fit arm kept (fit/arm's experiment decides), and if it is, does any fit it still runs need a coverage-normalised blend rather than Over?
  • arms gaussian-normalised against gaussian under Over, on the fit arm's surviving fixtures, at equal atom count.
  • criterion The fitter's own residual energy against exact-coverage truth, same targets and budget. Convention: a ratio inside 1.3x is a tie, and a tie goes to Over, because Over is the blend both the certified path and a 3D scene need.
  • cost Nothing of its own: it rides on fit/arm's decision, and the comparison reruns fixtures that exist (tests/fit_greedy.rs already fits both blends).
  • prunes This kernel, and with it the only kernel the Normalised blend has, if the fit arm goes or Over ties it.

kernel/super-gaussian

RE-JUDGEprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: no1082 Rust lines · 5 mutation rowsa1-atom-curvel135-per-atom-beta
why this verdict

The one kernel knob with positive evidence, and all of it on crisp UI content: beta=4 closes the right-angle corner's gap to exact box coverage 11.5x (a1), and a per-atom beta ladder beats the tuned gaussian on 42 of 43 rungs (l135) - while the ranking reverses on a 1 px stroke (a1), and the search was tuned for exp(-r^2/2), so each figure measures the curve and the search's affinity for it together (a1). None of it transfers: a flatter shoulder that meets a UI corner says nothing about whether a 3D surface needs fewer atoms. The certified path refuses every beta but 1 (KernelCurveNotGaussian, src/agreement.rs:292-298), and beta=1 is the gaussian bit for bit (a1).

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Fitted to a 3D object from rendered views, does a per-atom beta (l135's shaped ladder) reach a lower held-out-view error than the gaussian at equal atom count?
  • arms The per-atom super-gaussian against the gaussian, same seed, same search, same budget in renders.
  • criterion Held-out-view residual energy (the fitter's own metric) against a reference rendered by the ray caster under contract settings, at equal atom count and an equal budget in renders. Convention: 1.3x or better keeps the arm; a ratio inside 1.3x is a tie, and a tie prunes it, because the gaussian is the one kernel and Over the one blend the certified path accepts.
  • cost Not runnable today. The crate cannot fit a 3D scene: src/fit/backward.rs differentiates a 2D orthographic render of atoms at one depth, so the run waits on a perspective backward pass through a method, a build this audit does not size. After it, a fit is minutes per scene at l147's price of 0.89-1.46 renders per exact gradient.
  • prunes This kernel and greedy::fit_shaped, if it ties. If the PM rules that no 3D fit is coming, every row whose experiment this is - fit/arm, fit/adam, kernel/super-gaussian, kernel/gabor-colour, kernel/gauss-hermite-colour - falls to PRUNE without the run.

kernel/compact-bump

RE-JUDGEprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: partly94 Rust lines · 4 mutation rowsa1-atom-curvel123-frame-rate-1080p
why this verdict

Its hope was cheaper binning. The half aspect 1 measured on UI content was refuted by algebra - the box-over-ellipse waste is covariance anisotropy, r^2 cancels, and no curve can move it (a1) - and the half that survives is 1.40x fewer kernel evaluations on a 2D corner (a1). What transfers is a bound, and it lands on the noise floor: solving both supports at the contract cutoff of 1/255 (src/kernel/compact.rs:29-43 against src/kernel/gaussian.rs:17-23), the bump covers 0.761x the gaussian's area at peak 0.99 (the alpha cap), 0.826x at 0.5, and MORE below peak 0.1 (1.49x at 2/255). If bind and render both shrank by the best case, l123's 100k-atom splat frame (bind 18.187 of 23.923 ms) would shorten by 1.290x - under the 1.3x floor at full opacity and worse for fainter atoms. The certified path refuses it (KernelCurveNotGaussian).

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Does the compact bump shorten a 3D splat frame by 1.3x or more, where the support bound puts the best case at 1.290x?
  • arms compact-bump against gaussian, splat contract, f32, the same atoms on lights-bench's sphere ladder.
  • criterion Frame median of 9 reps with the first discarded, release, 1920x1080, at 10k and 100k atoms. Convention: 1.3x or more at both rungs keeps it; anything less prunes it (this crate's noise floor).
  • cost One flag, because lights-bench draws only the gaussian today; then one release build and under a minute of rendering (l123 measured 4.858 and 23.923 ms per splat frame at these rungs).

precision/f64

Measured on the surviving target and found free per frame: doubling every atom's bytes never moves a frame out of the 1.3x floor - splat's f64/f32 frame ratio is 0.985-1.019 from 100k to 10M atoms, the ray caster's 1.095-1.174 to 300k (l163). splat/per-tile-sort

RE-JUDGEprovenance: nonetransfers: no156 Rust lines · 2 mutation rowsl163-bandwidth-crossover
why this verdict

Never judged by anything. No preset selects it - SplatConfig::reference and ::contract both pick GlobalSort (src/method/splat/mod.rs:33-46) - no record measures it, and three tests reach it (determinism, splat_projection, method_matrix). As built it is not a different order but the same one: sorting by tile and then each tile's run by (depth, slot) ends in GlobalSort's (tile, depth, slot) order with a second pass on top, serially (src/method/splat/bin.rs:85-99). It sits on the term that matters most in a 3D frame, though: bind is 76-91% of splat's frame and its dominant work is exactly this sort, single-threaded (l163) - so one run is owed before it goes.

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Does PerTileSort as built bind a 3D splat frame 1.3x faster than GlobalSort, given that both produce the same key order?
  • arms SortKind::PerTileSort against SortKind::GlobalSort, the splat contract otherwise, f32, with the two images asserted bit-identical.
  • criterion Bind-phase median of 9 reps with the first discarded, release, 1920x1080, at 100k and 1M atoms on lights-bench's ladder. Convention: 1.3x or better at both rungs keeps it and makes it the default; anything less prunes it.
  • cost One lights-bench flag and one release run; bind at 100k atoms is about 18 ms (l123), so the rendering is seconds after the build.
  • prunes PerTileSort - one enum arm, one branch in bin.rs and its three test sites - if it misses 1.3x, which leaves a parallel per-tile sort, the idea this arm gestures at, as a new experiment rather than an arm already carried.

fit/arm

RE-JUDGEprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: no11795 Rust lines · 293 mutation rowsp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsa1-atom-curvep4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independencel96-sweep-parallelisml131-finite-difference-gradientl132-adam-against-the-ladderl142-prefix-shortfalll43-greedy-at-imagegs-bitratesl97-candidate-evaluation-costl135-per-atom-betal147-analytic-gradient
why this verdict

A feature the surviving mission never turns on: fit is not a default feature (crates/lights/Cargo.toml) and lights-mac depends on lights with default features only, so neither the 3D build nor the gallery compiles a line of it. Every measurement it holds was taken on 2D content at one depth under an orthographic camera - P2's six crisp UI classes and the aspects built on them, six CLIC crops (l43), glyphs - so none of its verdicts says anything about the 3D engine. It is also the richest paradigm demonstration in the crate: three searches measured against each other (the ladder, Adam, a line search), two independent gradients that agree to 9.1e-10 (l147), atoms against transforms. And it is where a game's atoms would come from if they are fitted rather than placed by hand, which is the one question never asked of it.

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Can the crate fit atoms to a 3D object from rendered views, and does a fitted set beat the showcase's hand-placed object at the same atom count?
  • arms The fit arm's own searches on a 3D target - the ladder's seed, then l147's line search over the exact gradient - against scene::showcase's hand-placed atoms at equal count.
  • criterion Held-out-view residual energy (the fitter's own metric) against a reference rendered by the ray caster under contract settings, at equal atom count and an equal budget in renders. Convention: 1.3x or better keeps the arm; a ratio inside 1.3x is a tie, and a tie prunes it, because the gaussian is the one kernel and Over the one blend the certified path accepts.
  • cost Not runnable today. The crate cannot fit a 3D scene: src/fit/backward.rs differentiates a 2D orthographic render of atoms at one depth, so the run waits on a perspective backward pass through a method, a build this audit does not size. After it, a fit is minutes per scene at l147's price of 0.89-1.46 renders per exact gradient.
  • prunes The whole fit feature - src/fit, lights-p2 to lights-p5, their tests and mutation rows - if fitted atoms do not beat hand-placed ones by 1.3x; the records it produced then stand as historical, pinned to the commit before the removal (L-200). If the PM rules that no 3D fit is coming, every row whose experiment this is - fit/arm, fit/adam, kernel/super-gaussian, kernel/gabor-colour, kernel/gauss-hermite-colour - falls to PRUNE without the run.

fit/greedy-ladder

KEEPprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: no2708 Rust lines · 40 mutation rowsp2-crisp-ui-cost-in-atomsl142-prefix-shortfalll43-greedy-at-imagegs-bitratesl132-adam-against-the-ladderl147-analytic-gradient
why this verdict

KEEP within the fit arm, whose own verdict is RE-JUDGE, and it goes if the fit arm goes. It is the incumbent every other search is measured against and seeded from: every published atom count rests on it (the record's shared-confound finding), Adam lost to it 0-6 twice (l132, l147), and the line search that beats it on two classes starts from its seed (l147). Its deficits are measured and recorded - a prefix is not the n-atom answer, up to 2.83x the energy at n=1 (l142); about 8 dB behind image-gs across datasets (l43) - and none is a reason to remove the baseline the other searches need.

fit/adam

RE-JUDGEprovenance: ui-crisptransfers: no1455 Rust lines · 13 mutation rowsl132-adam-against-the-ladderl147-analytic-gradient
why this verdict

Lost 0-6 to the ladder twice, first over the 18-render difference gradient (l132) and then over the exact one at equal renders (l147), and l147 found why: its first bias-corrected step is exactly sign(g)*rate and every improvement it made came in steps 1-5. All of it is on 4-8 atom fits of 2D UI content, whose landscape L-131 showed is cut by support-edge straddles (l131). Nothing in the record has run it at the atom counts a 3D fit would have, which is where an optimiser with per-parameter step sizes would earn or lose its place.

the experiment that would decide it
  • question On a 3D fit with thousands of atoms, does Adam over the exact gradient reach a lower held-out-view error than l147's line search at equal renders?
  • arms Adam against the backtracking line search, both fed the analytic gradient, same seed and render budget.
  • criterion Held-out-view residual energy (the fitter's own metric) against a reference rendered by the ray caster under contract settings, at equal atom count and an equal budget in renders. Convention: 1.3x or better keeps the arm; a ratio inside 1.3x is a tie, and a tie prunes it, because the gaussian is the one kernel and Over the one blend the certified path accepts.
  • cost Not runnable today. The crate cannot fit a 3D scene: src/fit/backward.rs differentiates a 2D orthographic render of atoms at one depth, so the run waits on a perspective backward pass through a method, a build this audit does not size. After it, a fit is minutes per scene at l147's price of 0.89-1.46 renders per exact gradient.
  • prunes Adam's half of descent.rs and its rate sweep, if it ties or loses. If the PM rules that no 3D fit is coming, every row whose experiment this is - fit/arm, fit/adam, kernel/super-gaussian, kernel/gabor-colour, kernel/gauss-hermite-colour - falls to PRUNE without the run.
where

crates/lights/src/fit/descent.rs · crates/lights/tests/fit_descent.rs

fit/finite-difference-gradient

KEEPprovenance: target-freetransfers: yes634 Rust lines · 13 mutation rowsl131-finite-difference-gradientl147-analytic-gradient
why this verdict

KEEP within the fit arm, and it goes if the fit arm goes. It is the oracle the analytic gradient is checked against: the two agree to 9.1e-10 of |g| on all nine partials in four cases (l147), and the difference's own step was measured rather than assumed, eps = 1e-6 on a plateau where |g| agrees to seven digits (l131). Two independent implementations of one quantity that must agree is the paradigm case again, and the difference is the only gradient for a kernel the analytic pass does not cover - it is written for the gaussian under Over.

fit/analytic-gradient

KEEPprovenance: target-freetransfers: yes778 Rust lines · 7 mutation rowsl147-analytic-gradient
why this verdict

KEEP within the fit arm, and it goes if the fit arm goes. The best-evidenced search improvement in the crate and the piece a 3D fitter would start from: exact to 9.1e-10 of |g| against the verified central difference, its forward image equal to the tile renderer's bit for bit, at 0.89-1.46x the cost of one render against the difference's 18 (l147). Those are properties of the math and transfer; the wins a line search draws from it - beating the ladder on t4-curve and t6-glyph-like from the same seed at equal renders (l147) - are on UI classes and do not.

why this verdict

KEEP within the fit arm, and it goes if the fit arm goes. It is the truth rather than a contestant: exact box coverage is what every fit target is rendered from, the ceiling the analytic arm was measured as (a1), and the geometric oracle the glyph work was checked against (l159, l171). Its correctness is a property of the integral, not of any target; if the fit arm goes, the Tier 0 glyph path loses its oracle with it.

fit/transform-haar

PRUNEprovenance: target-freetransfers: yes1066 Rust lines · 19 mutation rowsp4-aspect2-signed-atoms-and-waveletsp5-aspect3-resolution-independencel96-sweep-parallelism
why this verdict

A comparator for a question the record has answered, and structurally unable to become anything the surviving mission draws. The crate ruled on purpose that a wavelet is not a kernel - its positions are not free, it is filtered rather than evaluated, and its basis functions are not independent - and tests/layering.rs::the_transform_arm_is_not_a_kernel enforces it (src/fit/transform/mod_rs_comment/not_a_kernel.md). So it can never be placed in a scene, lit or depth-sorted; its only role was atoms against wavelets on 2D content in aspects 2 and 3, whose answers stand - Haar represents a 30 degree step exactly at 1024 coefficients and wins 1 of 160 byte rungs under one currency (p4). That reason holds on any target. No record's command runs its test; the p4, p5 and l96 runs lose its columns, so those records gain the removal commit and their Haar figures stand as historical (L-200).

ui/draw-list

PRUNEprovenance: optical-frametransfers: yes4046 Rust lines · 32 mutation rowsl175-drawlist-ui-framel176-renderer-arms
why this verdict

Judged on optical's frames, and the judgement transfers to anything a 3D engine would draw through it, because what it lost on was pixel traffic rather than anything about optical: 97.8-99.2% of the DrawList's frame is bytes moved (l175), and tiny-skia draws the identical op sets 2.22-10.16x faster from 4 B/px against 24 B/px with no pack (l176). A game's HUD is the same op kinds - panels, bars, strokes, glyph boxes, images - so the mechanism carries. And the surviving mission has already routed its chrome elsewhere: the gallery's selector, captions and frame time are AppKit views in the host (L-197, crates/lights-mac/src/bin/gallery/chrome.rs), and nothing in the 3D path calls DrawList. What goes with it is what L-196 counted in lights' favour on chrome - the 1x quality gate on a 1 px stroke, 0.9369 where tiny-skia reads 0.6157, and bit-exact parallel bands (l176) - the one place this table meets a PM sentence, so the PM should read this row before L-200 acts. RasterLayer stays: it lives in src/image.rs and the 3D cluster uses it (L-73). The deleted targets are exactly l175's and l176's, so both records gain the removal commit and stand as historical, and tiny-skia leaves the dev-dependencies with them.

text/tier0-glyph-path

RE-JUDGEprovenance: glyphtransfers: partly3390 Rust lines · 26 mutation rowsl159-glyph-inflation-over-scalel161-quad-to-arc-boundl168-real-glyph-inflation-over-scalel171-real-font-round-tripl173-system-rasteriser-oracle
why this verdict

Measured to WORK, not to fail: a real face round-trips at 1/4000 em with ink ratio 0.99941-1.00015 (l171), the quad-to-arc tolerance holds two-sided (l161), analytic inflation holds wherever the thinnest stroke and the thinnest gap are both at least 0.7 px (l159, l168), and it stays nearer the geometry than the platforms' own renderers (l173). What moved is its consumer: every one of those runs was taken for optical's labels, and the surviving mission's only text today - the gallery's captions - is drawn by the host's AppKit because lights has no text path (L-197). The geometry and its bounds transfer to any target; the reason to carry them does not, unless the engine is to draw its own text rather than ask every platform host to. L-183, L-184, L-162 and L-172 all wait on this row.

the experiment that would decide it
  • question Should the engine draw its own text, so that no platform host has to reimplement it - and if so, does a label drawn through Tier 0 hold the 1x quality bar at HUD sizes inside a frame?
  • arms A HUD label drawn by lights (outline, then Region, then exact coverage into a RasterLayer composited over the gallery frame) against the host's CoreText label the gallery draws today.
  • criterion Worst pixel against exact box coverage in 8-bit steps at 12-32 px/em, with l173's instrument and calibration, plus CPU time for one frame's labels at 1920x1016, median of 5. Convention: the lights label must sit no further from the geometry than the host's (whose default is 31.4-201.8 steps from it, l173), and one frame's labels must cost under 10% of l197's warm frame, 0.49 ms.
  • cost The quality half exists: l173's calibration runs in 15 s in debug. The time half needs one label path through PrimitiveSet::render into the gallery frame - a small build, then seconds of running. The deciding half is a PM ruling, because L-197 put text in the host.
  • prunes If text stays in the host: outline.rs, glyph_scale.rs, the five vendored fonts, ttf-parser and TEXT_LAYERS.md, closing L-183, L-184, L-162 and L-172 with them; l159, l161, l168, l171 and l173 then stand as historical.

3d/surfel

KEEPprovenance: 3d-scenetransfers: yes720 Rust lines · 7 mutation rowsl122-surfel-stored-normall197-gallery-frame-timesl153-interpenetration-composite
why this verdict

Built for the surviving target and measured on it: a stored normal keeps a camera-facing surfel at 1.000000000000 of its flat luminance under a headlight where the gradient normal keeps 0.255577, and a 900-surfel shell matches the ideal Lambert sphere to 0.0208 mean absolute error under a side light and 0.0074 under a headlight (l122); materials and emission landed with L-154, and it is the gallery's lighting scene (l197). Its cost is a debt, not a verdict: the pass tests every surfel at every pixel on one thread, 2.939-3.091 ns per pixel and surfel, 91.0-96.1 ms for the gallery's 255x135 frame, so it cannot draw at window size (l197) - a binning build this audit records and does not propose.

3d/moment-hierarchy

PRUNEprovenance: 3d-scenetransfers: yes643 Rust lines · 4 mutation rowsl166-moment-matched-early-stop
why this verdict

Proven useless on the surviving target itself. Moment-matched early stop was built and measured on four 3D scenes through both methods: no threshold that summarises anything stays inside one code value - the first merge alone costs 48.6-222.8 of them, and every scene is at 197-223 by tau 16 px - and the bias has two signs, the 3D cloud losing up to 74% of its coverage while atoms at one depth gain up to 24% (l166). The mechanism is structural, so no tuning reaches it: the moment match is exact for an additive 3D density, and the renderer projects and composites with over. Nothing but its own test calls it. That test is l166's command, so l166 gains the removal commit and stands as historical; Sym3::eigen, which it borrowed from L-165, stays for the cage.

crates/lights/src/scene/hierarchy.rs · crates/lights/tests/moment_hierarchy.rs

3d/cage-deform

KEEPprovenance: target-freetransfers: yes748 Rust lines · 3 mutation rowsl165-affine-closurel169-cage-deform-vs-refit
why this verdict

Animation for atoms, resting on algebra that holds in any scene: an atom is closed under an affine map, reproduced to 1.98e-15 relative over 9,519 random maps (l165), so a per-tet affine cage deforms it exactly up to the warp's curvature inside one footprint. Its cost was measured on 2D glyph fits (7.5-9.3 us per update against 0.107-0.283 s for one re-fit, l169) and on a 3D cloud, which is the figure that transfers: 100k atoms deform in 12.39 ms on one thread, 124 ns per atom (l169). The quality comparison against a re-fit rides on the greedy fitter and does not transfer; the verdict does not rest on it.

The fix the surviving target needed, measured on it: compositing a surfel floor and resting atoms as two passes disagreed with the interleaved truth on 15.6-15.7% of the frame wherever both kinds reach a pixel (l153), and the depth-limited render (render_depth_limited, src/dispatch.rs:80) is the arm L-156 chose to close that without a heterogeneous scene. host/fused-bgra8-fill

KEEPprovenance: 3d-scenetransfers: yes691 Rust lines · 32 mutation rowsl190-fused-bgra8-handoffl197-gallery-frame-times
why this verdict

The handoff every presented frame goes through: resolve_bgra8 (src/image.rs:110) writes final bytes into the host's buffer in one pass, 2.574-2.649 ms at 1080p against 6.843-7.092 ms for the three passes it replaced, byte-identical and with no allocation (l190). Its debt is recorded for the next speed work, not proposed here: it is serial, and in the gallery it is now half of a warm small-scene frame, 2.66-2.74 ms of 4.91-5.06 (l197).
